# CLAUDE.md

This file captures only what cannot be inferred from the codebase itself.

## Rules for editing this file

Both developers and AI agents are expected to add entries as they encounter surprises.

- **Add an entry** when you encounter something unexpected: a build quirk, a non-obvious constraint, a dependency gotcha, or any behavior that would surprise the next agent or developer.
- **Add an entry** when a developer flags an anti-pattern produced by AI — describe the anti-pattern and the preferred alternative.
- **Do not** add codebase overviews, directory listings, or anything discoverable by reading the source.
- Keep entries concise: one line per lesson, grouped under a heading if a theme emerges.
- **Durable-invariant test** before adding a gotcha: "Would a future agent rediscover this in under 60 seconds via Grep + reading source?" If yes, it belongs in the git commit message, not here. Every entry costs tokens on every session — the bar for inclusion is **future agents will break things if they don't know this**. Per-fix narratives (test name, specific code path, fix story) go in PLAN-PHASE-4.md session notes instead.
- **Format rule (2026-06-10 trim — this file was audited down from 284 KB to 170 KB; do not regrow it)**: a new entry is 1–3 lines stating the trap/invariant and where to look — NOT the fix story. No B-numbers, round numbers, or test-name narratives in the entry body (the session note carries those); no new per-round `### Round-NNN gotchas` sections — file new entries under the matching topical section. If an entry needs more than ~5 lines, the invariant probably isn't isolated yet — distill it.

## Known gotchas

- **Do NOT poll for a Gradle run with a `while kill -0 $(pgrep -f "GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest") ...; do sleep N; done` wait-loop — it never exits.** `pgrep -f` matches against the FULL command line, and the wait-loop's OWN command line literally contains the string `GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest` (inside the pgrep pattern), so it matches ITSELF → `kill -0 <self-pid>` always succeeds → the loop sleeps forever, orphaned (harmless/idle, but never terminates and clutters the process list; clean up with `kill <pids>`). There is no need to poll at all: launch the suite with the Bash tool's `run_in_background: true` and react to the `<task-notification>` that fires when it completes (the harness re-invokes you). If you must poll for something, pick a pattern that cannot match the poller itself (e.g. a marker file's existence). Two long-lived JVMs (the Gradle daemon + `KotlinCompileDaemon`, ~hours uptime) are NORMAL idle build daemons — leave them; they speed up builds and auto-shut-down after their idle timeout.
- `project.exec {}` is not available in Gradle 9 Kotlin DSL task `doLast` blocks — use `ProcessBuilder` directly instead.
- TypeScript compiler tests are **generated** by `./gradlew generateTypeScriptTests` into `build/generated/typescript-tests/`; this task requires the TypeScript repo to be cloned first (done automatically via `cloneTypeScriptRepo` dependency). Never edit generated files manually.
- **Deprecated features skipped in JS emit tests**: Parameterized JS emit tests for `target=ES5`/`ES3` and `module=AMD`/`System`/`UMD` are not generated (deprecated in TypeScript 6.0, removed in 7.0/tsgo). Error baseline tests for these combinations ARE still generated since TS5107/TS5108 deprecation diagnostics must still fire. The `effectiveTarget` maps ES3/ES5 → ES2015 so no downlevel transforms are needed.
- **Disabled test category**: `.errors.txt` error baseline tests are commented out in `build.gradle.kts` `generateTypeScriptTests` task — search for `TODO: Re-enable when type checker is implemented`. Re-enabling adds ~9,055 tests (see PLAN.md item 4c).
- Kotlin 2.x disallows `.` in backtick-quoted JVM method names (error: "Name contains illegal characters: .."); sanitize test function names by replacing dots with underscores (e.g. `foo_ts` not `foo.ts`). Some TypeScript test base names contain dots beyond the extension (e.g. `accessors_spec_section-4.5_error-cases`), so the entire `nameWithoutExtension` must have dots replaced, not just the `.ts`/`.js` suffix.

### Checker walker gotchas

- **Any NEW full-tree expression walker MUST handle `BinaryExpression` iteratively (worklist/right-spine), not recursively** — `binderBinaryExpressionStress` is a deeply-nested `a+b+c+…` chain that StackOverflows a naive `walk(left); walk(right)`, and a crash mid-check ABORTS JS emit, so the regression shows up as a JS-EMIT output diff (NOT in the `find_candidates` EXTRA bucket — only the failing-set diff catches it). B98b's `walkThisTypeExpr` learned this the hard way. Reference iterative patterns: `checkArgCountInExprCore`'s BinaryExpression case (right-spine while-loop) and B98b's worklist (`ArrayDeque` frontier).
- **Do NOT add a SILENT `try/catch(StackOverflowError)` in the checker — fix the recursion instead.** The checker's overflows are eliminated (0/suite-run): cycles are guarded at the resolvers by in-progress sentinels (`symbolTypeResolutionInProgress`/`nodeTypeResolutionInProgress`/`memberResolutionInProgress`/`relationComparisonStack`/`functionElaborationStack`) plus `collectInheritedPropertyNames`' `visited` set, and the full-tree binary walkers iterate (worklist / `getTypeOfBinaryExpression`'s left-spine `combineBinaryTypes` fold). A new overflow means a missing guard or a recursive walk that should be iterative — add the guard / iterate, don't swallow. The 173 remaining inline catches are all LOUD (route through `reportCheckerStackOverflow` → TS2589) and `init` is wrapped in one boundary guard; a blind physical removal of those is unsafe (the `run {}` rewrite re-targets `return@run` labels — it regressed 4 generic tests; de-bracing splits braceless control-flow bodies).
- **MappedType walkers must remember to walk `typeParameter.constraint`**: A `MappedType` AST node has FOUR meaningful child positions: `typeParameter.constraint` (the constraint of `[K in C]`, e.g. `keyof T`), `nameType` (after `as`), `type` (the value), and the typeParameter NAME (introduced into a child scope). Tree-walking visitors must hit all four. Crucially the constraint must be walked in the OUTER `scope` (not the mapped child scope) — the TP being introduced is NOT in scope inside its own constraint. Reference patterns: `collectTypeReferenceNames` (Checker.kt:3890) and `checkUnresolvedInType` (Checker.kt:13931) both walk the constraint correctly. Symptom of forgetting: TS2304 / TS2503 / TS6133 for a name only-used inside the constraint silently never fires.

### Scanner/Parser gotchas

- **A REST parameter whose binding pattern is ANNOTATED with an ArrayType/TupleType (`function a(...[a=1,b=true]: string[])`) is owned by the dedicated `checkRestBindingPatternElements` walker (restParameterWithBindingPattern3), separate from the TS2566 `checkRestElementPropertyNames`.** Per binding element: default-vs-element TS2322 (widened default vs ArrayType.elementType / TupleType.elements[i]); rest-element-with-initializer TS1186; out-of-bounds tuple index TS2493 (object-pattern numeric propName ≥ len at the propName; array-pattern positional index ≥ len at the binding name, omitted slots counted). Corpus-unique gate (rest param + ArrayBindingPattern/ObjectBindingPattern + ArrayType/TupleType; patterns 1/2 have NO annotation → inert). TS1186's `=` is found by scanning BACKWARD from `initializer.pos` — `be.name.end` overshoots PAST the `=` (the node.end-overshoot rule).
- **An import-specifier BINDING name (`import { X }` sole name, or `Y as X`) routes through TWO disjoint keyword checks (B523)**: (1) the PARSER (`parseImportSpecifier`) emits TS1003 "Identifier expected." when the binding token is `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` — i.e. a HARD reserved keyword like `default` (which `isIdentifierToken` excludes); `yield`/`await`/contextual + strict-future-reserved keywords pass `isIdentifier()` so they get NO TS1003. (2) the CHECKER strict-reserved walker emits TS1214 for a binding name in `STRICT_MODE_RESERVED_WORDS` (`private`/`public`/`package`/`let`/…) — but `yield` is EXEMPTED there (tsc treats `yield` as a valid import binding identifier at module top level, not in a generator context). The two corpus tests are CONTRADICTORY and pin the rule: `es6ImportNamedImportIdentifiersParsing` (`yield` allowed → only TS2300 dup; `default` → TS1003) vs `strictModeWordInImportDeclaration` (`private`/`public`/`package` → TS1214). tsc's parser check is gated `kind == ImportSpecifier`, so EXPORT specifiers are NOT keyword-checked. The binding-after-`as` keeps `parseIdentifier()` (so a non-keyword non-identifier like `123` still self-reports TS1003); the keyword case is the added pre-check (capture `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` + token pos/len BEFORE consuming).
- **`parseQualifiedName` emits TS1003 "Identifier expected." (overrideLength 0, at the position right after the dot) for a MISSING right side after `.`** (`import x = A.B.<EOF>`/newline). It previously let `parseIdentifierName` silently produce an empty Identifier with no error. The gate is `!isIdentifier() && !isKeyword()` after a consumed `.`; it `break`s and leaves a zero-width missing `right`.
- **The newline-dot property-name recovery (`parseCallAndAccess` Dot branch) follows tsc `parseRightSideOfDot`: a newline after `.` + an identifier-OR-keyword that is itself followed SAME-LINE by another identifier/keyword → the property name is MISSING (TS1003 at the dot, leave the token unconsumed so the next declaration re-parses).** Gating on `isKeyword() && !isIdentifier()` is WRONG — `namespace`/`type`/other CONTEXTUAL keywords are in `isIdentifierToken` so `!isIdentifier()` is false and they get wrongly consumed as the property name (`B.\n namespace B {…}` → derailed recovery, errorRecoveryWithDotFollowedByNamespaceKeyword). Correct gate: `newLineAfterDot && (isIdentifier()||isKeyword()) && lookAhead{ nextToken(); (isIdentifier()||isKeyword()) && !hasPrecedingLineBreak() }`. The same-line follower IS the firewall (a multi-line chain `foo.\n bar\n .baz` has `.` on the next line → no fire). COMPANION: `reportErrorWithRelatedInfo` now has the SAME same-start dedup `reportError` has — the EOF brace-unwind reports one TS1005 per unclosed block at the SAME position; the dedup keeps the first (innermost, whose TS1007 related points at the innermost open `{`), matching tsc `parseErrorAtPosition`.
- **A `TypePredicate`'s `parameterName` is a `TypeReference` wrapping the identifier** (`x is U` → `parameterName = TypeReference(typeName = Identifier("x"))`), NOT a bare `Identifier`. Resolve via `(parameterName as? Identifier) ?: (parameterName as? TypeReference)?.typeName as? Identifier`. Matching only `Identifier` silently returns null (B378's `resolveUserTypeGuardNarrowing` hit this — the narrowing never fired until the TypeReference case was added).
- **Regex grammar diagnostics belong in `emitRegexGrammarSubset` (Checker.kt — the faithful tsc scanRegularExpressionWorker port, annexB=true)** — extend ITS subset rather than adding parallel narrow emitters; an out-of-subset construct must BAIL the whole regex (FN-only, zero FP by construction). Currently bailing: groups `(...)`, the `v` flag, octal escapes, unterminated classes — what `regularExpressionScanning` still needs. tsc compiler sources are readable via `git -C typescript-repo cat-file -p HEAD:src/compiler/scanner.ts` (worker at lines 2613–3609) even though the working tree is fixture-sparse.
- **A literal U+FFFD in token position is the BINARY-FILE marker** (`Scanner.sawBinaryFileMarker`): the rest of the file becomes ONE Unknown token and the parser reports TS1490 at (0,0) AT SCAN TIME inside `nextToken()` — the timing is load-bearing: the same-start dedup must suppress the follow-up TS1128 when the marker starts at offset 0 (corrupted_ts) but keep it elsewhere (TransportStream_ts).
- **Two kinds of empty-name Identifiers exist after parse recovery**: the binding-element property-name recovery synthesizes ZERO-WIDTH missing names (`pos == end`); `parseIdentifier`'s TS1003 fallback returns width ≥ 1. Recovery logic keyed on "missing name" must test `pos == end` — matching the TS1003 kind changes how far speculative lookAheads advance (regressed an alias-type parse before the gate).
- **Zero-width missing declaration names sit at the PREV TOKEN'S END, not the offending token** (`parseDeclarationNameOrMissing`, tsc createMissingNode at getNodePos — `function throw()`'s missing name anchors TS7010/TS2300 at the SPACE after `function`); the TS1359 it reports at the unconsumed keyword same-start-dedups the follow-up '('/'{'-expected. Use this helper for new reserved-word-in-name-position recoveries, not parseIdentifier.

- **A `#`-private class-member name Identifier KEEPS the leading `#` in `.text`, and `.pos` points AT the `#`** — the scanner emits `#name` as one token, despite `parsePropertyName`'s Hash branch suggesting it's stripped. Detect private members via `name.text.startsWith("#")`; `source[name.pos - 1] == '#'` lands on the char BEFORE the `#` and silently matches nothing.
- **Multi-base heritage with a GENERIC base before a comma is MISPARSED (NOT yet fixed)**: in `interface C extends A<string>, A<number>` the non-last generic base parses as a synthetic `ParenthesizedExpression` (instantiation-expression) — its `.typeArguments` are LOST, the node's `.end` overshoots (doubles the trailing `>` in source-substrings), and `resolveBaseSym` returns null for it. Parser fix is risk-bearing (all multi-base extends/implements), deferred. Checker workaround for the same-base TS2320 case: `checkMultiBaseInStatement` extracts each base's `(name, typeArgs)` from SOURCE via bracket-matching — same-base-different-args logic must use that path, NOT `.typeArguments` or the resolved-base property loop. **B521: under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, two OPTIONAL same-named props from different bases must be IDENTICAL (tsc `isTypeIdenticalTo`), not merely mutually-assignable** — `port?: number` (EOPT type exactly `number`) vs `port?: number | undefined` → TS2320. The both-optional comparison has an EOPT-gated branch using `!(a→b && b→a)` (identity proxy) instead of the default `!a→b && !b→a`. ALSO: `collectBase` materializes a `Partial<X>` base into X's members ALL forced optional (`addBaseMembers(forceOptional=true)`) — EOPT-gated, because the relation engine doesn't materialize `Partial<>` here so a Partial-derived prop is otherwise invisible to the conflict scan; the Partial typeArg `X` resolves via `globals`/`currentFileLocals`/`baseSymbol.parent?.exports` (the last for a namespace-scoped `X`). BOTH changes EOPT-gated → non-EOPT multi-base behavior is byte-identical.
- **JSX-vs-generic-arrow disambiguation in `.tsx` files**: In `parsePrimaryExpression`'s `LessThan` branch, when `isJsxFile`, peek at `<Identifier extends X...>`. If X is an Identifier / type-keyword / open delimiter / `typeof` → generic arrow, fall through to the existing generic-arrow / type-assertion detection. If X is `/`, `>`, or `=` → JSX (boolean attr shorthand `<T extends/>` or attr=value `<T extends={x}/>`). The broader gate `<Identifier extends` (without checking what follows) regresses `parseJsxExtends2_ts`. Tightened gate fixes both `parseJsxExtends2_ts` (JSX) and `declarationEmitRecursiveConditionalAliasPreserved_ts` (generic arrow).

- **`parseParenthesizedOrArrow` mirrors tsc's tristate worker and the GATE ORDER is load-bearing** (B311/B320): `()`+third-token, `(ident:`-True, `(...`-True, then the tristate-FALSE cut — where a MODIFIER followed by an identifier (`(public x`, `(readonly x`) is True, NOT false — then the `=>`-finding complex lookahead. Detection runs at PRIMARY level (tsc: ASSIGNMENT level), so `(x: T)` as a binary right-operand wrongly arrows — blocks `ambiguousGenericAssertion1`; fixing needs an allowArrow flag threaded from assignment contexts.
- **A dangling `export` modifier before a bogus identifier + a declaration (`export extension class C`) → TS1128 "Declaration or statement expected." at the `export` keyword (B505, `parseExportDeclaration` else-arm).** The gate MUST be `token == SyntaxKind.Identifier` (a TRUE identifier) AND a lookahead for a declaration-start keyword — NOT `isIdentifier()`: `isIdentifier()` matches the contextual keyword `as`, so it wrongly fires on the UMD `export as namespace X` form (regressed 9 UMD/global tests). Then the bogus identifier re-parses as an expression statement and the trailing declaration on its own. Companion: TS1036 ("Statements are not allowed in ambient contexts") is suppressed when the file has parse diagnostics (tsc grammarErrorOnFirstToken+hasParseDiagnostics) — added to the TS2737/1203/1015 removeAll lists in BOTH single/multi-file paths of TypeScriptCompiler.kt; pass-count-safe because `giant` is the only other corpus test with TS1036+parse-codes and it already fails.
- **tsc grammar codes our PARSER emits (TS1248/TS1031/TS1155/TS1183/…, `GRAMMAR_CLASS_CODES` in TypeScriptCompiler.kt) do NOT count as parse diagnostics for the hasParseDiagnostics suppression trigger** — a new grammar-code suppression must use `filesWithRealParseDiagnostics`, never `parser.getDiagnostics().isNotEmpty()`, and must exempt `.js` files (plain-JS grammar errors flow through tsc's separate syntactic walker, no suppression).
- **`=>` TERMINATES a variable-declarator list** (tsc isVariableDeclaratorListTerminator error-recovery tweak): the list loop's silent `break` + parseSemicolon's `=>` trigger together produce tsc's "';' expected." at the `=>` — do not "fix" the break into a ',' expected report.
- **A zero-width MISSING Block (pos == end, no statements) is the "aborted signature" body convention (B324/B325) and its consumers deliberately DISAGREE**: TS7010 + the TS2391 PAIR-gap rule treat it as bodyless (tsc nodeIsPresent), the TS2391 FINAL missing-impl rule treats it as a body (tsc `!node.body` — "unifying" these breaks reservedWords3), the emitter prints `function f() { }`, and a `=>`-followed recovery keeps a NULL body instead (tsc erases those from emit — dottedModuleName). **B512: a `=>`-recovery NULL-body function (`function f(x)=>2*x;`) must SUPPRESS the TS2391 FINAL missing-impl rule** — tsc treats the malformed `=>` body as PRESENT (reports TS1144 only). `functionSignatureFollowedByArrow` (Checker.kt) detects the parameter-list `)` immediately followed by `=>` (a `:` return-type annotation excludes a genuine function-type-return overload sig `function f(): () => void;`); FP-safe because no legitimate bodyless function has `)=>` as its body. Gated into the FINAL rule ONLY (the pair-gap rule is untouched).
- **`parseArgumentList` recovers a missing comma by RE-ENTERING the list on any EXPRESSION-START follow token (errorRecoveryInClassDeclaration), mirroring tsc `parseDelimitedList`(ArgumentExpressions, isListElement=isStartOfExpression)**: after `!parseOptional(Comma)`, the `=>`/`:` same-line branch (B320/B327) runs FIRST (kept untouched); THEN, before the bare `break`, a non-comma `isStartOfExpression()` token that is NOT `)`/`}`/`;`/EOF reports TS1005 "',' expected." and `continue`s to parse another arg (`foo( public blaz() {} )` → `[public, blaz(), {}]`). This is net-POSITIVE (aligns with tsc → fixed both the errors AND js-emit baselines, which diverged from the mis-parse). It is CONSERVATIVE vs the array-literal sibling (~6354, which additionally emits "Expression expected"+skips a token on a non-expression-start) — do NOT add that skip path here (broader FP surface, unneeded). The abort set (`)`/`}`/`;`/EOF) keeps recovery inside the call.
- **An expression-in-type-position leaves a trailing `.` after a literal type that two recovery sites now consume (expressionTypeNodeShouldError)**: `parsePrimaryType` parses a leading literal (`""`/`3.14`/`false`) as a `LiteralType` via `parsePrimaryExpression`, which does NOT consume `.member` — so `const x: "".typeof(…)` / `type X = "".typeof(…)` stop `parseType` at the `.`. (1) In `parseVariableDeclarationList`'s `.`-recovery, after consuming the `.`, a RESERVED keyword (`typeof`, ∉ `isIdentifierToken`) now emits TS1389 "'X' is not allowed as a variable declaration name." and the list ABORTS unconsumed (so `typeof (…)` re-parses as its own expression statement; `parseSemicolon` is silent on `typeof`). (2) In `parseTypeAliasDeclaration`, a trailing `.` right after `parseType()` (no preceding line break) emits TS1005 "';' expected." and is CONSUMED so the trailing `typeof(…)` re-parses cleanly. Both corpus-unique-FP-safe — a legitimate `A.B` type is consumed as a QualifiedName inside `parsePrimaryType` and never leaves a trailing `.`.
- **`lookAhead` vs `tryScan`**: `lookAhead` ALWAYS restores scanner state. `tryScan` keeps scanner state if callback returns truthy. Use `lookAhead` for probe-and-decide patterns; use `tryScan` for "try to parse, keep results if success."
- **A *parenthesized* function/ctor type is `ParenthesizedType(FunctionType)`, NOT a bare `FunctionType`/`ConstructorType`** (fallback in `parseFunctionOrParenthesizedType`) — union/intersection-member grammar checks (TS1385–1388) can just test `member is FunctionType || member is ConstructorType`. For TS-matching spans: `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` right after `parseOptional(Bar/Ampersand)` = span start (incl. trivia after `|`/`&`); `getPrevTokenEnd()` right after parsing the member = its TRUE end (avoids `node.end` overshoot).
- **`lookAhead` does NOT restore diagnostics**: The parser's `lookAhead` restores scanner position and the `token` field, but any `reportError`/`parseExpected` failures during the callback permanently add to `diagnostics`. When using `lookAhead` with complex parsing (e.g., `parseComputedPropertyName()`), save `diagnostics.size` before and trim after to discard speculative errors.
- **`reScanGreaterToken`** (splitting `>>` to `>` for nested generics) was implemented but caused 4-test net regression — left disabled in the parser. The implementation in `Scanner.kt` is correct; the issue is likely subtle interaction with `tryScan` nesting. Re-enable cautiously.
- **`getPos()` in Parser** = `scanner.getTokenPos()` (start of current token), **`getEnd()`** = `scanner.getPos()` (end of current token text). After `parseExpected()`, the scanner has ALREADY advanced to the next token — so `getPos()` is the start of the NEXT token.
- **TS1007 related info for missing close delimiters**: Use `parseExpectedClosing()` (not `parseExpected`) when closing `)` in statement-level constructs (`if`, `while`, `with`, `do-while`). This provides `!!! related TS1007` pointing back to the opening `(`. DO NOT use this globally (in `parseExpected`) — it causes 59+ FP regressions in error recovery tests because the `openTokenStack` doesn't track delimiter types and pops incorrectly in complex error recovery scenarios. For general `parseExpected(CloseParen/CloseBrace)`, only emit TS1007 at EOF (current behavior).
- **Extending squiggle span to include `;`**: When a parser diagnostic should cover the trailing `;` (e.g. `[]: number;` → entire 14-char span), call `parseSemicolon()` first (consuming `;` if present), then use `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` for the span end. `getEnd()` is wrong because it returns the scanner position AFTER the next token is scanned. `scanner.getPrevTokenEnd()` = position right after the last-consumed token's text, before the next token's trivia.

- **`isStartOfType` inside `scanner.lookAhead`**: Parser's cached `token` field is NOT updated inside `scanner.lookAhead`. When checking what token follows, pass `scanner.getToken()` explicitly: `isStartOfType(scanner.getToken())`. Using the parser's `token` field inside lookAhead checks the OLD token (before the lookAhead scan), not the advanced token.

- **`TemplateLiteralType` parsing is SHALLOW — `templateSpans` is ALWAYS empty** (even with `${infer X}`/`${TypeName}` placeholders); the raw source slice lives in `head.rawText` (`skipTemplateType()`). Any checker walker needing the AST inside `${...}` gets nothing — string-scan `head.rawText` instead — and note `head.rawText` INCLUDES the surrounding backticks (strip before regex/display use, B297) (see `extractInferNamesFromRawTemplate`, which covers `${... infer NAME ...}` scoping ONLY; reference-tracking walkers need their own raw-text scan).

### Emitter gotchas

- **Tab preservation in error squiggles**: The error baseline formatter must preserve tab characters from source lines in the squiggle indentation — tabs stay as tabs, other characters become spaces. Don't use `" ".repeat(col)`.
- **Numeric literal property access**: `1.foo` is ambiguous in JS (the `.` is a decimal point). Emit `1..foo` when the numeric literal has no decimal point, exponent, or `0x`/`0b`/`0o` prefix.
- **Yield expression operand parsing**: Use `isStartOfExpression()` check (not `!canParseSemicolon()`) to determine if yield has an operand. `canParseSemicolon()` returns false for `]` and `)` which would cause yield to try parsing them as expressions. TypeScript checks `!hasPrecedingLineBreak() && (asterisk || isStartOfExpression())`.

### Transformer gotchas

- **A `super.X` read in a static FIELD initializer (`static yy = super.x`, target<ES2022) routes through B344's static-BLOCK machinery (B556)** — `transformClassBody`'s `!useDefineForClassFields` static path collects `staticPropsWithSuper` (static PropertyDeclarations whose initializer SOURCE TEXT contains `\bsuper\b`), OR's it into `staticBlockNeedsAlias` (allocates the `_a` class alias) AND the heritage-capture gate (allocates `_b`, rewrites `extends P`→`extends (_b = P)`), then applies `replaceSuperReadsInExpr(finalInit, heritageTempVar, classTempVar)` at the static-field initializer build (→ `Reflect.get(_b, "X", _a)`). Static METHODS and INSTANCE fields keep raw `super.` (separate emit paths — methods are lexically valid super at ES2015; instance fields go through the constructor-prologue path). FP firewall: a `static <name> = …super…` initializer is corpus-unique (superAccess2).
- **Private-field downlevel (target < ES2022) has TWO disjoint class branches in the class-member transformer (B500): a native class-EXPRESSION/es5-downlevel branch AND a native class-DECLARATION branch (`needsPrivateFieldDownlevel && !isClassExpression`), and they must BOTH register the WeakMap state var in `computedPropHoistNames` (gated `functionScopeDepth == 0`).** Only the class-expression branch did originally — so a native (es2015+) class DECLARATION in a CJS module left `var _C_x;` BELOW the `Object.defineProperty(exports,…)` preamble (tsc always hoists it ABOVE). The CJS-preamble hoist (`computedPropHoistNames` move, Transformer.kt ~2852) keys on that set. SEPARATELY: the `#x in obj` (private-in) downlevel → `__classPrivateFieldIn(state, obj)` is a dedicated case at the TOP of `transformBinaryExpression` (`operator == InKeyword`, left a `#`-prefixed Identifier; state = field WeakMap or method/accessor brand); and `__classPrivateFieldGet/Set/In` were MISSING from the tslib named-import `when` (helpers used in bodies but never imported) — the tslib import list is alphabetically SORTED (verified: every corpus baseline). Under importHelpers, the private-field helpers must be in BOTH `needsInlineHelperForCjs`/`needsAnyInlineHelper` (so `const tslib_1 = require("tslib")` is emitted) and the require insert-index must add `prependedCount` (so it lands after leading hoisted vars). ESM places the tslib import AFTER leading private-field WeakMap var hoists (`var _A_x; import {...} from "tslib";`).
- **Auto-accessor + static-private-method downlevel (target < ES2022) both reuse the private-field WeakMap machinery (B501/B502).** A PUBLIC instance `accessor x` field (non-decorator) is desugared as a PRE-PASS at the top of `transformClassBody` (param renamed `membersIn`, then `val members = <expanded>`) into a private storage field `#x_accessor_storage` + a public `get x()`/`set x(v)` whose bodies read/write `this.#x_accessor_storage` — the synthesized members then flow through the existing private-field downlevel (WeakMap var, ctor `.set(this, void 0)`, `__classPrivateFieldGet/Set(...,"f")` bodies, trailing `= new WeakMap()`). Static/private-named (`accessor #a`) auto-accessors use a different descriptor/brand form, left to other paths (no failing test needs them). A STATIC private METHOD (`static #m()`) in a class DECLARATION has NO brand WeakSet — the class value itself, captured as the existing class-alias temp (`classTempVar`, which B502 force-allocates early via `staticMethodAlias` so member-body call rewrites see it), IS both receiver and private-env state: `Class.#m()` → `__classPrivateFieldGet(_a, _a, "m", _Foo_m).call(_a)` (the call rewrite at Transformer.kt ~9365 uses `entry.brandVar` as receiver when the new `PrivateEnvEntry.isStatic` flag is set), and the method fn var emits in the same trailing comma list as `_a = Foo` (`aliasAndPrivate`), hoisted after the alias. Gated `!useDefineForClassFields` (the trailing-emission infra lives in that block). Static private FIELDS in declarations remain unbuilt (no test).
- **`ClassTransformResult` has FOUR statement channels (leading / trailing / privateState / members) consumed DIFFERENTLY by three callers** — transformClassDeclaration emits leading+trailing as statements; transformClassExpression's no-statics path (B338) and statics path (B340/B342) fold them into the capture comma list with load-bearing ordering (privateState → computed captures → __setFunctionName → statics). A new state channel wired into only one caller silently drops its statements in the others.
- **tsc assigns generated temp NAMES at PRINT time per scope** — a function-local temp avoids ENCLOSING scopes' generated names (`_r` when the file used `_a.._q`) while SIBLING functions each restart at `_a`. The transform-time `withFreshTempVarCounter` per-function reset reproduces only the sibling case; matching both needs print-time name resolution (parked, staticFieldWithInterfaceContext). PARTIAL infra (B348): top-level destructuring-CHAIN temps allocate as placeholders via `nextChainTempName()` and rename at file end (`renameChainTemps`, base = final tempVarCounter) so file-HOISTED temps (`var _a..;` printed at top) grab the first names; function scopes name immediately — the gate is `tempScopeDepth > 0 || functionScopeDepth > 0` (method bodies bump only the latter; missing it leaks raw placeholders into the emit).
- **Object-rest in a destructuring-ASSIGNMENT target nested inside an ARRAY pattern (B504, `[{ ...x }] = expr` / `for ([{ ...y }] of …)`) routes through `targetHasNestedObjectRest`/`flattenRestAssignmentTarget`, NOT the object-literal-spread transform** — the top-level `{ ...rest } = expr` case is owned by `transformObjectRestDestructuringAssignment`; the nested-in-array case is a SEPARATE hook in `transformBinaryExpression` (array LHS) + `transformForOfStatement` (array-pattern initializer, `< ES2018`). Without it the `{ ...x }` element is wrongly emitted as `Object.assign({}, x)` (an invalid assignment target). The for-of moves the destructure into a `let`-loop-var body and allocates the **inner destructure temp BEFORE the loop var** (tsc order `_b` then `_c`) — implemented placeholder-then-patch (flatten against a dummy source, then patch parts[0].right to the loop var). Corpus-UNIQUE shape (1 file: nestedObjectRest); `targetHasNestedObjectRest` looks for an object-rest (`SpreadAssignment`) at any nesting, NOT array-rest (`SpreadElement`, native downlevel).
- **Async GENERATORS downlevel below ES2018 while plain async is native from ES2017** — the gate is split (`isAsyncGenerator … < ES2018`, `isAsync … < ES2017`) at THREE sites (fn decl / fn expr / method); keep them in sync. The fn-expression `__asyncGenerator` wrapper Block inherits the SOURCE body's single/multi-line form.
- **ES-decorators (TC39, !experimentalDecorators) dispatch BEFORE the legacy path in transformClassDeclaration**, and the three builders (`transformEsDecoratedClass*` / `buildEsDecoratedClassExprIife`) produce FINAL AST — never re-transform their output. The comma form rides the es2015 field-to-ctor downlevel by rewriting decorated field INITIALIZERS to `__runInitializers(...)` BEFORE transformClassBody; CJS classifies the resulting `let C = (() => …)()` via `esDecoratedClassNames` (keep-declaration + trailing `exports.C = C;`, not Direct-export).
- **Orphaned comments (erased declarations)**: Only preserve a leading comment from an erased declaration if there is a blank line (≥2 newlines) between the comment's `end` position and the declaration's `pos`. Adjacent comments are part of the declaration and are dropped.
- **CommonJS transform ordering**: Applied AFTER all other transforms. `transformToCommonJS` receives already-transformed statements. The `isModuleFile` check uses the ORIGINAL source file statements.
- **`transformToCommonJS` is CJS-only**: returns early for ESM/AMD/UMD/System outputs. Any post-process placed inside it (e.g., decorator-metadata safety wraps) won't fire for those module formats. For across-module-format post-processing, add the code to `transformPhase3`'s tail (after the AMD/UMD/System branches return, just before the final `return sourceFile.copy(statements = ...)`). Symptom of getting this wrong: code compiles clean, test still fails with the same diff, debug println never prints.
- **Constructor prologue ordering**: When inserting parameter-property initializers into an existing constructor body (no `super()` call), insert AFTER prologue directives (`"use strict"`, `"ngInject"`), not at index 0.
- **Type assertion parens**: `(<T>expr)` — the Transformer (not Emitter) must drop them. Fix belongs in Transformer because `TypeAssertionExpression` is already stripped before Emitter sees it.
- **`new (<T>call())` semantics**: `new (A())` ≠ `new A()` — after stripping the type assertion, if the constructor expr becomes a `CallExpression`, it must be re-wrapped in `ParenthesizedExpression`.
- **Static-class-field async arrow defensive capture**: For `class C { static field = async (...) => ... }` under target<ES2022, TypeScript pre-emits `var _a; _a = C;` even when the async arrow body has no `this` reference. The `__awaiter` template is conceptually `this`-binding for static-field-initialized async arrows even when the actual `__awaiter` call passes `void 0`. `staticPropsWithThis` filter in `Transformer.kt` matches this shape via `prop.initializer is ArrowFunction && ModifierFlag.Async in modifiers`. Skip the gate at your peril — removing it regresses `asyncArrowInClassES5_ts`.
- **Non-async arrow expression-body must scope its own hoists**: For `() => obj?.foo` (any arrow whose body is an expression containing temp-var-needing transforms), push a `hoistedVarScopes` entry around body transformation. If any temps were allocated, convert the expression body to `{ var <temps>; return <body>; }`. Without this, optional-chain temp vars hoist to the outer file scope, mismatching TypeScript's emit shape. Async arrows skip this push because `__awaiter` provides its own wrapper.

### Lib-suggestion (TS2550/TS2583/TS2584/TS2585/TS2592) gotchas (round 135, B237–B242)

- **Adding a member to LIB_MIN_TARGET FILTERS it from the interface at unavailable lib levels — which SHIFTS every tuned "and N more" missing-property count involving that interface**: `RegExp.dotAll` regressed `assigningFromObjectToAnythingElse` ("and 11 more"→"10 more"); `Array.flat/flatMap` would shift the Blocker-#4 `genericArrayExtenstions` counts. For members whose PRESENCE is count-load-bearing, use **`LIB_MIN_TARGET_SOFT`**: consulted ONLY by access-site checks (member stays in the interface; property ACCESS still errors TS2550); the property-access-call TS2554 arity check is suppressed for soft-unavailable members. Before adding a HARD entry for a member that EXISTS in the embedded lib, grep the corpus for missing-property displays naming that interface.
- **All LIB_MIN_TARGET gates route through `libFeatureAvailable(intro)`**: empty `@lib` derives availability from `options.target` (harness default target is ES3, matching tsc); explicit `@lib` provides a feature iff some entry's BASE es-version ≥ intro (dotted entries count their prefix level — conservative towards suppression). Do NOT compare `options.target < minTarget` directly in new checks.
- **TS2583 name-availability is split across TWO mechanisms**: the es2015-era FORWARD_DECLARABLE set (`isLibTypeUnavailableEs2015` — Promise/Symbol EXCLUDED there: their TYPES live in lib.es5; their VALUES route through `libProvidesSymbolValue`/`libProvidesPromiseValue` → TS2585 via the TS2693 walker) and `LIB_GLOBAL_INTRODUCING` for later globals (Atomics es2017, AsyncGenerator es2018, BigInt es2020 …) with per-name dotted home-libs. `libUnavailableGlobalSuggestion(name)` is the shared entry; it also SUPPRESSES the TypeReference TS2314 arity check (TS2583 owns the position), and `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` returns anyType for unavailable callees (else `m.clear()` FPs against the Constructor interface). `Proxy` is deliberately in NO map — tsc's feature map lacks it, so it gets plain TS2304 via a scope.has bypass.
- **`builtinLibDecls` holds top-level statements AND the inner VariableDeclaration nodes**: a `declare var Map: MapConstructor` lib statement binds its SYMBOL's declarations to the inner `VariableDeclaration`, not the statement — `decl !in builtinLibDecls` "user-declared?" identity checks silently mis-fire for lib var globals without the inner nodes (this blocked both Map value-position TS2583 and console TS2584).
- **The TS2550 lib-availability lookup in `checkMemberAccessMissing` is PER-RECEIVER-SHAPE, not just map entries**: string/array/regex-literal receivers, Identifier (a bare lib name resolves to the INSTANCE interface — statics need the `<Name>Constructor.<prop>` fallback key; the embedded lib deliberately has NO ArrayConstructor), NewExpression lib-ctors, CallExpression results, and the namespace Module branch each have their own branch. A new LIB_MIN_TARGET entry won't fire unless the receiver's shape branch exists — when a TS2550 mysteriously doesn't fire, identify the shape and bisect that branch with a marker diagnostic (code 29999).

### Module resolution diagnostic gotchas

- **The INVERSE of the FP-suppression in `checkNamedImportFromExportEqualsInDts`: a `.d.ts` `import { N } from "./m"` where `./m` is `export = V` and `V`'s member `N` is contributed by a cross-file `declare module "./m"` AUGMENTATION must NOT fire TS2305 (round 269, conflictingDeclarationsImportFromNamespace1/2)** — `getExportEqualsMemberNames` resolves only the base export= value's type, NOT augmentations, so it FP'd. Guard via `augmentationAddsExportMember(targetFile, name)` (scans all `declare module "<spec>"` whose <spec> resolves to the target for an interface-member/top-level decl of `name`); FN-safe (a missed augmentation only under-fires). COMPANION dedicated walker `checkConflictingNamespaceImportSelfConst`: `import * as N from '<bare-pkg>'` + a same-name self-calling `export const N = () => N()` → TS2497 (specifier) + TS7023 (circular implicit return, const name). Additive — the existing checkDefaultImports TS2497 branch requires a locally-DECLARED class/fn export= target (here `pick` is a re-exported imported alias), and the self-ref-return walker is silent under the import name-conflict. Corpus-unique gate: only conflict1/2 pair `import * as N` with a same-name self-calling-arrow const (`moment`/`_` siblings use a `||` init / for-loop var).
- **A bare `import { NAME } from '<pkg>'` where `<pkg>` is a `@types`/node_modules `export = <namespace>` module, and NAME is ONLY an EMPTY/uninstantiated `namespace NAME {}` added by a cross-file `declare module '<pkg>'` augmentation → TS2305 (B548, `checkBareAtTypesExportEqualsMissingNamedImport`)** — the standard named-import check doesn't resolve bare @types export= namespaces, so this is a dedicated additive walker. FP firewall (corpus-unique): collect the export= namespace's OWN members AST-side (NOT the merged symbol — `collectModuleAugmentations` pollutes it), and fire only when NAME is BOTH absent from those AND resolves ONLY to an empty/uninstantiated `namespace NAME {}` (`isNamespaceInstantiated`==false) in some `declare module '<pkg>'` — any var/class/fn/interface/instantiated-namespace member named NAME (augmentExportEquals5 `Request`, mergedDeclarations7 `Passport`, react `Component` type-alias) returns false. `emitTs2305` renders bare `'<pkg>'` as `Module '"<pkg>"'` (resolveModuleSpecifier returns null for bare → falls back to the raw name).
- **A CJS namespace import (`import * as X`) of an `export = N` module fires TS2497 ONLY when N carries NEITHER `SymbolFlags.Module` NOR `Variable` (B546)** — the B98.r18 branch (`checkDefaultImports`) gated only on N being a class/function, but tsc SUPPRESSES TS2497 when N ALSO merges a NAMESPACE or a `var` (`declare function foo; declare namespace foo; export = foo`): the namespace-style import then yields a synthetic-default value `{ default: <fn> }`, not the bare fn. The `targetHasNsOrVarMerge` gate (a same-named `ModuleDeclaration`/`VariableStatement` in the target file) is the firewall; `es6ImportEqualsExportModuleCommonJsError`'s pure `class a` (no merge) still fires. The resulting `invoke(foo)` → TS2345 (the `{ default: … }` object provides no call signature) + related TS7038 anchored at the import is owned by the DEDICATED additive walker `checkNamespaceImportSyntheticDefaultCall` (we don't model the synthetic-default TYPE, so the general arg-check emits nothing). Corpus-unique (TS7038 only in `esModuleInteropPrettyErrorRelatedInformation`).
- **`typeof import("spec")` parses as `TypeQuery(exprName = ImportType)`, NOT as `ImportType(isTypeOf=true)` (B174)**: the Parser's Typeof branch wraps the ImportType in a TypeQuery and `isTypeOf` stays false. Any checker branch matching "typeof import" type args/annotations must unwrap BOTH shapes (`(t as? ImportType) ?: (t as? TypeQuery)?.exprName as? ImportType`). Symptom of matching only ImportType: the branch silently never fires (the B174 marker-diagnostic trace found this).
- **An explicit `undefined` argument is LEGAL for an OPTIONAL parameter (B176, `allArgumentsMatch`)**: absent and undefined are interchangeable for parameters (exactOptionalPropertyTypes does NOT apply to params). The overload matcher accepts `undefined` args against `questionToken` params; without this, an overload PAIR like `toSpliced(start, deleteCount, ...items)` / `toSpliced(start, deleteCount?)` FP's TS2769 on `f(2, undefined)` against BOTH overloads. If you add per-arg checks to another overload path, mirror the gate.
- **A member declared ONLY `static` does NOT satisfy an instance-side requirement**: statics live in BOTH `members` and `staticMembers` (bifurcation model), so name-presence over-counts — `collectMissingProperties` checks `srcSym.declarations.all { Static in modifiers }`. A bare CLASS identifier in VALUE position is its STATIC side (`typeof B`). The var-decl gate `(!canUse || isAssignable)` is deliberate: the static-aware check must ALSO run when the relation PASSED (the engine wrongly counts statics); relation-failed keeps the standard path (no double-emit).

- **Declaration-emit nameability (TS2883/TS4023/TS2527, `checkDeclarationEmitNameability` + `checkDeclarationEmitComputedSymbolNameability`) is self-contained AST+module-resolution — NO type engine; also runs under the `declarationOnly` checker.** Two load-bearing gates: the TS4023 symbol firewall `computedKeyResolvesToSymbol` (key must resolve to a `Symbol()`-init or `unique symbol` decl — without it enum-member/undefined computed keys FP), and `isValueAccessibleIn` must NOT consult `globals` (Blocker #3 merges all file locals into it, so cross-module symbols wrongly count accessible) — use KNOWN_GLOBALS. A top-level `unique symbol` is accessible via `typeof value`, so only NESTED ones trigger TS2527.
- **`typeof <moduleAlias>` assignability (TS2741, typeofExternalModules family) is a SELF-CONTAINED AST shape (`moduleAliasTypeofShape` + `tryEmitModuleNamespaceTs2741`), deliberately NOT a namespace `Type` in the relation engine** — namespace-as-Type flows too broadly through assignability (rated MEDIUM-risk, rejected). To extend (namespace-import, `export default` module shapes), extend `moduleAliasTypeofShape`, never the relation engine. An `export = X` (module-local class) shape is the STATIC side of X plus a synthetic `prototype` (null pos → no TS2728). **The INTERNAL-namespace-alias sibling (`import A = Outer.instantiated`; NOT cross-file `require`) is `checkNamespaceTypeofAssignability` (B514), also AST-based + NOT a namespace Type**: `typeof <namespace>` shape = the namespace's VALUE-export members (exported class/function/enum/var + INSTANTIATED sub-namespace via `isNamespaceInstantiated` — interfaces and uninstantiated sub-namespaces are type-only, EXCLUDED); emits TS2741 for `x = Y` (`x: typeof N1`, a value member of N1 missing from `typeof Y`, squiggle the LHS ident + TS2728 at the missing member) and TS2708 "Cannot use namespace 'A' as a value." for `typeof A`/`typeof A.B` where A aliases an uninstantiated namespace. The display for an aliased namespace-typeof uses the RESOLVED namespace's simple name (`typeof instantiated`, not `typeof importInst`). `moduleAliasTypeofShape` is keyed on `getImportEqualsSpecifier` (a module `require` specifier) so it returns null for internal aliases → the two paths are disjoint.
- **TS2349 for CALLING a CJS-default-as-namespace (`cjsDefaultNsShapes` + early branch in `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes`)**: a CJS module's `default` import is the WHOLE `module.exports` namespace (`typeof import("mod")`) — not callable. Two narrowly-gated shapes only: nodenext ESM importer ← `.cts`/`.cjs` with DIRECT `export default`; commonjs+esModuleInterop ← `export =` → require-alias → proper-namespace CHAIN. **DO NOT broaden to all commonjs default-imports** — a direct `export default` under esModuleInterop yields the callable VALUE via `__importDefault` (and `export = fn/class` are callable); the chain requirement is the FP firewall.
- **TS5097 ("import path can only end with '.ts' when allowImportingTsExtensions is enabled") has TWO emission branches in the import-specifier walker (Checker.kt ~23362, B141)**: (1) the original RELATIVE branch (`./foo.ts`); (2) the BARE branch for a `paths`-mapped specifier ending in `.ts`/`.tsx`. The bare branch approximates TS's **resolvedUsingTsExtension** rule with TWO load-bearing checks: the longest-prefix-matching `paths` pattern KEY must NOT itself end in the extension (`"baz/*.ts"` absorbs the `.ts` -> no error), AND a substituted `paths` target must end in `.ts`/`.tsx` AND directly name an existing source file in `fileResults` (use the DIRECT-key forms `t`/`/t`/`./t`, NOT `resolveModuleSpecifier` which does directory-index resolution -- else a package-dir name like `zone.tsx/index.d.ts` matched by `"*":["foo/*"]` wrongly fires TS5097). Both checks are required: dropping the file-resolution gate FP'd `pathMappingBasedModuleResolution_withExtensionInName`; the key-ext check is TS's pattern-absorption rule.

- **TS2694 for `X.Y` inside a `declare module "m"` body where `X` is a MODULE-LOCAL namespace MUST stay AST-based (`checkAmbientModuleLocalNamespaceMemberRefs`)**: the global symbol-merge UNIFIES the module-local and file-level `namespace X` (same name), so a symbol-table lookup wrongly sees the outer block's members and the error never fires. Single-file only — the cross-FILE merged-namespace sibling stays failing (Blocker #3).
- **A `.mts`/`.mjs`/`.d.mts` file is an ES module ONLY under node module kinds (`effectiveModule.isNodeNext`), NOT under commonjs/amd/system/umd** — the rule comes from `impliedNodeFormat`, computed only under node resolution; under amd/system/umd the GLOBAL module setting governs and `export =`/`import = require()` in a `.mts` is LEGAL (an unconditional .mts-is-ESM gate FP'd TS1202 on a 0-errors baseline). A `.d.mts` ESM declaration may NOT use `export =` (unlike a CJS-ambient `.d.ts`). `allowSyntheticDefaultImports`/`esModuleInterop` synthesize a default only for CJS-format targets — an ESM-format node-mode target with no `export default` is still TS1192.

### Module detection gotchas

MULTI-FILE test options BYPASS `parseCompilerOptions` (built in `parseMultiFileSource`'s own `applyDirective` loop) — any implied-option rule (e.g. `applyImpliedAllowJs`: checkJs⇒allowJs) must be applied at ALL THREE option-finalization sites: single-file parse, multi-file parse, and the driver's post-`optionOverrides` re-apply. Missing one silently drops files from binderResults → checker emits NOTHING.
- **Debugging "is this checker path reached?": gradle SUPPRESSES test stdout/stderr** (no `testLogging.showStandardStreams` in build.gradle.kts), AND the forked-JVM stderr does NOT land in the test XML's `<system-err>`. A `System.err.println` trace is therefore INVISIBLE under `./gradlew jvmTest`. To trace whether a check runs, emit a MARKER DIAGNOSTIC (`diagnostics.add(... code=29999 ...)`) — it surfaces in the `errorsMatchBaseline` expected-vs-actual diff. Also note: the Checker `init` gates most checks behind `if (!declarationOnly)`; `@emitDeclarationOnly` multi-file runs a `declarationOnly=true` checker that skips them.

- **A `declare global { namespace X { export const Y } }` member ALSO merges into the module that did `export as namespace X` (B555)** — two coupled pieces: (1) `collectGlobalAugmentationNames` registers the `declare global { namespace X }` name `X` as a global value name (so a bare `X.member` resolves and skips the UMD-global TS2686 reporter); (2) `checkCrossFileModuleAugmentationDuplicates` projects the namespace's `export const/let` members onto the UMD module's `(targetFile, name)` conflict groups via a `umd→moduleFile` regex map, and sorts `others` by (fileName, pos) so the hub's related-info is TS6203(first)+TS6204(rest) in source order (exportAsNamespace_augment). FP firewall: the projection fires only when `export as namespace X` exists AND the group reaches ≥2 decls (`total<2` guard; an empty-body `declare global { namespace N {} }` contributes nothing).
- `export as namespace X` has NO AST node — the parser MISPARSES it into a `namespace X` ModuleDeclaration (+ stray statements); detection is regex-only (`collectUmdGlobalsAndModuleFiles` etc.). Any walker scanning top-level statements for a genuine declaration of X is FOOLED by the misparse — exclude statements whose `.pos` falls within the regex-match span (`stmt.pos in match.range`).
- **`symbolToQualifiedName`'s `"basename".X` module-qualifier is EXPORT-gated, not declared-in-file-gated (B385)**: a file-level namespace renders as `"basename".X` ONLY when it is EXPORTED (part of the module's public shape) or imported from another module; a NON-exported local `namespace X {}` in an ES-module file renders unqualified (`X`). Gating on "declared in this file" instead regresses `internalAlias*` (`export namespace c` must keep `"file".c`). Companion: a USER type-only/UNINSTANTIATED namespace used in VALUE position (`Library.foo`) is owned by TS2708 — the namespace-member TS2339 in `checkMemberAccessMissing` must BAIL for it, but the bail must be gated `declarations.none { it in builtinLibDecls }` and placed AFTER the TS2550 lib-target check (else `Intl.PluralRules` loses its lib hint).
TS2567 enum-merge detection is split across THREE disjoint walkers (same-file per-file; `checkCrossFileEnumConflicts` for SCRIPT files only; `checkModuleAugmentationEnumMerge` for `declare module` augmentations). Do NOT broaden the script-only walker to module files — it FPs on legitimate module augmentations; extend the augmentation walker with gated branches instead.
- **A BARE (non-relative) ambient `declare module "X"` is a STANDALONE ambient module, NOT an augmentation of a sibling SOURCE file whose basename happens to be `X` (B387)**: `collectModuleAugmentations` merges augmented exports into the resolved target file's locals, but that merge must be SKIPPED when the specifier is bare AND the target resolves to a plain `.ts`/`.tsx` source (not `.d.ts`, not `node_modules`) — tsc only augments `.d.ts`/package ambient modules via a bare name, never a relatively-resolvable source. Without the skip, the bare ambient's members pollute the real source module's exports and a genuinely-missing-member access (`file1.bar()`) silently passes. Relative augmentations (`declare module "./foo"`) DO augment their `.ts` target — gate on the specifier being non-relative, not just the target file type.
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` does NOT resolve the `.`/`./` package-index specifier** (it returns null) — `import '.'` / `declare module '.'` mean "this directory's index". Any check needing that target must resolve it itself (cf. `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`, B412: sibling `index.{ts,tsx,d.ts}` in the importing file's directory). Multi-file test files are keyed by their `@filename` value verbatim (e.g. `./a.d.ts`, but `main.ts` without the `./`), so `dir = fileName.substringBeforeLast('/')` of `./a.d.ts` is `.` → `./index.d.ts`.
- **`resolveSpecifierAnywhere`/`resolveRelativeIncludingIndex` DO resolve bare `.`/`..` (B472), but ONLY to `<dir>/index` — NEVER to a sibling FILE whose stem equals the dir** (`.` from `/a/x.ts` → `/a/index.ts`, not the file `/a.ts`; the dir-index-only rule is keyed on the original `specifierIn == "." || ".."`, so route `.`/`..` through WITHOUT pre-normalizing to `./`/`../`). The default-import member-access TS2339 for such an import is a DEDICATED walker `checkDotDirIndexDefaultImports` (reads the resolved `export default {objLit}`'s keys), NOT the shared import-receiver member-access path (un-gating that is FP-risky, B152/B153). All B472 pieces are corpus-FP-safe: only `importWithTrailingSlash` imports from exactly `.`/`..`.
- **A checkJs CJS `module.exports = { name: "<lit>" }` JS export `name` (typed `string`) plus a cross-file `declare module "./X"` augmentation `export const/var name: T` is a DUPLICATE → TS2300 (B553, `checkCjsExportAugmentationConflict`)** — symmetric TS2300/TS6203 at the augmentation decl + the CJS object-literal key (reuses `emitAugReexportDup`), and a `import { name } from "./X"; name.<method>()` whose method ∉ `string` → TS2551 (the JS export wins so `name` is `string`). Purely ADDITIVE (CJS imports resolve to `any`; the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip B152/B153 stays gated). **GOTCHA: resolve the `./X` JS target with `resolveRelativeIncludingIndex` — `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`/`resolveModuleSpecifier` deliberately skip `.js`.** FP firewall: the augmentation member must be `export const/var` (an `interface` legally merges with a JS class export → excluded) AND the CJS target must be `module.exports = {objLit}` with a STRING-literal-valued matching key (corpus-unique).
- **A name declared in a `declare module "X"` AUGMENTATION that is ALSO re-exported into X via `export {N} from './other'` is a DUPLICATE (B412, `checkModuleAugmentationReexportDuplicates`)** — TS2300 for a type-alias aug decl, TS2451 for a block-scoped const/let, reported at BOTH the aug decl and the re-export specifier with cross-referencing TS6203. The whole `.d.ts` duplicate-identifier pipeline is skipped and the B92d walker (`checkCrossFileModuleAugmentationDuplicates`) only covers the target's OWN exports with EXPORTED aug decls, so this shape needed its own walker. FP-safe (a re-exported name + a non-mergeable aug decl is always a duplicate). **Which decl wins for TYPE RESOLUTION when both an augmentation `type Row2={a}` and a re-exported `interface Row2{b}` exist is FIXED (B507): tsc resolves the AUGMENTATION's local `type Row2={a}`.** Our cross-file symbol merge conflates the re-export alias (its `ImportSpecifier`/`ExportSpecifier` plus the re-exported `InterfaceDeclaration` pulled in via `mergeSymbolTable` pollution) with the augmentation's `TypeAliasDeclaration` onto ONE symbol that ends up flagged `Interface` — so `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker`'s `Class|Interface` branch resolved `{b}`. The fix is a FIRST `when` branch in `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker`: an `Alias` symbol carrying BOTH a `TypeAliasDeclaration` AND a re-export specifier (`ImportSpecifier`/`ExportSpecifier`) resolves the local type-alias. FP firewall: that exact decl-combo is corpus-unique (a plain interface, or a plain re-export of a type-alias, keeps its own resolution). The augmentation's decl reaches the symbol via the GLOBALS merge in `collectModuleAugmentations`; the LOCALS merge is skipped (`resolveModuleSpecifier(".")` returns null, would need `resolveAugmentationTargetFile`), but the globals path already carries the decl so it was unnecessary for this flip.
- **A BARE single-segment unresolvable specifier under EXPLICIT `@module: commonjs` gets TS2307 (B524) — but the FP firewall is load-bearing because the resolver does NOT model symlinks or untyped `.js` node_modules (the documented B98.r2 dead-end).** The `else if (!isRelative …)` B98.r107 branch in `checkUnresolvedModules` now includes `ModuleKind.CommonJS` BUT only with TWO extra suppressions vs the `null`/ES-module case: (1) `bareModulePackageInAnyInput(pkg)` consults the RAW input set `allInputFileNames` (untyped `.js` @Filename files are NOT in `fileResults` — `hasNodeModulesPackage` misses them) → covers `extendsUntypedModule`; (2) `bareModuleSymlinkTargetDir(pkg)` suppresses when a `<dir>/<pkg>/index.{ts,tsx,d.ts}` exists ANYWHERE (the symlink TARGET is the only thing materialized — the `node_modules/<pkg>/` symlink path itself is absent from both `fileResults` and `allInputFileNames`) → covers `moduleResolutionWithSymlinks`/`_withOutDir`. Both suppressions UNDER-fire (a coincidental `<pkg>/index.ts` dir, or any package we can't see, → no TS2307) which is the SAFE direction (we never fire bare-commonjs TS2307 otherwise → FN harmless). Do NOT remove the suppressions to "tidy up" — they ARE the firewall that makes this re-tread of B98.r2 land clean. `decoratorMetadataTypeOnlyImport` (`unknown-module`, no node_modules anywhere) is the sole flip.
Augmentation vs definition of `declare module "X"` is distinguished by ABSENCE of an inner `export =` (the definition carries it), NOT by `isModuleFile`. The shared `ambientModuleExportEqualsIsValueOnly` value-only gate (target declared in-block as var/function ONLY) is the FP firewall. Its paired TS2503 emit in `checkTypeNameResolved` MUST sit BEFORE the `!Module`/TS2702 path — the alias may carry a Module flag from the augmentation merge that would short-circuit it.
TS2303 lives in THREE disjoint emission sites by `moduleReference` kind/context: `checkCircularImportAlias` (entity-name cycles `import X = Y.Z`, skips .d.ts), `emitTS2303ForImportEquals` (bare-identifier unresolvable + re-exported under declaration:true), `checkCircularExportEqualsImportAlias` (`import = require`/`export =` cycles, incl. ambient and .d.ts). Pick the matching one when extending. Report-position rule (matches tsc): AMBIENT cycle entry → its own self-import; FILE entry → its PREDECESSOR; squiggle includes the trailing `;`.
- **TS2749 for a DEFAULT import used as a type (B466, `defaultImportIsStrictlyValue`/`defaultExportClassification`)**: `isValueOnlyTypeRef` suppresses TS2749 for aliases EXCEPT a default import whose target module's default export is unambiguously a VALUE (no type meaning). **MUST scan the CURRENT file's own `import` statements (`fileResults[fileName].sourceFile.statements`), NOT the alias symbol's `declarations`** — `mergeSymbolTable` addAll's same-name imports from OTHER files onto the shared symbol (CLAUDE.md merge-pollution), so iterating `alias.declarations` picks the wrong import and FPs (a `zzz`-from-./a used as type wrongly resolved ./b). Companion: an `export default interface` (type-space) MERGES with any value-providing default → it is excluded from TS2528 "multiple default exports" participants when a REF_VALUE/EXPR/class-or-function-DECL/local-`export{value as default}` default coexists (`checkMultipleDefaultExports`); two type-space defaults (interface + interface-REF_TYPE) still conflict.
Cross-file (script) class+function-with-body merge and clodule member collisions are TWO dedicated AST walkers (B528, `checkCrossFileClassFunctionMerge` / `checkCrossFileCloduleMemberConflict`, `duplicateIdentifiersAcrossFileBoundaries`), pure AST over `binderResults` (NOT symbol tables → sidesteps Blocker #3). A top-level class in one file + a same-named bodied function in ANOTHER → TS2813 (class) / TS2814 (function) + a TS6506 "add declare" related anchored at the class; a class + same-named namespace in DIFFERENT files with a member name in ≥2 value-kinds (static property vs exported var) → TS2300 + cross-referencing TS6203. **Load-bearing FP firewall: CROSS-FILE-ONLY gates — the class/function file sets must be DISJOINT (PIECE A) and member occurrences must span ≥2 DISTINCT files (PIECE B); a same-file pair is owned by `checkDuplicateDeclarations`/`checkCloduleValueSpaceConflicts` (double-emit otherwise).** Corpus-unique per a grep over the 490 multi-`@Filename` tests.
`checkCrossFileInterfaceMemberConflicts` detects only PROPERTY-vs-METHOD TS2300 — property-vs-property is deliberately NOT detected (identical-type merges are legal; different-type → TS2717, not TS2300; keeps FP surface zero). Collapse threshold (matches tsc `amalgamatedDuplicates`): <8 conflicting symbols per ordered file-pair → per-member TS2300+TS6203; ≥8 → one collapsed TS6200 per file + TS6201. Top-level interfaces count into the global scope only in non-module (script) files.
Cross-file mixed class/type-alias/const-let conflicts use the HUB model (matches tsc mergeSymbol dedup): the FIRST-processed decl is the hub, accumulating TS6203 (first other) then TS6204 (each subsequent); every other decl emits one diagnostic relating back to ONLY the hub (TS6203). The duplicate-identifier work is split across walkers 73a2 (`checkCrossFileIdentifierConflicts`)/73b/73g with disjoint gates; 73a2 registers handled block-scoped names in `crossFileIdentifierHandledBlockNames`, which 73b consults — bypassing that set double-emits.
- **`BaselineFormatter` pretty multi-file footer uses MIXED line endings (B92)**: the `Errors  Files` per-file summary table (emitted only when `pretty && errorCount>1 && distinctFiles>1`) has the `Found N errors in M files.` line + the blank separator line ending in CRLF (`\r\n`), but `Errors  Files` and each `<count.padStart(6)>  <file>:<firstLine>` row end in LF only (`\n`). This is TypeScript's actual byte output (verified via `od -c`) — don't "normalize" them to all-CRLF or the 9 footer-baselines (dup-identifier family + `manyCompilerErrorsInTheTwoFiles` + `typedefCrossModule5`) won't match.
- **Index signature param type validity (B68.4)**: When the index sig param type is an invalid `KeywordTypeNode` (`any`/`boolean`/`void`/etc., not `string`/`number`/`symbol`), the Parser SKIPS the TS1021 "must have a type annotation" emission and the Checker emits TS1268 instead (mirroring TypeScript — they don't double-report). The Checker walker `checkIndexSigInStatement` covers class / interface / module / TypeAliasDeclaration / VariableStatement TypeLiteral annotations.
`@module: none` + `@outFile`: (a) auxiliary `.js` files with import/export are NOT bundled into the outFile (type-info/allowJs-checking only); (b) dynamic `import()` is preserved verbatim for target≥ES2020, but below ES2020 rewritten to `Promise.resolve().then(() => __importStar(require(...)))` with the `./` prefix stripped. See `transformToCommonJS`/`buildCjsDynamicImport`.
- **`isolatedModules: true` ≠ `moduleDetection: force`**: `isolatedModules` tells TypeScript to check each file independently but does NOT force module treatment for files without imports/exports. Adding `isModuleFile = true` for `isolatedModules` causes regressions.
- **Node16/Node18/Node20/NodeNext default to CJS for `.ts` files (UNLESS a `package.json` declares `"type": "module"`)**: `isESModuleFormat` returns `false` for plain `.ts` files BY DEFAULT — only `.mts`/`.mjs` are unconditionally ESM. The `(options, fileName)` overload consults `options.packageJsonTypes` (populated by `collectPackageJsonTypes` from multi-file source `package.json` files outside `node_modules/`), walking from the file's directory upward to find the nearest enclosing `"type"` field. Use the `options`-aware overload in any new code that determines ESM vs CJS under nodenext — the legacy `(module, fileName)` overload has NO package.json context. Map keys preserve leading `/` (absolute paths) and use `""` for the implicit root.
- **`esModuleInterop` defaults to `true`**: TypeScript 5.5+ deprecated `esModuleInterop: false`. Test baselines without explicit `@esModuleInterop` expect helpers (`__importStar`, `__importDefault`). Only when `@esModuleInterop: false` is explicitly set should helpers be skipped for default/namespace imports.
- **`__importStar`/`__importDefault` conditional on `esModuleInterop`**: These helpers wrap `require()` only when `esModuleInterop: true`. Without it, use plain `require()`. `__exportStar`/`__createBinding` are ALWAYS used for `export * from` regardless of `esModuleInterop`.
- **A checkJs `.js` DEFAULT import binding to an ambient module's `export default <type-only namespace>` is type-only → TS18042 (import) + TS2708 (value uses) (B508).** `declare module "X" { namespace N { export type … }; export default N }` + `import D from "X"` in a `.js`+`@checkJs` file: the default binds to the type-only namespace `N`, so tsc emits TS18042 ("'D' is a type and cannot be imported in JavaScript files. Use 'import(\"X\").D' in a JSDoc type annotation." — the suggestion uses the LOCAL binding name, NOT `.default`) at the import binding, and TS2708 ("Cannot use namespace 'D' as a value.") at every value-position use (`typeof D`, bare `D`). New helper `ambientDefaultExportTypeOnlyNamespace(spec)` reads the ambient `declare module` body AST directly (B494 pattern — `resolveAlias` cannot reach an ambient module's `export default`); finds `export default <ident>` → a body-level `namespace <ident>` that is `isModuleBlockTypeOnly`. Wired in `checkTypeUsedAsValue`'s `ImportDeclaration` case (route the default name to `namespaceOnlyNames` not `valueNames` + emit TS18042, gated `isJsLikeFileName && options.checkJs`) AND the `TypeOfExpression` case — the `namespaceOnlyNames`→TS2708 branch was MISSING there (it only did `typeOnlyNames`→TS2693). `checkTypeUsedAsValue` runs on `.js` files (only `.d.ts` skipped), so this is consistent with the existing `.js` TS2693/TS2708 — NOT an un-gating of the load-bearing `checkPropertyAccess`/`checkCallExpressionTypes` `.js` skip (B152/B153). FP firewall: corpus-rare (4 tests carry TS18042).
- **A namespace import `import * as X` of a module whose exports are ALL type-only is itself type-only → tsc ELIDES the require, and this elision must be IMPORT-specific not name-based (B503).** The name-based `pureTypeNames = typeOnlyDeclaredNames - runtimeDeclaredNames` mechanism CANNOT catch the merge case: when the file also declares a same-name local `namespace X` (or any value `X`), that local puts `X` in `runtimeDeclaredNames`, so `X` is never "pure type" even though the IMPORT side is type-only (the namespace owns the value side; `X.foo`/`export {X}` bind to it, not the import). The fix is the conservative checker query `moduleHasOnlyTypeOnlyExports(modSpec, srcFile)` (true ONLY when the target resolves and EVERY export is a directly-declared type-only entity — interface/type-alias/uninstantiated-namespace/erasable-const-enum; false on ANY uncertainty: unresolvable, `export =`/`export default`, `export {…}`/`export *` re-exports, exported value decls, instantiated namespaces, `export import`), hooked at the CJS namespace-import emission and **gated `!options.isolatedModules`** (per-file emit can't prove type-only-ness there, so tsc keeps the require — un-gating it is a +1/-1 swap regressing `isolatedModulesReExportType`). FP-safe because under-classifying (→ keep require) is the current behavior; only a provably-type-only module elides.
tslib resolution is PER-FILE (nearest enclosing `node_modules/`, see `tslibResolvableFor`/`resolveTslibDedupKey`) — do NOT revert to a program-wide `hasTslib` boolean (it suppressed TS2354 for tslib-less packages whenever ANY package had tslib). Match `lastIndexOf("node_modules/")` NOT `"/node_modules/"` — fixtures mix leading-slash conventions and the no-slash form must still match.
TS2343 missing-tslib-helper dedups ONCE PER RESOLVED TSLIB INSTALL per helper (`reportedMissingTslibHelpers`, keyed `<tslibKey>|<helper>` via `resolveTslibDedupKey`). LANDMINE: the older helper emissions in `checkStmtForMissingHelper` (__exportStar/__extends/__rest/…) do NOT dedup — they pass only because their fixtures have a single occurrence. Any new helper check, or extending __awaiter to async methods/arrows, must gate on the install-keyed set or multi-occurrence tests get duplicate TS2343.
`exprContainsDynamicImport` (detection, drives `isModuleFile`) must handle EVERY expression kind that `rewriteCjsDynExpr` (rewriting) handles — keep the two in sync; a missing kind silently skips `transformToCommonJS` for files whose only module signal is a wrapped dynamic import.
`Emitter.hasModuleStatements` must mirror `Transformer.isModuleFile` (incl. dynamic-import detection; each has its own walkers — different files, cannot share). Drift produces a stray top-level `"use strict";` for AMD/UMD/System files. When adding module-signal detection to one, mirror it to the other.
- **`resolveModuleSpecifier` intentionally does NOT try `.js`/`.jsx` extensions**: Doing so globally causes TS2459 false-positives (our CJS/`module.exports` export analysis is incomplete) and other cross-file lookup regressions. For a specific check that needs JS-aware resolution under `allowJs`/`checkJs`, add a SCOPED helper (e.g. `resolvesAsJsOrJsx` used by `checkAmbientModuleAugmentations`) rather than modifying the main resolver.
The relative-missing TS2307 gate (Checker.kt ~20527) fires ONLY under EXPLICIT `@module: commonjs` — broadening to node10 (the no-`@module` default) re-exposes resolver gaps across many multi-file fixtures. A resolvable `.js`/`.jsx`/`.tsx` sibling (via the deliberately-permissive `resolveRelativeIncludingIndex`) must SUPPRESS TS2307 (tsc resolves those as untyped/TS6142, never TS2307). `hasTsErrorSuppressionAbove` is the ONLY `@ts-expect-error`/`@ts-ignore` handling in the codebase (narrow line-scan around the import line) — there is no general directive-suppression model.
- **ALL file locals merged into globals at Checker init** (`mergeSymbolTable(globals, result.locals)` for every binderResult): This conflates script-file locals (legitimately global) with module-file locals (should require an explicit import to be visible). Consequence: in file X, an identifier exported from an unrelated module file Y appears in X's scope chain via `scope.has(name)`. This blocks fine-grained module-visibility diagnostics. Splitting "true globals" from module-file locals would unblock several tests but has broad regression risk and needs per-file scope construction.

### Cross-file infrastructure gotchas

- **`node_modules/*.d.ts` files ARE parsed + bound + in `binderResults`, and their diagnostics DO reach the output** (despite a driver code comment saying node_modules files are "third-party and never reported on" — that comment is about EMIT, not diagnostics; `parsedSourceFiles[fileName]` is set BEFORE the emit-skipping `continue`). Confirmed by the passing `augmentExportEquals7` (reports a node_modules TS2649) and used by B443's `checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`. So a cross-file checker walk CAN emit diagnostics at node_modules positions — don't assume they're filtered.
- **A cross-file `declare global { namespace NS { … } }` merge is NOT modeled by the binder's symbol tables** — detecting a member-name CONFLICT across files in such a namespace (e.g. `type VNode` in one file vs `export import VNode` in another → TS2300) needs an AST-based dedicated cross-file walker (`checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`, B443), like the other cross-file conflict walkers. FP firewall: ≥2 DISTINCT files AND a type-alias in the mix (type-aliases are the only kind that can never merge).
- **A cross-file global `type N` (script file) + `namespace N` (DIFFERENT script file) is TS2649, NOT TS2300 (B449, `checkCrossFileTypeAliasNamespaceConflict`)** — tsc routes this degenerate global merge through the module-augmentation path: "Cannot augment module 'N' with value exports because it resolves to a non-module entity." at the type-alias name (the type alias is the "non-module entity"). AST-based (binder doesn't model the cross-file global merge). FP firewall is the SCRIPT-file gate (`!isModuleFile`): the only other corpus file with `type X`+`namespace X` is `reexportNameAliasedAndHoisted`, whose files are MODULE files (module-scoped → no global conflict, excluded). Cross-FILE only (same-file is TS2300, owned by the dup-identifier pipeline).
- **Function-call arity (TS2554) is cross-file via an OVERLAY, not per-file (B434, `checkArgumentCounts`)**: a per-file `funcParams` map only sees the current file's functions, so a SCRIPT-mode cross-file call (`f()` in B where `function f` is in A) was never arity-checked. A program-wide `crossFileFuncs` overlay (top-level FunctionDeclarations from all NON-module non-.d.ts files) is merged as a FALLBACK for NON-module files. Load-bearing FP firewall: merge ONLY for callees absent from BOTH the file's own funcParams AND its binder `locals` (a local binding of any kind shadows a cross-file function — without this guard a local non-function `f` + `f(1)` FPs); cross-file DUP names (same name declared in ≥2 files) are dropped (conservative). TS6210/TS6211 related-info must resolve against the DECLARING file (`FuncParamInfo.declFileName/declSource` → `emitTS2554TooFew(relSource, relFileName)`), else the param position is computed against the CALLER's source (wrong file+offset). Companion: JS-param optionality is JSDoc-aware — `paramInfo(params, isJsFile, jsDocRequiredParamNames(comments))`; a JS param is optional UNLESS a non-bracket `@param name` tag documents it (`@param [name]` stays optional).
- **`mergeSymbolTable` mutates the existing target symbol's `declarations` list**: When `target[name]` already exists, the helper does `existing.flags |= symbol.flags; existing.declarations.addAll(symbol.declarations)` — so calling it twice across the same `result.locals` corpus duplicates declarations on the SHARED symbol object. The init-block merge into `globals` already does this once for every cross-file same-name pair; any additional pass building per-file scope tables MUST NOT re-merge across binderResults via this helper. Use direct `map[name] = sym` assignment with first-write-wins for shared pools and last-write-wins for own-file overrides — no `addAll`, no symbol mutation. Failure mode is silent until a downstream check (e.g. TS6133 unused-T-param, declaration count) sees the duplicated declaration list and behaves incorrectly.
- **`isValueExport`/`isTypeOnlyExportName` cross-file lookups must NOT rely on symbol flags**: `mergeSymbolTable` pollutes the target file's `locals["X"]` symbol with flags merged in from importing files (e.g., `import { T } from "./type"; const T = 0` in file A causes /type.ts's T symbol to acquire BlockScopedVariable|Alias|ExportValue flags). Code that asks "is X exported from file Y as a value or type?" MUST scan `targetResult.sourceFile.statements` directly for declarations of X, not consult `targetResult.locals[X].flags`. Mirror pattern in `isValueExport` (Checker.kt:955) walks the AST and classifies each declaration's kind. Fall back to flag-based logic only when no direct declaration is found (ambient/re-export cases).

### Binder gotchas

- **A DOTTED `namespace X.Y.Z` records its INNERMOST segment symbol (`Z`) for the decl node** — `recordNodeSymbol` overwrites once per segment in `bindModuleDeclaration`'s loop, so `nodeToSymbol[decl]` is the LAST (innermost) segment, and same-named segments MERGE across declaration blocks. So `buildNamespaceScope`'s dotted branch (B512, Checker.kt) MUST resolve via `nodeToSymbol[stmt]` + walk `symbol.parent` `segCount` steps to add each segment's merged exports — NOT root the walk at `result.locals[firstSegment]` (the old code), which finds NOTHING when the dotted namespace is NESTED in another namespace (the first segment lives in the parent's exports, not file locals) → a merged sibling-block member is invisible → spurious TS2304 (`dottedModuleName`: `v` declared in a sibling `M.N.X.Y.Z` block). Companion: `findDeclarationRelatedInfo`'s TS2728 is OMITTED for a TS2552 suggestion that is an INTERMEDIATE (non-last) segment of a dotted namespace name (tsc gives only the innermost segment a `valueDeclaration`) — the `ModuleDeclaration` branch returns null when `decl.name` is a `PropertyAccessExpression` whose last segment ≠ the suggested name.
- **`declareSymbol` is FIRST-WINS for a Class+Class duplicate (B505), last-wins for every OTHER non-mergeable combo.** tsc keeps the FIRST class as the canonical `scope[name]` binding, so name/member resolution uses the first declaration's members (a later same-named class's members are NOT contributed — `class C{p} … class C{pe}` → `C` resolves to `{p}`, so `c.pe` is TS2339, not `c.p`). The later class still gets its own symbol (returned + node-recorded) for its own diagnostics. **TS2300 duplicate detection is AST-based (`checkDuplicateDeclarations` scans statements), so canonical-symbol choice does NOT affect duplicate detection**, and symbol-id allocation is unchanged (the 2nd symbol is still created) → no boundary id-drift. Gated to Class+Class ONLY: class+interface should MERGE (not first-wins) and var+class/fn+class keep last-wins (other tests depend on it).
- **`canMerge` must include Variable+Module**: `declare const b: T; declare namespace b {}` is valid TypeScript. The binder's `canMerge` must allow `Variable + Module` and `Module + Variable` merging, otherwise the second declaration silently overwrites the first symbol. This matters for `isTypeOnlyImportRequire` which then sees only the namespace and incorrectly elides the `require()`. **BUT (B98.r46): var+namespace merge is allowed ONLY when the namespace is VALUE-FREE (uninstantiated — only types/interfaces).** An INSTANTIATED namespace (one with value exports, e.g. `export var x`) is a SECOND VALUE binding → TS2300 even for `declare var` (`declare var console; namespace console { export var x }` conflicts). The `namespaceVarAllowed` gate (Checker.kt:~19710, duplicate-declaration TS2300/TS2451 path) is `group.filter{namespace}.all{ !isNamespaceInstantiated(it) }` — do NOT re-add a `|| allVarsDeclare` blanket allowance (the old comment "a declare var can merge with any namespace" was wrong; the empty-namespace `b{}` case survives because it's not instantiated).
- **`canMerge` must include Variable+Interface**: `interface Symbol {}` + `declare var Symbol: SymbolConstructor;` is the standard wrapper-type pattern in lib.d.ts. Without this merge, the var silently overwrites the interface — `Symbol` resolves to anyType in type position and `sym = Sym` skips type checking. Same gap blocks any user `class B extends A` where A is `interface A {}` + `declare var A: { new(): A }` (e.g. `staticMismatchBecauseOfPrototype`). Note: enabling this merge also makes lib types like `Object`/`Promise` resolve to real Type.Interface (was anyType), exposing two latent gaps in the relation engine — see `propertiesRelatedTo` OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter and async return-type Promise unwrapping below.
