- **Ambient module symbol overwritten by import alias**: `import x = require("X")` in the same file as `declare module "X"` creates an Alias symbol that overwrites the NamespaceModule in `locals["X"]` (Alias doesn't canMerge with Module). `isAmbientModuleTypeOnly` must analyze the ModuleBlock AST directly instead of looking up `locals[name]`.
- **Module augmentation vs definition**: `declare module "X"` in a module file (has imports/exports) is an augmentation. In a script file, it's a definition. `isAmbientModuleTypeOnly` must skip module files to avoid treating augmentations (which only add types) as the full module definition.
- **A `namespace`/`module` symbol does NOT carry bare `SymbolFlags.Module` — it carries `NamespaceModule` (uninstantiated) or `ValueModule` (has value members)**: a check that gates "is this a namespace?" on `flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Module)` SILENTLY returns false for most real namespaces (e.g. one with a `class`/`var` member is `ValueModule`). Use `hasAny(SymbolFlags.Module or SymbolFlags.NamespaceModule or SymbolFlags.ValueModule)` (cf. Checker.kt:71904/77421/4755). Symptom: a new namespace-detection walker fires for NOTHING (B393's first run was net-zero because of exactly this).
- **`SymbolFlags.ConstEnum` is set on namespaces holding only const enums**: `bindModuleDeclaration` sets `SymbolFlags.ConstEnum or NamespaceModule` when `ModuleInstanceState.ConstEnumOnly`. Cascades up: `namespace A { namespace B { namespace C { const enum E {...} } } }` makes A, B, C all carry `SymbolFlags.ConstEnum`. So `flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)` (= ConstEnum or RegularEnum) returns TRUE for these namespaces. Any code that distinguishes "real enum" from "namespace" must check `declarations.any { it is EnumDeclaration }` rather than relying on the flag alone.
- **Block-scoped declarations are NOT bound** (B83.5, 2026-05-27): `Binder.kt:bindStatement` only dispatches symbol-declaration cases via explicit `when` arms (Variable/Function/Class/Interface/TypeAlias/Enum/Module/Import/Export). The catch-all `else` branch silently ignores `WhileStatement`/`Block`/`IfStatement`/`ForStatement`/`TryStatement`/`SwitchStatement` and every other non-declaration statement, so any `class X` / `interface I` / `type T` / `enum E` / `function F` declared INSIDE their bodies is NEVER bound to file `locals` or `globals`. Consequence: any checker walker that looks up such a declaration by name via `globals[name]` / `result.locals[name]` / `perFileScope[file]?.get(name)` returns null. Symptom: TS2339/TS2551/TS2693/TS2304 silently never fires inside block-scoped class bodies because `classType = null`. Surgical fix pattern when the AST is in-hand: synthesize a transient `Symbol(SymbolFlags.Class, stmt.name.text)` with `declarations = [stmt]` and pass to `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol)`. The synthetic symbol must NOT be published to binder tables (`globals.put` / `result.locals.put`) — it's a checker-pass-local temporary; cross-decl merging or multi-pass identity is the binder's job. Used in `checkPropertyAccessInStatement`'s ClassDeclaration branch (Checker.kt:~61539); extend to similar `globals[stmt.name.text]`-based lookups if more shapes surface (other checker walkers that recurse into block bodies but resolve nested decls by name).
- **block-NESTED let/const redeclaration (TS2451) is owned by the dedicated additive walker `checkBlockScopedRedeclarations`, NOT the binder duplicate pipeline** (the binder doesn't bind block-scoped decls, above). It processes Block/switch/try/catch/finally scopes ONLY — function/method BODIES are already covered by an existing function-scope emitter, so processing them again DOUBLE-emits (the tell: a fn-body `let x; let x` reports TS2451 twice); recurse INTO function bodies for their nested blocks but don't treat the body itself as a scope. File/module top-level stays with the binder pipeline.
- **TS2451-vs-TS2300 for a let/const + var collision is decided by the FIRST declaration's kind** (duplicate pipeline `useTs2451`, Checker.kt ~28605): a block-scoped (let/const) declared FIRST → TS2451; a `var` declared first → TS2300. A `function` declaration anywhere in the SAME scope as the let also forces TS2300 (so let+var → TS2451 but let+var+function → TS2300).
- **`type X` + `const/let X` → TS2451 is a JS-ONLY rule (B485)**: in a `.ts`/`.tsx` file a type alias and a value const legally COEXIST (different declaration spaces) — NO error. tsc only reports the value-side redeclaration in a `.js` file, where `type X` is illegal (TS8008) and so does not occupy the type space. The duplicate-pipeline branch is gated `isJsLikeFileName(fileName)`; do NOT broaden it to `.ts` (the original 16.4ek comment's premise that `type foo=5; const foo=5` errors in TS was wrong — see longObjectInstantiationChain1's `type merge<…>` + `declare const merge`). **B485 had a HOLE (fixed round 210): the FOLLOWING general `allBlockScoped` branch (Checker.kt ~29237) did NOT exclude `type`, so a `.ts` `type X` + `const X` (which skips the JS-gated branch) fell through and FP'd TS2451 anyway.** The block-scoped redeclaration now counts only the let/const decls (`blockScopedDecls`, require ≥2) and emits TS2451 only for THEM — a `type` alias neither counts toward nor receives the diagnostic (so `type X` + single `const X` → no error; `type X` + 2×`let X` → TS2451 on the two lets only). Provably FP-safe: no passing `.ts` test can have tsc emit TS2451 on a type alias.
- **A `var` (or for-init `var`) in a NESTED block hoisting into a FUNCTION body whose TOP declares a same-named let/const → TS2451 on BOTH, owned by `checkVarHoistRedeclaration` (B455)** — the binder hoists nested-block vars into NAMESPACE/FILE scopes (so the duplicate pipeline's Block-var collection, gated `inNamespaceBody`, already fires TS2451 there) and body-TOP for-init vars are collected by `checkDuplicateDeclarations`, but NEITHER covers a function body's NESTED-block vars (binder doesn't bind function bodies). The dedicated walker handles function bodies only (collect body-top let/const ∩ nested-block vars). **COMPANION: `checkVarDeclForShadowing` (the TS2481 "cannot initialize outer scoped variable" walker) now SUPPRESSES TS2481 when a var matches the nearest function/MODULE body-TOP let/const** (threaded `bodyTop` set) — that's a redeclaration (TS2451 from binder for ns/file, from `checkVarHoistRedeclaration` for functions), NOT a TS2481. TS2481 still fires for a var matching an INTERMEDIATE-block let/const (the 6 legit TS2481 baselines are file-scope OR the let sits in an intermediate block, never a function/module body-top). Initializer-presence does NOT distinguish the codes (a no-init `var v` in `for(let v;;){var v}` is TS2481 because the let is the LOOP var, not body-top).

### Use-before-declaration / TDZ gotchas

- **An IIFE in a let/const initializer runs EAGERLY, so its body's references to block-scoped names are TDZ reads (TS2448/2449/2450), but a non-invoked arrow/fn captures LAZILY (no error) — both UBD walkers (`checkUBDForwardInExpr` forward-refs to LATER decls, `checkUBDInInitializer` self-refs to the var being initialized) detect the IIFE callee and walk its body (B456).** Helpers: `ubdIifeCallee` (callee unwrapping parens is arrow/fn-expr), `forEachUbdEvaluatedExpr` (eagerly-evaluated body exprs, NOT crossing nested function bodies — a nested IIFE re-enters via the handler), `collectUbdBodyBlockNames` (body-declared names SHADOW the outer TDZ set → excluded, FP-safety). **GATING matches tsc and is load-bearing**: a GENERATOR IIFE body doesn't run on call → skipped both walks; an ASYNC IIFE runs its synchronous prefix → forward refs to LATER decls fire but a SELF ref to the var being initialized does NOT (self-walk gated `!async`, forward-walk gated only `!generator`). The two mechanisms are POSITIONALLY DISJOINT (self: ref-after-decl via `selfNames`; forward: ref-before-decl via `blockDecls` positional `ref.pos < decl.pos`) so a given (ref,decl) is flagged by at most one → no double-emit.

### Type system gotchas

- **The weak-type rule (TS2559/TS2560, B482) is DEDICATED WALKERS, NOT the relation engine.** A WEAK target (object/intersection with ≥1 property, ALL optional, no call/construct/index sigs) is VACUOUSLY assignable from any source (our relation PASSES — all props optional → nothing required can fail), so tsc's "must share ≥1 property" check is an ADDITIONAL diagnostic fired AFTER a passing relation. FIVE disjoint emit sites: (1) the call-arg check in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (placed EARLY, right after `isRestParam` — the intervening fn-vs-fn/excess blocks `continue` past anything inserted later); (2) `tryEmitNestedWeakVarDecl` (var-decl one-level nested chain `let x: T = src`); (3) the B473 push-walker `pdduCheckExpr` extension (array method-call args never reach `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`); (4, round 196) `tryEmitTopLevelWeakVarDecl` (`let x: W = src`, W weak, at the var NAME — hooked BEFORE the primitive→object / object-literal→intersection TS2322 so it pre-empts the FP); (5, round 196) `tryEmitObjectLiteralWeakLeaves`/`descendObjectLiteralWeak` (fresh object-literal initializer, MULTI-level: a non-object-literal LEAF whose target prop type is weak → TS2559 at the property KEY + related TS6500). **FP firewall is `weakSourcePropertyNames` returning null (→ no emit) for union/intersection/typeparam/Reference/any/unknown/nullish sources** — be GENEROUS counting source props (callables add `FUNCTION_TYPE_PROPERTIES`, primitives use the wrapper apparent type) so a missed property never FPs (under-count → FP; over-count → harmless FN); PLUS the **empty-source guard** `if (srcNames.isEmpty()) return false` in `tryEmitWeakTypeAssignment` (`let x: AllOptional = {}` is vacuously fine — tsc emits nothing). **The object-literal-leaf descent (5) is ANNOTATION-TYPE-NODE-based, NOT resolved-Type-based** (`findWeakPropAnnotation` walks IntersectionType/TypeLiteral/TypeReference→alias-or-interface): `getTypeFromTypeNode` of an intersection annotation containing a `Partial<…>` member returns errorType (collapses the WHOLE intersection), so a Type-based descent bails — the TypeNode walk finds the prop in whichever constituent declares it and skips the unresolvable `Partial<…>`, and `formatTypeForDisplay(parentAnn)` renders `Partial<{…}>` SYNTACTICALLY for the TS6500 enclosing display even though it has no Type. SOURCE classification for (4) (`topLevelWeakSource`): `expr as T` → cast target + alias display; `E.A` enum-member (`getTypeOfExpression` returns anyType — special-cased via `enumMemberWeakSource` to the enum's string/number base for names + the enum NAME for display); primitive literal → wrapper apparent props + literal display. Display traps: source `12` stays literal (`literalTypeOfExpression`); a named function `getDefaultSettings(){return {a:1}}` renders `() => any` (our `inferReturnTypeFromBody` has no object-literal branch — DON'T add it, blast radius) so `weakFunctionDisplay` renders a Signature copy with the inferred object return for DISPLAY only; `expressionTrueEnd` overshoots an AsExpression cast by one token; an optional `any`/`unknown` prop renders `nope?: any` NOT `nope?: any | undefined` (`any` absorbs undefined — fixed in `formatTypeForDisplay`; the SAME pattern at typeToString ~84090 is deliberately untouched, fix it there only if a test needs it); the combined nested-chain inner display (`A1.x & B1.x` → `{ a?: …; } & { b?: …; }`) uses `collectPropertyTypesAcross` (all constituents), not `getPropertyAcrossType` (first only). Remaining weak tests (`intersectionPropertyCheck`) need intersection-SOURCE + non-weak pieces (TS2353 nested-excess, return-spread). **A SIXTH weak emit site (round 267, `checkNoInferIntersectionWeakArgs`, noInferCommonPropertyCheck1)**: a generic-fn CALL `test<T…>(a, …, weakArg)` whose weak-arg param is a NoInfer-bearing intersection (`NoInfer<T> & {prop?}`, `NoInfer<T1> & NoInfer<T2>`, or bare `NoInfer<T1 & T2>`) → TS2559. Engine resolves `NoInfer<T> & {…}` to errorType (NoInfer unmodeled + T unbound) so the general path is silent → purely ADDITIVE. AST-only: each TP binds from its positional bare-`T` arg's `Partial<{…}>` declaration; the display preserves `Partial<…>` via `formatTypeForDisplay` of the arg's annotation TypeNode (`getTypeOfExpression` MATERIALIZES Partial → wrong display). **tsc's NoInfer display is INCONSISTENT and the walker hardcodes it: `NoInfer<bareTP>` KEEPS the wrapper (`NoInfer<Partial<…>>`), but `NoInfer<T1 & T2>` DROPS it and expands (`Partial<…> & Partial<…>`).** Corpus-unique (only intersection-NoInfer test; the union sibling `noInferUnionExcessPropertyCheck1` is owned by `tryEmitNoInferUnionExcessPropTs2353`).
- **Index-signature assignability is asymmetric source-vs-target (B418b)**: a SOURCE index signature does NOT satisfy a TARGET's required NAMED property — `propertiesRelatedTo` must NOT shortcut `sourceProp==null && source.stringIndexInfo!=null` to "present" (`{[k:string]:any}` is NOT assignable to `{one:number}` → TS2741, same as the number-index source). Conversely a PRIMITIVE source vs an ANONYMOUS index-sig-only target relates via the source's APPARENT type: `string`→`{[n:number]:any}` PASSES (String has a numeric index sig) but `string`→`{[s:string]:any}` and `boolean`→`{[n:number]:any}` FAIL — the `structuredTypeRelatedTo` branch must check the apparent type HAS each index sig the target needs (string-index source also covers a numeric-index target). Delegating to `checkTypeRelatedTo(apparent, target)` OVERSHOOTS — `objectTypeRelatedTo`'s index-sig check does NOT enforce source index-sig PRESENCE when the target value type is `any`.
- **An INTERSECTION-of-string-index-sig target value-check (TS2322 missing / TS2353 excess) is a DEDICATED walker `checkIntersectionIndexSigValueExcess`, NOT the relation engine.** `objectTypeRelatedTo` PASSES `{ y: {a:0} }` against `{[x:string]:{a:0}} & {[x:string]:{b:0}}` — the intersection has no NAMED props (`propertiesRelatedTo` true) and the index-sig-value-presence check only runs for nominal/function sources (an anonymous object-literal source is skipped), so a source property's VALUE is never checked against the intersected index-value type. The walker gates to a var annotated as an `IntersectionType` (≥2) whose every member is a `TypeLiteral` holding a SINGLE string-keyed `IndexSignature` with a `TypeLiteral` value (corpus-unique; a `Record<…>` member is a `TypeReference` → excluded), then per RHS object-literal property value: missing required prop → TS2322 at the OUTER key + TS2728 at the prop decl; excess prop → TS2353 at the excess key. **The value display MUST be built AST-based via `literalTypeOfExpression` (`{ a: 0; }`)** — tsc contextually pins `a:0` from the index-sig value type, but `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` (no contextual type set in the init walker) WIDENS it to `{ a: number; }`.
- **`Parameters`/`ReturnType`/`ConstructorParameters` are now MATERIALIZED (B407, `materializeSignatureUtility`, symbol==null dispatch in `getTypeFromTypeReference`)** — `Parameters<F>`/`ConstructorParameters<F>` resolve to a real TUPLE of the sig's param types, `ReturnType<F>` to the return type (not errorType anymore). Additive: returns null (→errorType) for rest/optional params or no matching sig. The TS2344 "does not satisfy `(...args: any) => any`" for `Parameters<typeof Class>` (constructor type, no call sig) is a SEPARATE check (`checkUtilitySignatureConstraint`) gated on the whole ref resolving to errorType.
- **Array-literal→tuple element checking (TS2322) is VAR-DECL-only (`checkArrayLiteralElementsAgainstTuple`, B407)** — the relation engine still SKIPS array→tuple (Checker.kt ~64488 "needs contextual typing, not yet implemented"); the var-decl path now handles the array-LITERAL case element-wise. To cover call-arg/assignment tuple targets, extend that walker (do NOT un-skip the relation engine — array-VARIABLE→tuple is a different, intentionally-conservative path).
- **`getTupleType` COLLAPSES a VARIADIC tuple's rest element to its ARRAY type as a single `tupleElementTypes` entry (B526)**: `[...number[], number]` → `tupleElementTypes = [number[], number]`, so prop "0" is `number[]` (wrong — element access should give `number`). There is NO rest/minLength tracking on the tuple `Type.Object`. So tuple-element semantics (element access past the fixed count, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` `| undefined`) CANNOT be derived from the resolved Type — they need the AST `TupleType` node. `indexedAccessWithVariableElement`'s `const x:number = recv[i]` is handled by a dedicated AST walker (`receiverVariadicTupleNode` + `vtiaCheckVarDecl`, reusing B221's `trduTupleOf`/structure-parse) hooked as a SUPPRESSION guard at the top of `checkVarDeclAssignability` (the collapsed-model standard check FP's on rest-region indices + misses out-of-bounds, so delegating+returning is strictly better). tsc's rule: a literal index `i` gets `| undefined` iff `i >= fixedBefore + fixedAfter` (count of NON-rest elements); the rest's ELEMENT type is the rest `ArrayType`'s `.elementType` (unwrap it — the raw rest node is the array). Any FUTURE variadic-tuple feature (destructuring past min length is the separate B221 `checkTupleRestDestructureUndefinedUse`; call-arg/assignment tuple indexing; tuple length/assignability) must likewise read the AST node, NOT `tupleElementTypes` — or fix the representation to track `fixedBefore`/`fixedAfter`/`restElementType` (broad).
- **The per-property arg-check loop in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (Checker.kt ~102925) is `!isRestParam`-gated, so an object-literal arg at a REST position (`arr.push({…})`, any `...rest` param) is NEVER per-property-checked there.** B473's `checkArrayPushDiscriminatedUnionElements` is the dedicated walker for the one sub-case that needed it: `<recv:T[]>.push({ p: [<objLit>] })` where `p`'s element type is a discriminated union and an element selects ONE member by a shared literal discriminant but misses its required props → TS2322 "not assignable to `<unionAlias>`" + chain "Property X missing… required in type `<member>`" + TS2728. Reuses `arrayElementUnionAlias` + `collectMissingProperties` (FP-safe); the SOURCE display preserves the contextual literal (`{ type: "A"; }`) via a local builder, NOT widened `getTypeOfObjectLiteral`/`typeToString` (which widens to `string`). Corpus-rare gate (only this push shape) = zero double-emit. To cover other rest-position contextual object-literal checks, extend the walker, NOT the `!isRestParam` loop.
- **A FRESH empty array literal `[]` used as a method-call receiver is `never[]` in tsc, but we type ALL `[]` as `any[]` (B87.6, deliberate — evolving-var/arg FP-avoidance, do NOT change globally).** So "X can never satisfy never[]'s method args" diagnostics are AST-SHAPE-GATED dedicated branches at the TOP of `pdduCheckExpr` (B487/B487b), matching an empty-`[]` receiver directly (bypassing the any[] typing): `[].splice(start,deleteCount,...items)` → TS2345 at the FIRST value item vs `never` (tsc reports only the first); `[].concat([{…}],…).map(b=>b.prop)` → TS2769 (2-overload `ConcatArray<never>[]` chain, widened element display via `widenType`) at the first concat element + TS2339 on `never` in the chained map callback body. Corpus-FP-safe: the ONLY other direct `[].<method>` calls are `[].push.apply(…)` (call callee is `[].push.apply`, name "apply" → not matched). B487c is the sibling "ARRAY arg can never satisfy a `{ [x:string]:T }` param" (arrays carry a numeric, not string, index sig) → TS2345 "Index signature for type 'string' is missing" — a bare-Identifier-callee branch in the same fn, gated to a generic fn with a single `{[x:string]:<bare TP defaulting to unknown>}` param + array arg (param display hardcoded `{ [x: string]: unknown; }`). These are all "structurally-impossible assignment, corpus-unique shape" walkers — the pattern that still yields clean FP-safe +1s after find_candidates' ≤3-code window and triage agents both miss them. **B559 is the VAR-DECL sibling: `var x: <named class/interface> = []` (empty array → a non-array named type) — a dedicated branch in `checkVarDeclAssignability` (right after `displayTarget`) overrides the missing-property source display to `never[]` (not our `any[]`) and reorders the missing set OWN-first via `ownInstanceMemberNamesOfInterface` (own-body members before inherited), then `return`s (replacing the general `any[]`+base-first emission). Gated `init is ArrayLiteralExpression && init.elements.isEmpty() && targetType is Type.Interface` (an array-TYPE target is a `Type.Reference`, NOT `Type.Interface` → excluded) + non-empty missing set.**

- **The `getPropertyElaborationChain` missing-property LEAF emits the MULTI-prop form `Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b` for ≥2 missing (own-first via `ownInstanceMemberNamesOfInterface`), and the single-prop `Property 'X' is missing in type 'Y' but required in type 'Z'.` for exactly 1 (B559).** Previously it ALWAYS used the single-prop form (first missing only) — wrong for ≥2 (e.g. array-element chain `C3[]`→`C1[]` listing IM1,C1M1). The gate stays `getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol(source,target) != null` so this changes only the FORMAT, never whether the chain line appears; for the multi-prop branch it CLEARS `lastChainMissingPropSymbol` (tsc attaches no single TS2728 "declared here" when ≥2 props are missing). The same-target-ref array-element chain's no-header terminal check (`firstTrimmed.startsWith("Property '")`) was broadened to ALSO match `contains("is missing the following properties")` so the multi-prop line stays a 2-space terminal (no parent header). Full-suite-verified net-zero — despite the broad call surface, no passing test relied on the single-prop form for a ≥2-missing chain.
- **NameScope carries type-param CONSTRAINTS (`typeParamConstraintOf`, B406)** — populated at `addTypeParam(name, constraint)` sites; a new `addTypeParam` call should pass `it.constraint` if available. Used to detect e.g. `T extends string[]` for the indexed-access TS2536 check. An invalid string-literal index in an indexed-access TYPE (`string[]["0.0"]`→TS2339, `T["0.0"]`→TS2536) fires ONLY for a numeric-LOOKING-but-non-canonical key (`isNumericLiteralName`: canonical iff `String(Number(s))===s`) on an array-shaped object — never a valid array member/index, so FP-safe.

- **Generic type-parameter DEFAULTS validation (TS2706/TS2716/TS2344/TS2707/TS2345) lives in `checkGenericDefaultsValidation` + `checkGenericDefaultParamCall` (B498), all SELF-CONTAINED (AST + the assignability engine) — NOT a relation-engine opening.** Load-bearing tsc rules (the source of the 2 transient build FPs): **TS2716 "circular default" fires ONLY for a bare ENCLOSING-name self-ref** (`interface SelfReference<T = SelfReference>` — default = the generic's own name, NO type args), gated `tp.constraint == null`. tsc does NOT flag a DIRECT self-ref `<T = T>` (genericDefaults baseline has ZERO TS2716) nor `<T extends C = T>` (the constraint breaks the cycle). **TS2344 default-vs-constraint must SKIP when the default-TP's OWN constraint satisfies the target** (`<T extends string = T>` — T trivially satisfies `string`; our relation engine's TypeParam-source path under-resolves, so an explicit `checkTypeRelatedTo(dType.constraint, cType)` skip is needed, mirroring the existing type-arg TS2344 logic ~94442). The TS2344 chain follows the B214 rule: related TS2208 ("might need an `extends C` constraint") when the DEFAULT is an unconstrained TP; "could be instantiated" chain when the CONSTRAINT is an unconstrained TP; "Type 'X' is not assignable" when the default is a constrained TP. **TS2707** ("requires between N and M type arguments") is emitted at the two `checkTypeArgCount`/`checkHeritageTypeArgCount` bail points (`info.minRequired != maxTotal`) — previously these just `return`ed. TS2558 (call-arity-with-defaults) + TS2428 (interface-redecl-non-identical-TPs) were ALREADY handled elsewhere.
- **`new`-expression constructability has dedicated cases for non-Identifier callees (B497, `checkSingleNewExpressionTypes`)**: a primitive-LITERAL callee (`new 53()`→Number, `new ''()`→String) or a `new`-expression callee whose returned INSTANCE has no construct sig (`new new Date`→Date) → TS2351 "Type X has no construct signatures" — these fall through `getCalleeType` (anyType) so they're handled as a dedicated block BEFORE the `val args = expr.arguments ?: return` guard. That guard was MOVED below the union-callee branch so a NO-ARGS union callee (`new union;`) is still constructability-checked. Separately, `new <recv>[]` (empty element access — `argumentExpression is OmittedExpression`, the TS1011 shape) on a value-position class → TS7053 "expression of type 'any' can't be used to index type 'typeof X'" (`typeofClassValueDisplay`): the namespace member MUST carry the `export` modifier — the binder puts ALL namespace members in `.exports`, and a non-exported `M.ClassA` is itself a TS2339 error with NO TS7053 (`cannotInvokeNewOnErrorExpression`). The display renders the LAST segment (`typeof T`, not `typeof M.T`).
- **Two DISTINCT typed-array interfaces are never mutually assignable (B496, `checkTypedArrayCrossAssignment`)**: `x = y` between two `let z = new <TypedArray>(<num>)` locals (no annotation) whose typed-array interfaces DIFFER → TS2322 with the `[Symbol.toStringTag]` literal-mismatch chain. Dedicated AST-shape walker (corpus-UNIQUE `<typedArrayVar> = <typedArrayVar>;` shape; the named→named relation gate skips lib-interface comparison). **Our embedded typed-array interfaces are structurally INCOMPLETE** (e.g. `Int16Array` lacks methods `Int8Array` declares + none model `[Symbol.toStringTag]`), so the `canUse&&!isAssignable` assignment branch (Checker.kt ~75833) FPs a name-presence TS2740 — SUPPRESSED there when both `sourceType`/`tt` symbol names ∈ `TYPED_ARRAY_NAMES` (the walker owns the correct TS2322). `new T(<numericLiteral>)` always yields `T<ArrayBuffer>` (hardcoded in the display).
- **A homomorphic mapped type `{ [P in keyof T]: … }` over a constraint union containing a `readonly [...]` tuple yields a READONLY result → not assignable to a MUTABLE `any[]` (B495, `checkHomomorphicMappedCastToArray`)**: dedicated AST-shape walker for `const x: any[] = <expr> as Homo<T>` (T constrained `… | readonly [...]`). PURE AST — homomorphism = `mapped.typeParameter.constraint` is `TypeOperator(KeyOfKeyword, TypeReference(<alias's own TP>))`; readonly-ness = a `TypeOperator(ReadonlyKeyword, TupleType)` member in the cast TP's constraint `UnionType`; mutable target = `ArrayType(KeywordTypeNode(AnyKeyword))` NOT wrapped in a readonly TypeOperator (a `readonly any[]` target is fine). No mapped-type evaluation. Corpus-UNIQUE shape (FP-safe by construction).
- **TS2799/TS2800 ("too large to represent") for a variadic-tuple / const-array spread chain is a DEDICATED AST walker `checkExcessivelyLargeTupleSpread`, NOT the type engine (excessivelyLargeTupleSpread)** — our `getTupleType` collapses a `RestType` to ONE element (never expands the spread), so the resolved tuple length is always the literal element count and the engine never hits tsc's `length >= 10000` limit → additive. THREE branches, all top-level-only + length-gated (fire ONLY at computed length ≥ 10000, first-crosser only = direct sub-aliases all < 10000), so FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION: (A) concrete tuple-alias chain `type T14 = [...T13, ...T13]` → TS2799 on the TupleType; (B) `const a14 = [...a13, ...a13] as const` → TS2800 on the ArrayLiteralExpression; (C) recursive doubling builder `type A = BuildTuple<3>` (a generic alias whose conditional false-branch is a self-ref with a `[...T, ...T]` 2nd arg, used with a NON-power-of-2 numeric literal → never terminates) → TS2799 on the use TypeReference. **Companion fix: B498's default-vs-constraint check FP'd `[any]` does-not-satisfy `any[]` (the relation engine lacks tuple→array here) — a narrow correct skip was added (a TUPLE default satisfies an ARRAY constraint when each element relates to the array's element type).**
- **TS2589 ("excessively deep / infinite") for a recursive conditional-type ALIAS is a DEDICATED AST walker `checkRecursiveConditionalAliasInstantiation` (round 268, recursiveConditionalCrash4), NOT the type engine** — our conditional eval short-circuits infer-bearing conditionals to errorType and never recurses to a depth bail, so we emit no TS2589 for these. The ONLY FP-safe syntactic signal it uses: a generic alias `A<…>` whose body contains a ConditionalType where BOTH `trueType` AND `falseType` are self-references to `A` (`Foo<T> = … ? Foo<T> : Foo<unknown>`) — that conditional has no base case → mathematically non-terminating → tsc ALWAYS emits TS2589 (so zero FP by construction; a valid recursive conditional like `Awaited<T>=T extends Promise<infer U>?Awaited<U>:T` self-refs in only ONE branch). Squiggle = the FALSE-branch self-call (tsc's target), width via angle-bracket matching (`typeRefSourceWidth`). Does NOT generalize: `awaitedType` (recursive thenable + TS7010/TS2493) and `recursiveConditionalTypes` (needs the relation engine for its TS2322/TS2345 chains) need real recursive instantiation with a converging depth bail.
Conditional-type `infer` is AST-guided POSITIONAL unification (`tryEvaluateConditionalWithInfer`), not type-level — `getTypeFromTypeNode` yields errorType for infer-bearing extends-types. FP firewall (load-bearing): ONLY the positive-unification path returns non-null; union-checkType / different-target / arity / non-infer mismatches all return null so the caller falls back to the prior anyType behavior. Bindings layer onto `currentTypeAliasArgs` (a non-null map bypasses the nodeTypes cache → fresh resolution). Tuple/array/fn-type infer positions and union distribution are NOT handled.
- **A DEFERRED conditional-alias param resolved via its tp's CONSTRAINT (TS2322) is the dedicated walker `checkDistributiveConditionalConstraint` (B564, distributiveConditionalTypeConstraints), NOT `getTypeFromConditionalType`** — that eval bails to anyType for a bare-TypeParam checkType (~118716), so `function f<T extends C>(x: Cond<T>) { let v: <lit> = x }` (where `type Cond<P> = P extends Ext ? <litA> : <litB>`, both branches LITERAL) resolves `Cond<T>` to anyType and the var-decl check is SILENT → purely ADDITIVE. The deferred conditional is resolved AST-side via T's CONSTRAINT (no general conditional eval): `assignable(constraint, Ext)` → TRUE branch; `disjoint` → FALSE branch (both display the alias `Cond<T>` at top + chain "Type '<branch>' is not assignable to type '<target>'."); `overlap-but-not-assignable` → the union of both branches, displayed as `boolean` when the branches are `true`/`false` (NO chain). **The OVERLAP check is per-union-member and load-bearing: overlap = `assignable(Ext,constraint) || any-member-of-Ext-union assignable to constraint` — `string` vs `"abc"|42` is NOT disjoint because `"abc" ⊆ string` → boolean, NOT the false branch.** FP firewall: corpus-unique shape (single-conditional-literal-branch alias + constrained-tp arg + literal-target var-decl) + emit ONLY when the relation genuinely fails (additive); the target is gated to a `LiteralType`, so a `boolean`/union target is naturally excluded (no FP). Squiggle = the var NAME (length = name).
- **The B565–B572 cluster are all dedicated ADDITIVE walkers for engine-gap shapes (the general path resolves them to `any`/errorType so it emits NOTHING) — corpus-unique gates, chains reconstructed from the AST (B540/B564 template).** Roster: **B565** `checkGenericConditionalReturnTypeAssign` (`x = a2`, a2 `ReturnType<T[M]>`, T extends a homomorphic `FunctionsObj<T>` mapped, → 7-level apparent-type chain; `keyof <unconstrained TP>`→bare string so silent). **B566** `checkCircularBaseTypeReferences` (`type A = I[..]` + `interface I extends Base<A>` → TS2310 on I + TS2456 on A; `interface S extends Alias<S>` with Alias a `[K in keyof <Alias-param>]` mapped → TS2313 on K + TS2751 + TS2310 on S — NO double-emit because `checkCircularInterfaceBases` follows only the extends HEAD name and the existing TS2456 needs a DIRECT identifier self-cycle). **B567** `checkInstantiationExprAliasCast` (`e as Alias<X>` where Alias is over `typeof Class<T>`; `getTypeFromTypeQuery` DROPS `.typeArguments` so both sides collapse equal → silent; TWO hardcoded display shapes — alias-body `TypeQuery` fully expands `{ new (): C<X>; prototype: C<any>; } & (() => X)`, alias-body `IntersectionType` preserves the alias name). **B568** `checkMappedSpyValueMemberAccess` (`recv[idx].m.prop`, recv `Alias<T> = T & {[k in keyof T]: ValueIface}`, ValueIface.m typed `Function`, prop ∉ `FUNCTION_TYPE_PROPERTIES ∪ RUNTIME_PROPERTIES` → TS2339). **B569** `checkInvariantGenericThisElaboration` (`x: Runtype<any> = Num` where Runtype has a polymorphic-`this` `constraint: Constraint<this>` member → fixed depth-2 chain; squiggle the var NAME (branch A) or the property KEY + TS6501 at the index sig (branch B `Obj({k:Num})`)). **B571** `checkOptionalMappedArrayParamArg` (`fn(foo, key, value)`, fn's object param `{[x in K]?: Lower<T>[]}`, foo[key] element ≠ widened value → TS2345 5-line chain; inference bails on the `Lower<T>[]` param). **TWO load-bearing specifics:** (1) **B570 `checkRecordStringAmpEmptyIndexAccess`** — `a.<nonLiteral>` where `a: Record<KU, V>` and KU has a `(string & {})` member resolves to named props (the literals) PLUS a STRING INDEX SIG → a non-literal access is `V | undefined` under noUncheckedIndexedAccess → **TS18048, NOT TS2339**; the `string & {}` member is the load-bearing distinguisher (without it KU is a finite literal union → TS2339). No `Record` materializer → silent → additive. (2) **B572 `checkTemplateUnionIntersectionComplexity` is a SUPPRESS-AND-REEMIT, not purely additive** — `x = y` where `y: A & T2`, `x: A` (legal → suppress our FP TS2322) or `x: A | null` (→ TS2859 ×2), A a high-cardinality template-literal-union alias (our shallow `TemplateLiteralType` parse → bare string, so the relation FP-emits TS2322). It MUST run BEFORE `checkTypeAssignability` (Checker.kt:1709), registers the assignment's `BinaryExpression.pos` in `relationComplexityHandled`, and a guard at `checkAssignmentExpression` ENTRY (`expr.pos in relationComplexityHandled → return`) suppresses the FP. Cardinality gate = `head.rawText` placeholder count (`split("\${")`) ≥3 (templateSpans is always empty — shallow parse). **B573 `requireOfJsonFileWithoutResolveJsonModule`** is a TWO-FILE split (driver + checker): `import X = require('./y.json')` where y.json content is ONLY bare identifiers + whitespace (`contents Not read`) is a recovered object literal in tsc. Driver `scanMalformedBareJson` (TypeScriptCompiler.kt, beside `scanJsonKeysForTS1327`, runs for EVERY .json file) emits `'{' expected.`+TS1136 at the first token, `',' expected.`+TS1136 at each subsequent, `'}' expected.` at EOF (0-width); the FP-safe gate is "content has NONE of `{ } : " ' [ ] , .`" (excludes valid/structured json — corpus-unique; NOTE a single bare keyword `true`/`false`/`null` IS valid json but no corpus fixture has that content). Checker `checkRequireOfMalformedJsonInTs` (NOT checkJs-gated — these are `.ts` files; `import = require`, not `const = require`) gives X the shape `{ <id>: any; ... }` and flags `X.<prop>` for prop ∉ the bare-id keys → TS2339. Both additive (the require resolves to `any` today). **B574 `checkNonHomomorphicMappedKeyofAssign`** — `a = b` between vars typed `Gen2<E.X>`/`Gen2<E.Y>` where `Gen2<T> = { [P in keyof Gen<T>]: … }` and `Gen<T extends E> = { disc: T } & ( {disc:E.A,…extrasA} | {disc:E.B,…extrasB} )`: `keyof Gen2<E.X>` = `{disc} ∪ extras-of-branch-X` (the `{disc:T}` intersection collapses the non-matching branch — its disc becomes `E.X & E.Y = never`). Engine → anyType (`getKeyofType` has NO `Type.Intersection` branch → `stringType` → `getTypeFromMappedType` bails) → additive. Reconstruct the key sets AST-side → TS2741 + related TS2728 (at the missing prop's decl in the matching Gen branch).
- **A SUBCLASS-of-Array source (`class C<T> extends Array<T>` → `C<A>`) assigned to a `ReadonlyArray<B>` whose element FAILS elaborates the `concat(...)` RETURN-type collapse, NOT the generic property-mismatch chain (B575, the subclass branch in `getPropertyElaborationChain` + `arrayElementTypeOfSubclass`)**: tsc reports "The types returned by 'concat(...)' are incompatible…  Type 'A[]' is not assignable to type 'B[]'…  Property 'b' is missing in type 'A' but required in type 'B'.". The B75.3 plain-Array elaboration shortcut is gated `source.target === globalArrayType` (EXCLUDES subclasses), and the general property iteration mishandles the inherited `concat` as a function mismatch — wrongly reporting the CONTRAVARIANT param `B[] ≁ A[]` instead of the COVARIANT return `A[] ≁ B[]`. The new branch (gated subclass-of-Array source vs ReadonlyArray target) mirrors B75.2's base-type walk (`arrayElementTypeOfSubclass`: walk `srcInterface.baseTypes` for an Array ancestor, `instantiateType` with the source's type-arg mapper) to get the source element type, then reuses `getPropertyElaborationChain(srcElem, tgtElem)` (so TS2728 "'b' is declared here" fires via `lastChainMissingPropSymbol`). FP-safe: 0 baselines carry the old "Types of property 'concat'" param chain; only 2 corpus tests have the extends-Array + ReadonlyArray shape and the other (`genericCallAtYieldExpressionInGenericCall2`) passes → elaboration unreached.
- **A conditional-FILTER mapped handlers param `handlers: { [P in keyof T as T[P] extends <prim> ? P : never]: (value: T[P], prop: P) => void }` on a generic fn called `f({data}, {handlers})` is a DEDICATED additive walker `checkGenericFilteringMappedHandlerCalls` (B576), NOT mapped-type eval**: tsc infers T (WIDENED) from the data literal, the `as` filter keeps only handler keys whose data value extends `<prim>`, and a handler key NOT in the surviving set → TS2353 (excess, against the FILTERED display `{ <s>: (value: <prim>, prop: "<s>") => void; }`) + TS7006 on that handler arrow's un-annotated params (which then have no contextual type). Our engine resolves the filter mapped type to anyType → SILENT → additive. The display value type is `T[P]` WIDENED (`value: string`) but the prop is the LITERAL key (`prop: "bar"`). A VALID handler key (in the surviving set) is SKIPPED — its params are contextually typed so our checker already emits nothing (NONE test); the walker only ADDS the invalid-key errors. A non-conditional mapped `as` (`[P in keyof T as P]`) keeps `nameType` non-conditional → excluded. Corpus-unique: the conditional-filter mapped shape is in 3 corpus files, the other 2 are type-alias/declaration contexts, not a generic fn call.
TypeScript UNFOLDS a type-alias that resolves to a PRIMITIVE in error displays (`type N = number; let x: N = "s"` shows 'number', not 'N'); object/interface aliases keep their name. Use `displayTargetAnnotation` — currently wired only at the general var-decl TS2322 site; route any other site through it when it shows an alias name where tsc shows the primitive.
`formatPropertyDisplayName` preserves the SOURCE quote char of a non-identifier string-literal property name (via `nameNode.singleQuote`) — do NOT hardcode one quote style; baselines require both single- and double-quoted renders. Numeric-literal names (`{ 0: 1 }`) are NumericLiteralNode → rendered unquoted.
`elementAccessResultType` distributes over Type.Union receivers ONLY when EVERY member yields a concrete (non-any/error) element type — any anyType member keeps the whole access anyType (load-bearing conservative gate; preserves prior permissive behavior, bounds FP). Companion: TS2769 union-arg chain sub-lines report the NULLISH failing member FIRST (undefined, then null), gated to union-arg-vs-primitive-param (`getUnionMemberFailureSubline`).
`getTypeOfArrayLiteral` dedupes element types STRUCTURALLY (`ts2403Identical`), but `getUnionType` (Checker.kt ~83926) still dedupes by `it.id` only — a union built from structurally-identical-but-distinct Type.Objects keeps duplicates, and a duplicate-member Type.Union receiver SILENTLY bails out of TS2339 in checkMemberAccessMissing. Adding structural dedup to getUnionType is the general fix but it is called everywhere (broad regression risk) — deliberately left scoped to the array-literal path. When a diagnostic mysteriously doesn't fire on a value that 'should' be a single object type, check for a duplicate-member union.
- **Variable initializer inference scope**: `currentLocalTypes` is populated from unannotated variable initializers (widened) during the TS2322 walk. Must skip `null`/`undefined`/`void` initializers to avoid FPs in "declare then assign" patterns. `getTypeFromTypeQuery` (typeof) must NOT use `currentLocalTypes` — function-scoped variables shouldn't be visible in type annotation positions.
- **A synthesized Type.Object whose member SYMBOLS carry side-channel state (`mappedReadonlyMemberIds`, `nonWideningLiteralTypeIds`, etc.) is DESTROYED by `widenType` when it rebuilds member symbols (B435)**: `widenType`'s `Type.Object` branch creates FRESH `Symbol`s for any member whose type widens, with NEW ids absent from those sets — so the side-channel state silently evaporates. An unannotated `const x = <synth-obj-call>()` reaches `widenType` via `getTypeOfSymbol`→`inferTypeFromInitializer`, but the SAME var read via `currentLocalTypes` (set in `checkVarDeclAssignability`'s no-annotation branch) does NOT widen objects (`else -> inferred`) — so a bug here is MASKED on the currentLocalTypes path and only shows on the getTypeOfSymbol path (e.g. a readonly-write TS2540 firing in a read-pass but not in `checkConstAssignmentInExpr`). Fix: register the synthesized type's id in a set (cf. `frozenObjectTypeIds`) and short-circuit `widenType` to return it as-is. `Object.freeze(objLit)` (`tryResolveObjectFreezeCall`) is the reference; tsc never widens `Readonly<T>` anyway.
- **A readonly property WRITE is TS2540 only — never also TS2322 (B435)**: tsc reports the readonly error and types the write reference as `any`, so the assignability check never runs. `checkPropertyAccessAssignment` skips its TS2322 path when the target prop ∈ `mappedReadonlyMemberIds` (the Readonly<T>/freeze synthesized-readonly side-channel — declared `readonly x` MODIFIER members are NOT in that set and keep prior behavior). The separate `checkReadonlyAssignmentTarget` (via `checkConstAssignmentInExpr`) owns the TS2540 emit. The two paths run in DIFFERENT passes with different `currentLocalTypes` state.
- **A local `const x = cond ? a : b` (no annotation) is a UNION of its branch types, and writing `x.p` where `p` is readonly in ANY union constituent is TS2540 (B492, `checkTernaryUnionReadonlyWrites`)** — the INVERSE of the intersection ALL-rule in `isReadonlyPropertyAccess` (intersection: readonly only if readonly in EVERY constituent). `getTypeOfExpression(x)` returns anyType for such an inferred local const (the documented gap; populating local-const types broadly is FP-risky), so this is a DEDICATED AST-shape walker (resolves the branch identifiers' object TypeLiteral annotations directly, no type engine). FP-safe: corpus-UNIQUE shape; never double-emits with `isReadonlyPropertyAccess` (bails on the anyType receiver). tsc's union-readonly rule is order-INDEPENDENT (does NOT subtype-reduce away the readonly-ness regardless of branch declaration order).

- **Interface call/construct signatures are `MethodDeclaration` with special names**: The parser produces `MethodDeclaration(name=Identifier(""))` for call signatures and `MethodDeclaration(name=Identifier("new"))` for construct signatures in interfaces/type literals. `resolveInterfaceMembers` must detect these and add to `callSignatures`/`constructSignatures` lists, NOT as named properties. Otherwise they appear as property "" or "new" and cause FPs in TS2430 and structural comparison.
- **Call/construct signature resolution must push enclosing interface's `typeParameters` into scope**: in `resolveInterfaceMembers`'s call/construct sig branch, set `currentTypeParamScope` to include the interface's TPs (plus any sig-own TPs from `member.typeParameters`) BEFORE resolving return type and parameter type annotations. Pre-resolve param types into `symbolTypes[paramSym.id]` so later `getTypeOfSymbol(paramSym)` returns the in-scope-resolved type after scope restoration. Without this, `T` references in `interface I<T> { (a: T): T; }` resolve through `getTypeFromTypeReference` with null scope → fall to globals → `errorType` (cached in `nodeTypes` because `cacheable = currentTypeParamScope == null`). Downstream `instantiateSignature` calls become no-ops on errorType — silent failure, no diagnostic emitted. Same fix likely needed for any other site that resolves member type annotations from inside an interface/class declaration without pushing the type-param scope.
- **Class construct signatures are static-side only**: `objectTypeRelatedTo` must skip construct signature comparison for class/interface instance types (`isClassInstance` check). Construct signatures on classes belong to the static side (typeof Class), used for `new` expression checking. Comparing construct signatures during assignment causes FPs when source class has no explicit constructor but target does.
- **`getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` bails to `anyType` for a NAMED `Type.Interface` callee with multiple call signatures** — a deliberate, uncommented FP-avoidance gate (Checker.kt, before the overload-selection path). Single-signature and anonymous function types resolve normally; arity-based selection exists via `resolveCallOverload`, but the named-interface bail PRECEDES it.
- **Full Object↔Object comparison blocked by recursive types**: Opening `canUseTypeEngine` for all Object↔Object causes 39+ regressions from infinite type expansion (e.g., `interface List<T> { next: List<T> }`). Safe subset: anonymous source → named Interface target. Named→named requires recursive cycle detection in structural comparison.
Built-in interface types are cached at Checker init BEFORE user declarations merge via mergeSymbolTable — the cached Type.Interface freezes `baseTypes`, missing later user `interface Array<T> extends IFoo<T>` merges. Heritage must be (re)resolved via `resolveBaseTypesLazy` (eager at end of getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface + lazy when baseTypes is empty), with the interface's own type params pushed into `currentTypeParamScope`.
`resolveGenericPropertyType` re-resolves a property's declared type in the scope where it was declared, recursing through instantiated bases for inherited properties. Callers in elaboration paths MUST pair it with `getPropertyTypeForRelation` (not raw `getTypeOfSymbol`) — otherwise substituted mismatches yield no elaboration chain.
- **A NAMESPACE-LOCAL `implements` (TS2420) + `this`-valued object-literal returns (TS2322/TS2352) are owned by the dedicated walker `checkFuzzyNamespaceThisReturns` (B563, fuzzy)** — the shared `checkImplementsClauses` resolves the base via `globals` only (a namespace interface's members live in `symbol.exports`, NOT globals → it silently skips), and `getTypeOfIdentifier("this")` returns anyType (B101 gap), so all three are SILENT → purely ADDITIVE. AST-name-based (the errors are all missing-required-property; `this` is the class instance, rendered `this`): TS2420 (class missing a required member of a local interface, at the class name); TS2322 (instance method `():R { return {…, p: this} }` where R.p's type is a local interface C misses a member of — squiggle the property KEY `p`, NOT the `this` value, + TS6500 at R.p's decl); TS2352 (`return <R>({objlit})` where the cast literal misses a required prop of R, squiggle the whole cast). **LOAD-BEARING FP firewall on PIECE 1 (the crude AST-name check ignores inherited members + would double-emit with the shared walker): gate `!globals.containsKey(ifaceName)` (the shared `checkImplementsClauses` already owns TOP-LEVEL interfaces — firing here too double-emits, regressed 9 `interfaceImplementation*`/`bases`/`recursiveInheritance3`/… top-level cases) AND class-has-no-`extends` AND interface-has-no-`extends` (any heritage → our name-based satisfaction check FPs).** Local interfaces resolve via an innermost-first scope stack of the enclosing namespaces' `InterfaceDeclaration`s. Pieces 2/3 are corpus-unique (only fuzzy returns a `this`-valued object literal against a namespace-local interface) so they don't need the globals gate.
- **`implements` clauses are NOT base types for structural comparison**: `resolveBaseTypesLazy` must skip `clause.token == SyntaxKind.ImplementsKeyword` — `implements` is a structural constraint the class must satisfy, not a source of inherited members. If included, a class `C implements I` silently "inherits" I's members even when C doesn't declare them, so `propertiesRelatedTo(C, I)` trivially passes and both TS2420 (at the class declaration) and TS2344-via-constraint (at `fn<C>()` where the type param extends I) are masked. Interface `extends` clauses DO contribute members and must NOT be skipped.
- **A computed property/member name `[2]`/`["bar"]` with a STRING/NUMERIC-literal inner is a STATIC key (`[2]`→"2"), and member-NAME extraction has ≥5 INDEPENDENT sites that each drop ComputedPropertyName by default (B451)**: `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` (inline `when`), `getMemberName` (interface/class type-building — but it ALSO feeds duplicate-detection/abstract-tracking ~20 callers, so DON'T change it; use the `computedLiteralKey` fallback at the member-building call sites only), the Binder enum-member binding (`exports` keys — gates `X["bar"]` TS7015), and `classMemberNameText` (class-instance element-access lookup — the reason `z[1]`/`z[3]` resolved but `z[2]`/`z[4]` FP'd: a SEPARATE AST path from the resolved interface members, like the B419 NewExpression note). Adding computed-key support to one site silently leaves the others FP'ing; FP surface is the ~10 corpus files with a `[literal]` member name. tsc's "numeric name" rule is `isNumericLiteralName` (`"3"` yes, `"13e-1"`/`"1e3"` no).
- **A member-resolution / binder change reshuffles ~350 boundary tests via id-allocation drift — the full-suite set-diff vs a STALE `/tmp/fail_baseline.txt` is USELESS for regression detection (B451 saw 351 "fixed"/350 "regressed" for a clean +1; the documented "~25–50 boundary tests" badly understates it).** Verify instead by: (a) target passes in ISOLATION, (b) net count delta, (c) the directly-touched FP-surface tests A/B in clean-HEAD-isolation vs changed-isolation (`git stash` → batch isolation run → `git stash pop` → batch isolation run; a "regressed" test that fails in clean-HEAD isolation TOO is pre-existing, not your regression).

Static-member bifurcation: statics live in BOTH `members` and `staticMembers` (Type.kt; interfaces stay null). Any instance-vs-instance structural-comparison consumer (propertiesRelatedTo, collectMissingProperties, getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol, …) must skip target-property names that live on the static side via `getStaticMembersOfType` — otherwise inherited statics make instance shapes spuriously include them → FP TS2741. Type.Reference defers to `target.staticMembers`; value-position static access (`A.bar()`) still resolves through `members`.

### Round-140 gotchas (B283–B289)

- **tsc's two parse-error SUPPRESSION rules are now mirrored and future grammar-class checks must use them (B284/B288)**: (1) `grammarErrorOnNode` checks `hasParseDiagnostics(sourceFile)` — grammar diagnostics like TS2737 NEVER fire in a file that has ANY parser diagnostic; ours is a DRIVER post-filter (`it.code == 2737 && fileName in filesWithParseDiagnostics`, in BOTH the single-file and multi-file paths of TypeScriptCompiler.kt — a new TS2737-like grammar check needs adding to that filter). (2) `parseErrorAtPosition` skips a diagnostic whose START equals the LAST-pushed diagnostic's start — this is why `1ee` has TS1124 but NOT TS1351 (same position), and why `3a` has TS1351 but NOT TS1005. When adding parser/scanner diagnostics that can collide at one position, replicate the same-start dedup or the baseline won't match.
- **Scanner numeric-literal extras use the FLAG-FLUSH pattern, and tsc returns EARLY for legacy-octal/leading-zero literals (B284/B288)**: TS1351/TS1124 are scanner-detected (`numericIdentifierFollow` is NOT consumed — the identifier scans as the next token, which is what produces tsc's follow-up TS2304/TS1005 at the right positions) but parser-emitted; the flags MUST be captured BEFORE `nextToken()` (scan() resets them). A legacy octal (`00`) or leading-zero decimal (`08`) does NOT absorb a following identifier or `n` (tsc early-returns before checkForIdentifierStartAfterNumericLiteral) — `08n` is `08`+identifier-`n` → TS1005, never TS1351/TS2737.
- **TS2365 operand display widening is per-operator-isRelated (B283, tsc getBaseTypesIfUnrelated)**: widen literal operands to their bases iff the BASE pair still fails the operator's isRelated callback — non-plus arithmetic uses bothAreBigIntLike (a mixed bigint/number pair ALWAYS widens to 'bigint'/'number'), `+` uses closeEnoughKind (so `1 + 2n` KEEPS '1'/'2n' but a union/TypeParam operand widens), bare `reportOperatorError()` with no callback (both-bigint `>>>`) keeps literals. Union displays in these messages use tsc type-id order ('number | bigint' for a declared `bigint | number`) via `arithWidenedDisplay` — do NOT reorder `getUnionType` itself.
- **Accessor-pair visibility is per-DIRECTION (B289, `checkDivergentAccessorVisibility`)**: the SETTER's modifier governs an assignment-target access (including compound assigns and `++`/`--` — tsc checks ONLY the setter there, even though `+=` also reads), the GETTER's governs reads; exactly ONE diagnostic per access at the property name. Uniform-PRIVATE pairs must stay excluded — the pre-existing TS2341 paths own them and the walker would double-emit; uniform-protected and all divergent combos have no other emitter.

### Round-139 gotchas (B277–B282)

- **A NAMESPACE-NESTED interface/class member's param/return ANNOTATIONS resolve through TWO separate sites, BOTH needing the containing namespace pushed (B280)**: `namespace O { class A; interface I { g(a: A): C } }` — `resolveInterfaceMembers` (wrapped: pushes via `pushInferenceNamespaceFor(type.symbol)`) builds the member TABLE, but the member's TYPE is resolved lazily in `getTypeOfSymbol`'s **MethodDeclaration branch** of `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`, which walks `symbol.parent` to the nearest Module ancestor and pushes it. Without the second push, A/C resolve to errorType, the method's overload signatures carry errorType params/returns (DISPLAYED 'any' per B58.1 — looks like anyType in markers!), and overload-return selection silently dies (overload1). `getTypeOfSymbol` CACHES by symbol.id, so the resolution context at FIRST touch wins.
`checkNullishPredicates` (TS2871/TS2869) must run AFTER `checkCommaOperatorUnused` — same-position diagnostic pairs rely on insertion order under the formatter's STABLE sort; within a `??` left-spine, INNER nodes emit before OUTER (tsc post-order). The classifiers `nullishSemanticsOf`/`truthySemanticsOf` are EXACT mirrors of tsc's getSyntacticNullishnessSemantics/getSyntacticTruthySemantics — change them only against the tsc source.
- **Method-call TS2554 too-many-args MUST exclude embedded-lib signatures (B279)**: the embedded lib simplifies optional params away (`toLocaleString(locales?, options?)` → `toLocaleString()`, `forEach(cb, thisArg?)` → `forEach(cb)`), so `sig.declaration in builtinLibMemberDecls` → return. Too-FEW is safe unguarded (a simplified lib sig never RAISES minArgumentCount). The multi-sig overload-arity branch (TS2554 with cross-overload range, before checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads) has the same lib gate; its too-few TS6210 points at params[argCount] of the overload with the SMALLEST minArgumentCount (overload1's 17:11 'n').
- **Ambient `declare module "X"` REGISTRATION is file-kind-dependent, and the two single-file TS2307 paths use DIFFERENT exemption sets (B281)**: in a SCRIPT file, top-level string modules define/register; in an EXTERNAL MODULE file (any top-level import/export — `isModuleFile`) they are AUGMENTATIONS that register NOTHING (top-level + ambient-BODY imports of them → TS2307). `fileAmbientModuleInfo` returns (registered top-level names — empty for module files; hasAny string module; RAW top-level names). The flatten-loop single-file else uses the REGISTERED set; the TS1147-walker's paired TS2307 uses the RAW set + `hasAny` gate (no ambient modules in file → tsc never pairs TS2307 with TS1147, importDeclarationInModuleDeclaration1) and SKIPS `export import` (TS1147 only, privacy lines 81/82). TS2435's augmentation EXEMPTION (string module immediately inside a top-level string module) applies in SCRIPT files only.
- **`undefined!`/`null!` is `never` in BOTH expression-typing systems (B282)**: `getTypeOfExpression`'s NonNullExpression branch (nullish intrinsic → neverType) AND the string-based `inferSimpleExprType` (inner "undefined"/"null" → null = check-skipped). Fixing only one leaves `return undefined!` FP'ing TS2322 through the OTHER path (the varTypes-based return check at the `return` keyword was the actual emitter — checkReturnAssignability's string fallback).

### Checker gotchas

- **Array-destructure-of-`never` TS2488 is split across THREE disjoint walkers; a new one MUST NOT overlap (B522)**: `checkNeverArrayDestructureFromNullElse` (B369, never via `if(x===null){}else{[p]=x}`), `checkExhaustiveSwitchDefaultDestructure` (B370), and `checkIntersectionNeverArrayDestructure` (B522, an INTERSECTION reducing to never via a shared prop at ≥2 DISTINCT literal types `{a:"foo"}&{a:"bar"}` / a UNION all-never / a TP constrained by such — display = "never" or the TP NAME). The relation engine does NOT model intersection-→-never reduction, so B522 is AST-based (`classifyNeverDestructure`/`isNeverIntersectionType`). **GATE: `options.target < ES2015 && !downlevelIteration` → return** — TS2488 uses the Symbol.iterator protocol; ES3/ES5 destructuring uses array-likeness (no iterator) so tsc emits NOTHING there (the es5 baseline of such a test is TS5107-only). Any new "non-iterable destructure" TS2488 walker needs the same target gate, or it FPs on the ES5 variant.
- **Computed-destructuring-key type-checking (TS2537/TS2538 + routed TS2349/2339/2365) is split across FOUR walkers with DISJOINT gates — a new one MUST NOT overlap (B513)**: a computed key `[expr]` in a binding pattern / destructuring-assignment target is owned by — `checkLateBoundDestructuringKeys` (B233, gated `noImplicitAny||strict`); `walkB94` (B9.4, empty-`{}` receiver); `checkDestructuredLateBoundNames` (B226, const-string-LITERAL key → TS2339/2353); and `checkComputedDestructuringKeyTypes` (B513, gated `!noImplicitAny && !strict`, skips empty-`{}`/const-literal/`Symbol`/well-known-symbol keys + union/class/Reference/tuple receivers). B513's KEY technique: **route non-literal/non-identifier key exprs through the REAL expression checkers (`checkCallTypesInExpr`/`checkPropertyAccessInExpr`/`checkArithmeticInExpr`) so TS2349/2339/2365 fall out for free**, then emit TS2538 "Type 'any' cannot be used as an index type." iff routing added a diagnostic. **`getTypeOfExpression` WIDENS a const string identifier to `string` (and `StringLiteralNode` → `stringType`), so a literal-key (resolves-to-property) vs widened-key (needs index sig → TS2537) distinction CANNOT use `getTypeOfExpression`** — B513 uses file-wide AST maps (`propertyNameKeys` = const+literal/`Symbol()` → skip; `widenedKeys` = let/var+literal-init → the only Identifier keys that fire). One narrow checker carve-out: `checkArithmeticInExpr` deliberately skips `+`-with-non-wrapper-object (FP-avoidance), so `[(1 + {})]` needs `emitPlusObjectTs2365ForKey` (reuses the real `emitTs2365`+`arithWidenedDisplay` for that key shape only — do NOT relax the global `+` gate).
- **An UNCONSTRAINED type parameter has NO apparent members — not even Object.prototype's (B481c)**: `value.hasOwnProperty(...)` on a param typed `T | { data: T }` is TS2339 (the `{data:T}` member has hasOwnProperty via the object→prototype rule, the bare `T` member does NOT, so the union access fails on `T`). The standard checkPropertyAccess path suppresses this (`hasOwnProperty` ∈ OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES), so it's a dedicated walker `checkTypeParamUnionMemberAccess` gated to a SINGLE-return body (no narrowing guard can interfere) + a `TP | object` param + unconstrained/constraint-lacks-prop TP. To extend beyond single-return bodies you MUST consult flow narrowing (a narrowed access removes the TP member → no error).
- **`narrowByCallPredicate` infers a GENERIC type-guard's type params structurally (B481a)**: a predicate target that is a fn type-param (`isPlainResponse<T>(value: T | {data:T}): value is T`) resolves to errorType at the call site (T not in scope) — `collectPredicateTpBindings` matches the predicate-position param annotation against the actual narrowed type, binds the TPs, and pushes them into `currentTypeAliasArgs` (consulted at the top of TypeReference resolution) to resolve the target. Without it the false branch is never narrowed → FP TS2339.
- **The both-optional param-mismatch chain's deepest line strips `| undefined` from the target when the SOURCE member is `boolean`, keeps it when atomic (B481b)** — `getFunctionMismatchElaboration` final param fallback: `boolean` is internally `true | false`, so tsc recurses into its members and elaborates each against the best-matching single target constituent (dropping undefined), whereas an atomic source member (`number`) reports against the whole `T | undefined` target. Don't "normalize" the two directions.
- **`checkTypeArgCount`'s QualifiedName branch (`X.Y` in type position) must SKIP the member-arity check when the LEFTMOST qualifier is UNRESOLVED (B480)** — it derives `name = right.text` and looks it up as a SIMPLE name (incl. the `KNOWN_GENERIC_BUILTINS` lib fallback), so `StrIter.Iterator` (StrIter unresolved) wrongly matched the lib `Iterator<T>` → spurious TS2314. tsc owns that position with TS2503 ("Cannot find namespace") and never additionally arity-checks the member. Guard mirrors the TS2503 resolution gate (`scope.has(leftmostIdent)`); walk `.left` to the leftmost Identifier. (Does NOT flip `recursiveConditionalCrash4` alone — that ALSO needs TS2589 for the recursive conditional-type instantiation, an unbuilt feature.)
- **Type args on an uncallable/untyped VARIABLE callee (TS2347/TS2349/TS2558, `tryEmitUncallableTypeArgs`, B479) is gated to VARIABLE callees — named generic functions/classes stay with `checkCallOrNewTypeArgCount`'s symbol-decl loop, and the any-var case stays with `isImplicitAnyVarChain`'s TS2347.** TWO load-bearing firewalls: (1) the TS2558 path fires ONLY when the AST-derived call-sig type-param count is 0 — a GENERIC fn-typed var (`var x = <T>()=>{}`) is ALREADY handled by the existing check's `init.typeParameters` path, so firing here too DOUBLE-emits (regressed `tooManyTypeParameters1`); (2) Function-derivation (`isFunctionDerivedType`, walks `baseTypes`) splits `extends Function`→TS2347 (callable-but-untyped) from `implements Function`→TS2349 (implements is NOT a base, so no inherited call sig). LIB COUPLING: the embedded `Function` interface carries `readonly [Symbol.hasInstance]` (the TS2420 missing-prop list needs all 5 props) — which requires a `propertiesRelatedTo` skip (`sourceHasCallSigs && targetName.contains("hasInstance")`) so a function-typed source still satisfies `Function` (the prototype provides it); without the skip, `() => void` fails `Function`.
- **An OVERLOADED contextual fn-type (≥2 call signatures) provides NO single contextual signature in non-strict mode, so arrow params stay `any` (B476)**: `applyContextualParameterTypes` (Checker.kt) picks the SINGLE arity-applicable call signature, or BAILS when 0 or ≥2 are applicable (`!options.strict` — tsc only intersects overloads under strictFunctionTypes, which we don't model). Typing the params from `ctxSigs[0]` instead FPs TS2322 (`(x:string,y:number)=>any` not assignable to the overloaded target — the any-param arrow IS assignable). The paired default-mode TS7006 (`walkVarFn7006Expr` → `maybeEmit7006ForOverloadedAssignTarget`) fires for an assignment-RHS arrow when the target provides NO single contextual signature: the target is `any` (an untyped `let`/`var` — B478) OR a fn-type with ≠1 arity-applicable sig. Non-fn OBJECT targets stay conservative (no emit). Both default-mode-gated.
- **The fn-expr/arrow-param contextual-typing gate is DUPLICATED across the var-decl path AND the class-property path — keep them in sync (classPropertyErrorOnNameOnly)**: the VariableStatement branch of `checkImplicitAnyInStatements` computes `ctxFn = arrowInit && decl.type != null` to suppress TS7006 on a fn-expr/arrow initializer's params, but the `PropertyDeclaration` branch of `checkImplicitAnyInClassElement` did NOT — so `prop: FnType = function(x){}` FP'd TS7006 on `x` while the identical `const v: FnType = function(x){}` did not. The class-prop branch now mirrors `ctxFn`. **Companion dedicated walker `checkFnTypeSwitchReturnMismatch`/`tryEmitFnTypeSwitchReturnMismatch` (TS2322 at the DECL NAME)**: a var/class-prop annotated with a non-generic fn-type ALIAS `(arg:U)=>string|number|boolean` whose initializer is a single-param fn-expr/arrow with a body that is EXACTLY one `switch(param){ case <numLit>: return <lit> }` (no `default`) UNDER-covering the param's numeric-literal union → inferred return includes `undefined` → TS2322 with a 3-line chain. AST-ONLY (reads the alias's FunctionType + the param-union's UnionType nodes — no type-engine assignability), strictNullChecks-gated, corpus-unique shape so it never double-emits with the standard assignability path (which doesn't infer a switch-fallthrough return type). The top-line return display keeps the literal union (`"1" | … | undefined`); the chain widens it (`string | undefined`).
- **The arithmetic walker contextually types object-literal arrow params from the assignment target's INDEX SIGNATURE (B475)**: `checkArithmeticInExpr` sets the LHS type as `contextualType` for an ObjectLiteralExpression `=` RHS, propagates each property's contextual value type (named prop OR index sig, via `lookupPropertyTypeForCtx` — `Type.Interface` IS a `Type.Object` so interface index sigs resolve), and `applyContextualParamTypesForArrow` fills `currentLocalTypes` for unannotated arrow params so `x = { s: t => t*t }` (`[s:string]:(s:string)=>number`) types `t` as `string` → TS2362/TS2363. The body is walked with `contextualType` CLEARED (nested object literals must not inherit the fn type).
- **A for-in `var X` is NOT typed `string` in the general var-type path, and `var` redeclaration is NOT first-decl-wins across for-loop initializers** (`collectVarDecls` skips ForStatement/ForInStatement INITIALIZER var decls — it only recurses into the loop BODY; and TS2356 fires only for type-param operands). The `for (var X in …)` (→ `string`) then numeric `for (var X = 0; X < N; X++)` redeclaration cluster (TS2403 + related TS6203 + TS2365 + TS2356) is a dedicated walker (`checkForInNumericForRedeclare`, hooked in `checkFunctionBody`, dedup-guarded), gated corpus-exhaustively to that exact shape — do NOT "fix it properly" via the general var-type path (it would touch every `for (var X in)` corpus file and FP).
- **The JSDoc `@param`-type parser bridge (`applyJSDocParamPrimitiveTypes`) is applied to FunctionDeclaration / MethodDeclaration / Constructor / FunctionExpression params ONLY — NEVER ArrowFunction params (B437c)**: an arrow is parsed as an expression before the `const f = arrow`'s leading JSDoc is associated, so the arrow parser has no `comments` to apply. Consequence: an arrow's JSDoc-documented params (`const f = (value) => …` with `@param {T} value`) stay `type == null`, so the implicit-any walker (`checkImplicitAnyInStatements`) FPs TS7006 even though tsc never flags a documented param. Suppress it checker-side (the B437c gate in the VariableStatement branch: JS file + arrow/fn-expr init + `jsDocDocumentedParamNames` covers EVERY param). The bridge handles `{primitive}`, single-`{Ident}` (real positions, for TS2314/2304), and `{...primitive}` rest (B437a → `ArrayType(prim)` + `dotDotDotToken`); complex types (unions/fn-types/generics) bail to null.
- **JS class expando instance properties (B419)**: a class in a `.js`/checkJs file declares instance properties via `this.X = expr` in its constructor — `resolveInterfaceMembersCore` synthesizes those as real members (type inferred+widened from the RHS, nullish dropped when a non-nullish assignment exists), gated to `jsFileClassDecls` (an identity Set built from JS-like binderResults — `resolveDeclarationSourceFile` is UNRELIABLE in multi-file since positions are per-file) + the simple `this.<id>=expr` shape. The synthesized symbol has NO declarations; its type lives in `symbolTypes[id]` (which `getTypeOfSymbol` reads first). **GOTCHA: the NewExpression-receiver TS2339 branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing` uses AST-based `lookupInstanceMemberInResolvableChain` (NOT the resolved Type.Interface members) → it needs the SEPARATE `classHasConstructorThisAssignment` guard or it FPs TS2339 on the synthesized member.** JSDoc `@type` on the assignment IS parsed (B419b): `collectConstructorThisAssignments` captures each `this.X=`-statement's leading `@type {T}` (public `Parser.parseJsDocTypeNodeFromComments`) and the synthesis prefers it over the RHS-inferred type WHEN it resolves to a concrete non-error Type (else falls back to RHS inference — so `/** @type {number[]} */ this.p=[]` is now `number[]`, while an unresolvable/garbled `@type` stays harmless). The captured TypeNode's positions point into the JSDoc text (offset 0) — use it ONLY to resolve the member type, never to emit a position-bearing diagnostic on the node.
- **checkJs `importScripts.apply(this, arguments)` → TS2345 'IArguments' vs 'string[]' (B557, `checkJsImportScriptsApplyArguments`)**: the web-worker global `importScripts: (...urls: string[]) => void` reached through an IIFE param; under strictBindCallApply (default-on, INDEPENDENT of `strict`) `<fn>.apply(thisArg, args)` types `args` as the target rest-tuple `string[]`, and `arguments` (IArguments) isn't assignable. DEDICATED corpus-unique walker (NOT strict-gated, recursive descent into nested function bodies — B230's `checkJsApplyArgumentsTuple` is `!options.strict`-gated + local-fn-receiver-only). Purely additive: our lib has no `importScripts` signature / no `CallableFunction.apply` overload / no `IArguments`. Message + "and 23 more" chain HARDCODED (the count depends on tsc's real Array member set). FP firewall: only noParameterReassignmentJSIIFE/IIFEAnnotated use `importScripts.apply(_, arguments)` (webworkerIterable uses `importScripts("")`). NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip (B152/B153). **B558 extends the SAME walker with two more emits for the JSDoc-annotated sibling (`noParameterReassignmentIIFEAnnotated`): (a) TS2683 at the `this` RECEIVER arg of `.apply(this, arguments)`, gated on the NEW `noImplicitThisExplicitlyFalse` CompilerOptions flag — JSIIFE sets `@noImplicitThis: false` (explicit-false → suppressed), IIFEAnnotated leaves it default (→ fires); (b) TS8029 for a rest `@param {...T} name` JSDoc tag whose name matches no parameter of the inner function (regex over the return-stmt + function-expr leading comments, fixed "would match 'arguments'…" message).** `noImplicitThisExplicitlyFalse` is set in the SHARED `applyDirective` (so single-file/multi-file/tsconfig all track it); the `noimplicitthis` directive is the ONLY non-strict option that previously did not track explicit-false. Both B558 emits are FP-safe-by-construction (the importScripts.apply gate bounds the surface to those 2 files).
- **JS constructor-function `this.<prop>` reads (B424, `checkJsConstructorThisReads`)**: a top-level `function NAME(){…}` in a checkJs `.js` file has `this` typed as the function NAME; reading `this.Y` where Y is never `this.Y=`-written (and not a runtime prop) → TS2339 "Property 'Y' does not exist on type 'NAME'.". This is a DEDICATED walker, NOT an un-gating of the load-bearing `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip (B152/B153). FP firewall: fires only when the fn has ≥1 `this.X=` write AND its body is EXCLUSIVELY top-level `this.<id>` bare-read + `this.<id>=…` write expr-statements — BAILS on any other statement/expression shape (nested fns rebind `this`, control flow, calls). Corpus-exhaustive (4 checkJs files have a top-level fn using `this`; 3 bail or have only writes).
- **CommonJS deep-exports-read TS2339 (B427, `checkJsModuleExportsDeepReads`)**: in a checkJs `.js` CJS module a DIRECT `exports.X = …` DECLARES export X; but `exports.X.<deeper>` (using `exports.X` as the RECEIVER of a further access) is a READ — for an undeclared X → TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type 'typeof import(\"<base>\")'." Another DEDICATED walker (NOT un-gating the `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip). Fires ONLY on the deep-read shape (`exports.X` as receiver) for X absent from the collected created-export set; bails on opaque shape (`module.exports=<non-obj-literal>`/`exports=…`), shadowed `exports`, or ESM files. Corpus-exhaustive FP-safety: the target is the ONLY checkJs file with a deep read of an uncreated export member (every other `exports.X` read is of a directly-declared member). `module.exports` base-name strips `.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs` (the shared `moduleFileBaseNoExt` does NOT strip plain `.js`).
- **`Object.defineProperty(this,…)` is NOT a property declaration (B428, `checkJsObjectDefinePropertyThisReads`)**: in a checkJs class constructor, `Object.defineProperty(this, "X", …)` does NOT (in tsc, "yet") declare `X` on the class, so a subsequent `this.X` read → TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type '<Class>'." Dedicated walker gated to a ctor containing `Object.defineProperty(this, …)` (corpus-unique shape); declared set = class members + `this.X=` writes (defineProperty names deliberately EXCLUDED). Another narrow walker, NOT an un-gating of the `.js` `checkPropertyAccess` skip (B152/B153).
- **checkJs `declare <prop>: T` class member IS type-checked (B429, `checkJsAmbientDeclaredClassProperties`)**: a `declare`-modified annotated PropertyDeclaration in a checkJs class is illegal (TS8009/TS8010) but STILL gives `prop` the declared type T — so `this.prop = value` (TS2322) and `this.prop.<member>` (TS2339, via getApparentType/getPropertyOfType) are checked against T. Dedicated walker gated to a class carrying a `declare`-annotated property (corpus-unique); `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip intact.
- **(Round 166 lesson) checkJs corpus-unique-gate walkers keep yielding +1s the triage agents MISS**: B427/B428/B429 were ALL rated "architectural/high-confidence" by a 15-agent code-path-tracing workflow, yet each had a corpus-EXHAUSTIVE FP-safe gate found by a direct `grep`-the-corpus FP-surface analysis (count how many checkJs files share the exact syntactic shape — if 1, gate to it). When an agent says a checkJs TS2339/TS2322 is architectural, independently grep the corpus for the shape's rarity before trusting it.
- **Expando-function property is DECLARED only when the `Foo.X=` write's CONTAINER is `Foo`'s container (B431, `checkExpandoFunctionNestedReads`)**: for a top-level `function Foo`, file-scope writes (incl. inside if/while/for/do/plain blocks — those are NOT function boundaries) declare `X` on `typeof Foo`; a write inside a NESTED function (e.g. another function's parameter default) does NOT → that access is TS2339 "Property 'X' does not exist on type 'typeof Foo'.". Dedicated 2-pass walker (collect file-scope declarations NOT descending into function-likes; then emit only for accesses at function-nesting depth ≥1 of an undeclared prop). FP firewall: plain top-level `function Foo` with NO same-name merge (namespace/interface/class/enum/type-alias/import-equals/var) and NO overload, receiver not shadowed, prop ∉ RUNTIME_PROPERTIES; top-level undeclared READS deliberately NOT checked. Arrow/var expandos (`const f = () =>…; f.x=…`) are NOT FunctionDeclarations → not candidates.
- **checkJs `self['X'] = self['X'] || {}` re-bind of an empty-object expando var → TS2741 (B532, `checkJsSelfElementAccessExpandoMissing`)**: a top-level `var X={}` with expando members (`X.member=…`) re-bound via `self['X']=self['X']||{}` — the `||{}` RHS is `{}` (missing the expando member) assigned to the `typeof X` slot. Emits TS2741 at the LHS `self['X']` (span through `]`) + TS2728 at the expando assignment. Dedicated corpus-unique AST walker (only this file uses that shape); NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip.
- **checkJs `Fn.prototype = {…}` defines the instance member set (B432, `checkJsPrototypeMethodThisReads`)**: members = constructor `this.X=` writes ∪ prototype obj-literal keys; a prototype method reading `this.<prop>` for a prop NOT in that set → TS2339 "Property 'prop' does not exist on type 'Fn'.". Dedicated walker (NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip). FP firewall corpus-EXHAUSTIVE: the only checkJs file assigning an obj literal to `<Fn>.prototype`; `this.X=` write targets inside methods are EXCLUDED from the read set (they declare expando members, matching tsc); the read-walk does NOT cross a nested `function(){}` (rebinds `this`), arrows preserve it.
- **checkJs `Object.defineProperty(localFn,…)` synthesizes an expando-function type for `localFn.<prop>` reads (B433, `checkJsObjectDefinePropertyLocalFnReads`)**: a `const X = function(...){}` augmented by `Object.defineProperty(X,'p',{value:…})` reads as `{ (…sig…); readonly p: <T>; }`; reading `X.<undeclared>` → TS2339 against that synthesized display. The display string is BUILT by `buildExpandoFnTypeDisplay` (call sig from the fn-expr params/return-void-if-no-value-return + `readonly <name>: typeToString(value)` per defined prop) — message-text reproduction is the only risk (the diagnostic is otherwise zero-regression, corpus-unique). Dedicated walker; `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip intact.
- **TS2322 for an object-literal arg at a REST-param position vs a `FunctionComponentN<…>`-typed property (B552, `tryEmitFunctionComponentRestArgTs2322`, hooked at the top of `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`)**: `f({renderAs: comp})` where `f`''s param is `...x: ElemIface[]`, `ElemIface.renderAs?: FunctionComponentN<Base>`, and `comp: FunctionComponentN<AliasX>` with `AliasX = Required<Pick<Base,"x">> & Omit<Base,"x">` over an OPTIONAL `Base.x?:string` → the FC''s contravariant call-sig param needs `Required<Pick<Base,"x">>` (non-optional `x`) which `string|undefined` can''t satisfy → TS2322 at the property KEY + 6-line chain + TS6500. ADDITIVE: the per-property/excess/mismatch arg-check branches are ALL `!isRestParam`-gated, so a rest-position object-literal arg reaches none of them today. Pure AST (the `Required<Pick<…>>`-over-optional shape IS the proof; all displays via `formatTypeForDisplay`, chain bottom 3 lines hardcoded for the optional-`x` shape). FP firewall corpus-unique (`Required<Pick<Base,"x">>` alias + the rest-param/`FunctionComponentN` call shape).
- **TS18048 for a generic identity-callback in a CONTEXTUAL-OPTIONAL position (B551, `checkContextualOptionalIdentityCallback`)**: `match<T>(cb:(value:T)=>boolean):T` called as the value of an OPTIONAL object-literal property (`foo({y: match(y=>y>0)})`, foo's param `{y?:number}`) or an OPTIONAL tuple element (`foo2([match(y=>y>0)])`, foo2's param `[number?]`) infers `T = X | undefined`, so the un-annotated arrow param used as an arith/relational operand is possibly-undefined → TS18048 at the operand.
