Dedicated AST-shape walker (we have NO contextual-RETURN generic inference — the mapper only flows T FROM the callback body, never from the contextual-return position — so the param resolves to `any` and the general path emits nothing → purely additive). Gated `(strict||strictNullChecks) && !exactOptionalPropertyTypes` (the eopt=true variant has zero errors). FP firewall corpus-unique by construction: the `<T>(cb:(value:T)=>boolean):T` identity shape + contextual-optional position + an un-annotated arrow whose body is one arith/relational binary using the param as an operand IS the proof — no contextual type need be materialized. **GOTCHA: the parser CONSUMES a tuple element `?` (`[number?]`) without recording it — there is NO OptionalType node for a tuple element** (`parseTupleType` calls `parseOptional(Question)` and discards it). Detect tuple-element optionality from SOURCE text (`tupleHasOptionalElement`: a `?` at the tuple's top bracket level, outside nested `{}`/`()`/`<>`).
- **checkJs `const x = require("./y.json"|"./y.js")` module-shape member-access TS2339 + JSDoc-`@type` TS2741 (B550, `checkRequireOfJsonOrCjsModuleInJs`)**: a checkJs `.js` `const x = require(spec)` where spec is a resolvable JSON object literal OR a JS file whose SOLE export is one `module.exports = {objLit}` gives `x` that closed object shape; `x.<undeclared>` → TS2339, and a `/** @type {{...}} */`-annotated such var requiring a prop MISSING from the module shape → TS2741 at the var NAME + TS2728 at the prop INSIDE the JSDoc comment (position = `comment.pos + indexOf(\bprop\b after the @type '{')`, since `parseJsDocTypeNodeFromComments` returns positions relative to the brace substring, not the source). Dedicated walker, NOT an un-gating of the `.js` checkPropertyAccess skip (B152/B153) — a `require()`-var resolves to `any` (the `require` global is `any`) so the general path emits nothing → purely additive. FP firewall (corpus-unique): the module shape display is built MANUALLY from property value-KINDS (`{ a: number; }`, via `requireObjLitShape`/`requireJsonValueDisplay`) — do NOT route the JSON case through `inferTypeFromInitializer` on a sub-parsed objLit (the sub-parse positions could collide with the real file's nodeTypes cache); any non-`key:<simple-literal>` prop, any `exports.X=` form, multiple `module.exports`, or a non-object shape bails the var. A var WITH a `@type` annotation is typed by the annotation (NOT member-access-checked); only un-annotated require-vars get TS2339.
- **An `enum W` MERGED with a same-name `namespace W` makes the value-position `W` a `typeof W` object — var-decl assignability against `number`/`W`/`typeof W`/`typeof W.member`/a structural interface is a DEDICATED hardcoded table (B554, `tryEmitEnumNamespaceMergeVarDecl`), NOT the relation engine.** Hooked EARLY in `checkVarDeclAssignability` and SUPPRESSES the general path on a hit (the general path FP's `var x: WStatic = W` and SWAP-displays `W` instead of `typeof W`, so the walker must own ALL var-decls of this shape, not just additively). Classifies SOURCE from the init AST (`W`→typeof W `{a,b,c,D}` / `W.a`→enum-member type, display `W` / numeric literal) and TARGET from the annotation (`number` / `W`=enum / `typeof W` / `typeof W.a` / a `TypeReference` to an interface whose members are ALL typed as the merged enum). tsc display rule: a literal source widens to `number` for OBJECT targets (`typeof W`, `WStatic`) but keeps the literal for enum/`typeof W.a` targets. Per-file context cached lazily (`enumNsMergeContext`); member values via `collectEnumsForAsg`/`EnumAsgInfo`. FP firewall corpus-unique: fires only when the file has the enum+namespace merge AND the var-decl's (source,target) references the merged name — the ~14 other enum+namespace merges lack the `typeof W`-annotation + structural-interface combo (enumAssignmentCompat/enumAssignmentCompat2, identical shape, decl order swapped → one walker flips both).
- **Enum-to-enum VARIABLE assignability (TS2322, B425, `checkEnumToEnumAssignments`) is NOMINAL via a dedicated walker — NOT the relation engine** (enum types don't flow through it for this). Rule (derived from `enumAssignmentCompat3`): two enums relate iff they share a SIMPLE name AND every source member is present in the target with an EQUAL value; a const enum relates only to itself; the chain reports the FIRST failing source member ("Property 'X' is missing in type 'TGT'." vs "Each declaration of 'E.X' differs in its value, where 'V_tgt' was expected but 'V_src' was given."). Type names are qualified `Namespace.E` ONLY when src and tgt share a simple name (relative-name disambiguation; `Nope` vs `E` → simple). DISJOINT from `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` (literal RHS) + `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments` (union annotations). String/un-evaluable enums → `values=null` → conservatively assignable (FN, no FP). Remaining enum tests need broad features (typeof-Enum value-position object typing; string-enums+function-params+nested-scope shadowing+`import("f")` cross-file display; enum-relation wired into TS2416/TS2394).
- **Object-literal SPREAD support (B426)**: `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` now merges a spread's GUARANTEED props (via `spreadGuaranteedProps`: single object → required props; UNION → props required in EVERY NON-NULLISH constituent — spreading undefined/null is a no-op so nullish members are filtered, which is why `{id, ...(find()??{label})}` no longer FP's "label missing" WITHOUT any `??`-type change). Merge is ADDITIVE (only adds props not already explicit). TS2783/TS2785 (explicit prop overwritten by a later guaranteeing spread) lives in the separate full-tree walker `checkSpreadPropertyOverrides` — FP firewall: the spread must GUARANTEE the prop (`{y}|{y,x}` and optional `{a,b?}` do NOT fire); squiggle = whole property assignment, related at the spread `...` (`expression.pos - 3`).
- **Ternary-condition TS2872/TS2873 lives in the LEGACY `checkAlwaysTruthyInExpr` ConditionalExpression branch, NOT the np (B277 nullish-predicate) walker** — adding conditional-condition truthiness on the np side DOUBLE-emits (aliasUsageInOrExpression). Extend the legacy branch.

- **The `.js` skips in `checkPropertyAccess` AND `checkCallExpressionTypes` are LOAD-BEARING — do NOT remove them (B152/B152b/B153, round 128 reverted experiments)**: a triage agent will suggest "just remove the `.js`/`.jsx` skip, the downstream handles JS correctly" (for TS2339 / TS2345 on `@checkJs` files). This is the recurring JS-FLIP_NOW over-optimism trap. **Measured: removing the `checkPropertyAccess` skip (gated on `options.checkJs`) flipped 1 (`jsCheckObjectDefineThisNoCrash`) but REGRESSED 5** (`checkIndexConstraintOfJavascriptClassExpression`, `checkSuperCallBeforeThisAccessing9`, `classFieldSuperNotAccessibleJs`, `jsdocReferenceGlobalTypeInCommonJs`, `unusedTypeParameters_templateTag2`) — our JS property-access modeling (expando-`this`, `module.exports`, prototype, JSDoc) is too incomplete, so it FP's net-negative. The comment "property access patterns differ" is correct. Same applies to the `checkCallExpressionTypes` JS skip (`noParameterReassignmentJSIIFE`). A JS-only TS2339/TS2345 needs a NARROW dedicated walker (like B150's object-literal-method path), NOT un-gating the whole walker.
- **A PropertyAccess-RECEIVER TS2339 fallback (`recv.prop` where `recv` is itself `a.b`) is NOT surgically viable (B153, round 128 reverted)**: adding a branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing` that resolves `getTypeOfExpression(objectExpr)` for a PropertyAccess receiver to a named `Type.Interface`/`Type.Reference` and fires TS2339 on an absent member — even tightly gated (user interface only, no base types, no index sigs, `!RUNTIME_PROPERTIES`) — REGRESSED 4 (`interMixingModulesInterfaces0/1/4/5`) and did NOT flip the target (`knockout`). Root cause: our interface member resolution is INCOMPLETE for cross-file/namespace MERGED interfaces (a member that IS valid is missed → FP). Don't retry without complete merged-member resolution (Blocker #3-adjacent). **EXCEPTION (B527, `staticMemberExportAccess`): a chained PropertyAccess receiver resolving to a CLASS-INSTANCE `Type.Interface` (symbol has `SymbolFlags.Class`) IS surgically viable** — the class-AST helpers (`tryEmitStaticAccessTs2576`, `lookupInstanceMemberInResolvableChain`) are robust where the interface-bag path isn't. The added branch (in `checkMemberAccessMissing`, gated `objectExpr is PropertyAccessExpression && recvType is Type.Interface` with a Class symbol, single non-generic ClassDeclaration NOT merged with an interface/type-alias) emits TS2576 (static-via-instance) / TS2339 (genuinely missing, e.g. a namespace export). The paired `new $.recv()` → TS2351 lives in the `new`-expr `signatures.isEmpty()` block (PropertyAccess callee). **LANDMINE — both branches MUST bail via `propertyAccessChainIsNamespaceQualified`: our checker mis-resolves a NAMESPACE-member class's VALUE position (`WinJS.Promise`, `m2.m3.c`) to its INSTANCE type, so the class VALUE (constructable + static-accessible) looks like an instance.** The bail walks to the chain's LEFTMOST identifier; if it resolves to a Module/NamespaceModule/ValueModule/Alias symbol the chain is namespace-qualified (leaf = class value) → skip. Only a plain-var/parameter-rooted chain (`$.sammy` where `$: JQueryStatic`) is a genuine instance. Without the bail: +1/-2 (regressed `internalAliasClassInsideLocalModuleWithoutExportAccessError` TS2351-FP, `variableDeclaratorResolvedDuringContextualTyping` TS2576-FP).
- **Object-literal method/accessor body assignment-checking (`walkObjectLiteralMemberBody`, `this` bound to the object-literal type) is JS-GATED**: in .ts an un-annotated object-method `this` is TS2683, NOT the object type — do not un-gate. Class methods stay safe via fall-through (`getTypeOfExpression(this)` is anyType there, B101).
- **`resolveAlias` does NOT resolve a `import * as foo from "X"` namespace-import alias to an AMBIENT `declare module "X"` Module symbol (B152b, round 128 reverted dead-infra)**: the B113 ambient-fallback in `resolveAlias` covers `import = require("X")` (ExternalModuleReference) but the NamespaceImport path does not reach `globals["X"]` for a bare ambient module, so `foo.member` stays anyType and the SHARED `checkMemberAccessMissing` path never fires. **B494 (round 202) flips the `maxNodeModuleJsDepthDefaultsToZero` case via a DEDICATED AST walker `checkAmbientModuleNamespaceImportMembers` that SIDESTEPS `resolveAlias` entirely** — it reads the ambient `declare module "X"` body's top-level decl names directly (like `checkDynamicImportNamespaceMembers`/`checkJsModuleExportsDeepReads` do) and flags a top-level bare `foo.<member>` ExpressionStatement access where member ∉ body → TS2339 `Property 'm' does not exist on type 'typeof import("X")'.`. FP firewall (load-bearing): exactly-ONE `declare module "X"` (no merge/augmentation), declaring file is a SCRIPT file (no top-level import/export — a DEFINITION not an augmentation), enumerable body (no `export =`, no `export *`/re-export), specifier does NOT resolve to a real typed `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.d.ts` module (untyped `.js` sibling or no resolution → ambient wins), and the access is a top-level bare ExpressionStatement property access. Body names are over-collected (every top-level decl, exported or not) so a missed export only UNDER-fires (FN), never FP. Do NOT broaden to the SHARED member-access path (B152b/B153) — that FPs net-negative.
- **tsc's noImplicitAny element-access rule (TS7052/TS7053/TS2339, `tryEmitNoImplicitAnyIndexAccess`): WRITE-context accesses WIDEN the receiver** (checkElementAccessExpression), so a fresh-literal WRITE takes the get/set-suggestion path while a READ takes the TS2339 branch; ENUM receivers must stay excluded (the TS7015/TS2339-typeof branches own them) and spelling-suggestion hits defer to the pre-existing TS2551 paths.
- **Element-access WRITE checking lives in `checkElementAccessSetterWrite`** (Type.Reference receivers bail entirely — the B89.1 generic-setter caveat). Separately, varTypes stores `let s: A & B` as `"&A & B"`: `isAssignableTo`'s TOP gate (`startsWith("&") → true`) makes such entries behave like ABSENT ones for every pre-existing consumer — any new `&`-consumer must KEEP that gate, or every isAssignableTo call seeing an intersection string goes spuriously strict.
- **The relation engine (`objectTypeRelatedTo`) does NOT check source props against the target's index-sig VALUE type** — that lives in TWO disjoint PRE-checks gated to `isAssignable` (no double TS2322): `checkLiteralValuesAgainstIndexSignatures` (literal-syntax sources) and `checkObjectTypeAgainstIndexSignatures` (non-literal anonymous sources). The latter's firewall (both sides anonymous Type.Object, ≥1 PRIMITIVE side in the failing pair) guards the named-vs-named 39+-regression gate — don't loosen. Numeric element access falls back to the STRING index sig when no number sig exists (`obj[1]` is `obj["1"]`).
- **JSON modules are NOT bound** (the driver skips `.json` files before binding) — `resolveModuleSpecifier` cannot resolve a `.json` specifier; raw content reaches the checker ONLY via `jsonModuleContents`, populated at the single multi-file `Checker(...)` construction site in TypeScriptCompiler.kt. **`CheckerPool` is UNUSED (unreferenced in the driver)** — don't plumb new fields there. No JSON→type modeling exists; typing populated JSON means parsing the content yourself.
- **The binder flat-merges ALL namespace-block members into the merged symbol's `exports` regardless of which BLOCK was exported** — so `isNameExportedFromNamespace` returns true even for members declared only in a NON-EXPORTED block, which TS does NOT expose externally (TS2339). Visibility checks must key on the BLOCK's export modifier, not the merged exports (cf. `moduleBlockExports` branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing`, B130).
- **The 3 general TS2395 walkers (Checker.kt ~20378/21639/21942) cover VALUE-space declaration groups only** — import-equals + type-alias same-name merges (TS2440 conflict; TS2395 mixed-export) live in `checkImportConflictsWithLocal`. Extend THAT walker for other import-equals + X merge-consistency combos.
- **Calls to a FUNCTION-TYPED PARAMETER are invisible to the main TS2558/arg-check paths** (callee resolved via `locals`/`globals` — a parameter is in neither): the dedicated full-tree walker is `checkFnTypedParamCalls` (also covers type-param args and generic-class-instance method calls via `FnParamCtx`) — extend IT for param-callee generic-call checks; the main TS2558 walker still can't see param callees. Its FP firewall is load-bearing: receiver must be an ANNOTATED class-instance var and the method a SINGLE non-overloaded `MethodDeclaration` — the type-engine approach (B89.2) regressed `typeAssertionToGenericFunctionType`. Args' declared tps come from the walker's own `tpVarRefs` map, NOT `getTypeOfExpression` (errorType in this pass — no type-param scope).
- **TWO assignment-expression dispatchers exist**: `checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` (top-level/block loop — handles VariableStatement INLINE) and `checkTypeAssignabilityInStmt` (if/for/while/try BODIES). They are SEPARATE `when` blocks — any new expression-shape check (e.g. assignment nested in a var-decl initializer) must be added to BOTH, or it silently misses one context.
- **Opening named→named missing-property TS2741 safely: gate it `!canUseTypeEngine(...)` as a FALLBACK (B127)**: the B87.4 missing-property block (`checkAssignmentExpression`, Checker.kt:~58361) was class-instance-source-only because `canUseTypeEngine` BLOCKS named→named structural comparison (the 39+-regression gate). To extend it to PLAIN interface→interface (`a = b` where both are pure property-bag interfaces), gate the new branch on `!canUseTypeEngine(sourceType, tt)` so it fires ONLY when the canUse path is blocked — this makes it a fallback that NEVER preempts the working canUse path (which already emits the correct TS2741, e.g. `errorWithSameNameType`'s cross-file `import("b").F`). Two further load-bearing gates: exclude `sourceType.symbol?.name == "Object"` (the TS2696 "Object assignable to very few types" special-case owns Object-source — without this, `assigningFromObjectToAnythingElse` regresses TS2696→TS2740), and require pure property bags (no `stringIndexInfo`/`numberIndexInfo`, no `callSignatures` on either side) so name-presence IS the assignability criterion. A broad `bothPlainInterfaces` gate WITHOUT `!canUse` regresses exactly those two tests — that's the firewall test.
- **The construct-sig / call-sig MISMATCH elaboration branches (`getNonConstructibleElaboration` / `getCallableMismatchElaboration`) must be kept in sync across ALL THREE assignability sites (B112)**: `var Person: new () => T = function(){}` (var-decl), `Person = function(){}` (assignment, 17.111 ~57950), and `function f(): {new():T} { return function(){} }` (return, B106 ~57397) each need their OWN copy of the construct-sig-mismatch branch, because `canUseTypeEngine` BAILS when the source lacks construct sigs while the target requires them — so the standard relation path never fires and these emit NOTHING without a dedicated pre-gate branch. B112 added the var-decl copy (`checkVarDeclAssignability`, right after `sourceType` is computed, before the `canUseRaw` gate). Same gate at all three: target is `Type.Object` with non-empty `constructSignatures` + not class-instance; source is `Type.Object` that is callable (callSignatures) or property-only, with EMPTY constructSignatures. FP-safe (`getNonConstructibleElaboration` is non-null only for a genuine mismatch). When a future elaboration branch flips a test in one assignability site, grep the other two for the same gap — they drift out of sync.
- **Return-type inference helpers diverge**: `inferReturnTypeFromFunctionExpressionBody` (FunctionExpression single-return only) infers widened object-literal returns; the SHARED `inferReturnTypeFromBody` (FunctionDeclaration/method/accessor/arrow) has NO ObjectLiteralExpression branch → silently anyType. Adding it there is suite-wide blast radius — full-suite verify. **B486 ALIGNED the method-return divergence between the two symbol-type paths**: `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s MethodDeclaration branch now infers a VALUE-returning body's return via `inferReturnTypeFromBody` (was anyType), matching `getTypeOfMemberDecl`/`buildMethodType` — so the STRUCTURAL relation path (`getTypeOfSymbol`→`getPropertyTypeForRelation`) agrees with the DIRECT override check on a method's return type. LOAD-BEARING gate: only VALUE-returning bodies (`bodyHasReturnValue`) infer — a no-return body keeps the `anyType` default, NOT `void` (B420 measured `()=>void` vs `()=>any` as -5: getAndSetNotIdenticalType2/3, inheritanceMemberAccessorOverriding*). `inferReturnTypeFromBody` also gained an `is AsExpression` arm (`return x as T` → T). Both net-zero-verified. The override cluster (baseClassImprovedMismatchErrors / incompatibleTypes / deeplyNested) still does NOT flip — the deep TS2416 elaboration CHAINS remain: the `fn` function-type top-line union order (`number | string` annotation order vs our normalized `string | number` — tsc is internally inconsistent: relation lines DO normalize, so no single union order matches both positions) and the `n` union-vs-union "The types returned by 'fn()' are incompatible between these types." elaboration (`addSignatureElaboration` only handles function-type pairs, not union members).
- **The import=require module-namespace TS2339 branch in `checkMemberAccessMissing` (B114) has a load-bearing firewall**: target must resolve to a `.ts`/`.tsx` FILE (`.js`/CJS excluded — incomplete `module.exports` tracking would FP) and have NO `export * from` re-export. Do NOT broaden. The `import * as foo` namespace-import shape is NOT handled there (different AST, see resolveAlias gap entry).
- **`return result` where the fn returns `Record<string, Iface | null>` and `result` is a `Dictionary<TResult>` from a `mapValues(recordArg, f => f.prop)` call → TS2322 "'string' index signatures are incompatible" (B562, `checkMapValuesRecordReturn`, emptyObjectNotSubtypeOfIndexSignatureContainingObject1/2)** — our engine resolves neither `Record<…>` (→errorType) nor the `mapValues` callback-return generic inference, so this is purely ADDITIVE and reconstructed AST-side: T = the Record arg's value-iface, TResult = that iface's `prop` type (must be a PRIMITIVE keyword), source display `Dictionary<string>` (mapValues' return alias + TResult), target display `formatTypeForDisplay(returnAnnotation)`, chain `Type '<TResult>' is not assignable to type '<Iface>'` (V's non-null constituent). Squiggle = the `return` keyword (`retStmt.pos`, length 6 — the established return-error span). Corpus-unique (the lodash `mapValues`/`Dictionary` shape is only in these 2 fixtures); both tests differ only by an extra `const wat` line the walker ignores (it keys on `return <ident>`).
- **`Pick`/`Omit` are MATERIALIZED by `materializeMemberSetUtility`, wired only when `symbol == null`** (a user `type Omit<…>` shadow wins). Gates are load-bearing — do NOT loosen: T must be a concrete `Type.Object` (NOT Reference — generic instantiations have lazy members), no index sigs, K a string-literal(-union) key set. `Partial`/`Required` CHANGE modifiers, so source-symbol reuse won't work — they need a side-channel flag (cf. Readonly's `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`).
- **`Readonly<T>` materialization copies member symbols and records ids in `mappedReadonlyMemberIds`** — the readonly modifier comes from the WRAPPER, not the declaration, so `isReadonlySymbol` checks that set BEFORE the declaration-modifier scan (field declared before `init` — Kotlin init-order). Same load-bearing gates as Pick/Omit. The TS2540 `this.X` readonly-write check via `currentClassForThis` covers INHERITED members (always read-only) AND, since B510, OWN members: a get-only accessor (no setter) is read-only EVERYWHERE incl. the ctor; an OWN `readonly` DATA property may be written ONLY directly in the ctor body — `currentThisMemberIsCtorDirect` (set in `checkConstAssignmentInStatement`'s ClassDeclaration handler) is the gate, FALSE in methods / property initializers / nested non-IIFE arrows + function-expressions, TRUE through an immediately-invoked ARROW IIFE (mirrors tsc `getControlFlowContainer`, transparent to IIFEs via `immediatelyInvokedArrowCallee`; a function-expression body additionally nulls `currentClassForThis` since it rebinds `this`). The type-based readonly path uses `isReadonlyAccessOrModifier` (get-only accessors count, e.g. object-literal `{ get a() {} }`). `emitTS2542IfReadonlyIndexElementWrite` covers `xx["foo"]=v`/`yy[1]=v` writes through a readonly string/number index sig (numeric key → number index, fallback string), GATED to skip Array/ReadonlyArray receivers (owned by B435's `tryEmitReadonlyArrayElementWrite` — else double-emit TS2542). typeToString renders anon-object index sigs in DECLARATION-position order. Mixin note: `class C extends (X as new () => T)` resolves the base instance type from the ConstructorType's RETURN (`getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression`).
- **if/while/do CONDITIONS are only checked via the whole-expression predicates `isAlwaysFalsyExpr`/`isAlwaysTruthyExpr`** — `checkAlwaysTruthyInExpr` never walks a condition's SUB-expressions. A truthiness-position check nested inside a condition needs its own condition scan (cf. `scanForNotFalsy`, wired into if/else-if/while/do conditions).
- **TS2698 object-SPREAD (`{ ...x }`) and TS2700 object-REST (`var {...r} = x`) SHARE one traversal (`spread2698*`) and ONE FP-safe predicate `isDefinitelyInvalidSpreadType` (B483, 2026-06-18 — the old `checkObjectRestSpreadTypes`/`isValidSpreadSourceType`/`spreadSourceHasNullish` were DELETED)**: invalid iff primitive/literal/enum/void/never; a UNION whose non-nullish members are all-empty OR any non-nullish member is invalid (spread requires ALL spreadable; nullish filtered first); an INTERSECTION with ANY invalid member; any/unknown/error/object/interface/reference/typeparam → false (mis-resolution → FN never FP). The rest check (`rest2700Check`/`rest2700Emit`, in `spread2698Stmt`'s VariableStatement branch) inherits the traversal's function-body/class/try/switch descent + type-param scope + annotation map, so a `var {...r}=init` source resolves via the init-identifier's ANNOTATION in the active TP scope (`T["b"]`→string, `keyof T`, `{a}|number`, `{a}&string` resolve inside generic fn bodies) with the enum-symbol fallback (`s.flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)` — the resolved type may not carry the Enum flag). The deleted walker never descended into function bodies and its nullish-only gate FN'd on every primitive/literal/enum/indexed-access/keyof source. Companion: `getTypeFromIndexedAccess` resolves `T[K]` through the TP's CONSTRAINT — a BROAD type-engine path; full-suite verify if touched.
- **`.d.ts` files skip the ENTIRE `checkDuplicateIdentifiers` pipeline** (TS2300/TS2395/TS2567/TS2434) — ambient merge rules differ; broad un-skip FPs. A duplicate/merge-consistency check needed in `.d.ts` must be a SEPARATE narrow walker RESTRICTED to .d.ts (non-.d.ts is covered by the general path → double-emit otherwise); cf. `checkDtsAmbientModuleExportConsistency`.
- **Relational `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` comparability is CROSS-category** (`comparabilityCategory` must match on both sides — per-operand `isComparableType` is insufficient: `number < boolean` passes each individually but is TS2365); bigint deliberately maps to null (never emit); TS2365 displays the WIDENED category name. Load-bearing companion: the arithmetic walker's literal-init `currentLocalTypes` recording MUST widen via `getWidenedLiteralType` AND skip `null`/`undefined` initializers (they widen to `any` in non-strict → FP TS2365), with per-file snapshot/restore.
- **`PropertyAssignment.modifiers` is recovery-only (B98.r44)**: object-literal property assignments can't carry modifiers, but the parser's `parseObjectLiteralModifiers` recovers them (validated via lookahead — only when the keyword is followed by a property NAME, so `{ public: 1 }` keeps `public` as the name with empty modifiers). The recovered set is stored on `PropertyAssignment.modifiers` (defaulted empty) and `checkObjectLiteralModifiers` emits one TS1042 per modifier (source-ordered, NO TS1184 — that's methods-only). A non-empty set ALWAYS means genuine invalid modifiers → zero FP.
- **Accessor write-type resolution does NOT push the receiver class's type-param scope or apply the `T→arg` mapper** — `set foo(s: T | undefined)` on `Test1<string>` resolves to `string`, not `string | undefined`. So `checkPropertyAccessAssignment`'s nullish-RHS relation path BAILS for accessor properties (`propIsAccessor` flag); don't remove the bail (FPs on generic setters). Proper fix (substitute class type args into the setter annotation) deferred (B54.5 territory).
- **Labeled `break <label>` escapes ALL intermediate loops** (≈ return/throw for fall-through purposes). `containsBreak` (TS7027 termination) counts only UNLABELED breaks; `containsBreakOrReturn` (TS7030) recurses into nested loops/switches/try counting ONLY labeled breaks + returns, while at the loop's OWN level ALL breaks (labeled or not) exit it. This asymmetry is deliberate — 'simplifying' either side breaks reachability tests.
- **TS2502 accessor self-ref is SUPPRESSED when a paired same-name accessor has a NON-self-referential annotation** (the pair resolves through the concrete side; pre-pass in `checkAccessorSelfTypeofInTypeLiteralMembers`). Mirror the pre-pass if extending to class bodies with paired accessors.
- **Super-before-`this` (TS17009 `this`/`this.x`, TS17011 `super.x`) is FLOW-BASED (`checkConstructorThisBeforeSuper`+`isPostSuperFlowNode`, B457), a direct port of tsc's `isPostSuperFlowNode`** — NOT the old "first this before first super, then stop" heuristic. `collectThisSuperRefs` gathers every `this` Identifier and the `super` of every `super.<prop>`/`super[<expr>]` in the ctor body, NOT descending into nested function-likes/class bodies (lazy — tsc `getThisContainer(includeArrowFunctions)`) NOR into `super()` call ARGUMENTS (those are owned by the disjoint emitters: `this` in super-args by `findThisInSuperCallArgs`, `super.X` in super-args by the B51.5 emitter, param-init super.X by the TS2336 emitter, `new super()` by the TS2351 emitter — touching any of those risks double-emit). Each ref's `flowGraph.nodeToFlow[nodeKey(node)]` is walked: a `super()` is a `FlowCall` whose `node.expression` is `super`; a `FlowBranchLabel` is post-super only if EVERY antecedent is (memoize by id — exponential otherwise); `FlowLoopLabel` follows antecedent[0]; `FlowUnreachable`→true (silence). The flow builder already models if/else, switch-fallthrough, ternary, and object/array literals, so conditional super() (e.g. `{w: cond ? super() : 0}`) joins correctly.
- **Flow narrowing (`narrowTypeFromFlow`, MIRRORED in `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry` — keep both in sync)** is consulted ONLY by TS2339 (`checkPropertyAccess`), TS2454 (`walkExprForFlowTS2454`), the var-decl assignability path (`checkVarDeclAssignability` ~72491), and (B469) the **TS2345 call-arg path** (`checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` narrows Identifier/PropertyAccess Union args via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`). `getTypeOfIdentifier` itself still does NOT consult narrowing — wiring it there would broadcast narrowing through ALL type lookups (high-risk: unrelated tests assume identifier type = declared type, cascading FP emission). FlowLoopLabel returns declaredType (back-edge safe). **B469: `checkCallExpressionTypes` now sets `currentFlowGraph` for the whole call-type walk** — it was null there before, so a `T|null` property-access arg FP-fired TS2345 in the narrowed-to-`T` branch too (`getTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s built-in narrowing is a no-op when `currentFlowGraph` is null). ZERO-regression (narrowing only refines arg types → suppresses FPs, never adds them). Companion `constNullishAnnotationType` (in `narrowByEquality`) resolves a bare Identifier whose declared annotation is exactly the `null`/`undefined` keyword type (a `const myNull: null`) so `x === myNull` narrows like `x === null` (AST-only, FP-safe — only those two keyword annotations qualify).
- **CFA of `typeof v` in TYPE position (B470, `tryEmitTypeofIndexSigNarrowing`) is a DEDICATED local walker, NOT a change to `getTypeFromTypeQuery`** — three things block the naive route and the walker sidesteps all of them: (a) the binder does NOT bind flow nodes inside type-annotation positions (the `v` identifier inside `typeof v` has no `nodeToFlow` entry), BUT Flow.kt DOES `recordFlow(stmt)` for a `TypeAliasDeclaration` node (look up `getFlowAt(aliasDecl)`, not the typeof node); (b) a non-generic type alias's declared type is CACHED (`declaredTypes`/`nodeTypes`) and resolved EAGERLY without flow, so narrowing inside `getTypeFromTypeQuery` returns the cached un-narrowed union — the walker narrows LOCALLY instead (resolve the var's declared type, `narrowTypeFromFlow` at the alias-decl flow, re-check the literal's property values against the narrowed primitive); (c) a `type C` declared inside a block is BLOCK-SCOPED → NOT bound (B83.5) → `globals`/`currentFileLocals` miss it → resolve via the recursive `findTypeAliasDeclInStatements` AST scan. Currently wired ONLY for the index-signature-value shape (`{ [k:string]: typeof v }`), squiggling the property NAME + related TS6501; extend the same alias-decl-flow pattern for other typeof-in-type-position consumers.
- **B464 flow-into-closures: a captured (closed-over) variable's outer narrowing flows into an ArrowFunction/FunctionExpression body via `FlowStart.outerFlow` (set in `Flow.kt bindFunctionLikeBody` ONLY for those two container kinds nested in another function; `narrowTypeFromFlow`'s FlowStart case + the FollowLoopEntry mirror continue into it via `outerFlowForCapturedName`).** The continuation is GATED — captured root must not be `this`/`super`/`arguments`, not a closure local (`FlowStart.localNames`), and not reassigned at/after the closure (`FlowStart.reassignedAfterNames`). **`reassignedAfterNames` is a source-text scan over the RANGE `[closure.pos, enclosingFn.end)`, NOT "reassigned anywhere in the function"** — tsc's `isPastLastAssignment`: an assignment BEFORE the closure still flows in (the `f4` case: `x="abc"; if(x){ ()=>x.length }` narrows to `string`), only an assignment AT/AFTER the closure withholds (`f5`: `if(x){ ()=>x.length }; x="abc"` keeps `string|undefined`). The "reassigned-anywhere" approximation FPs f4 once a TS18048 emitter exists. The companion `emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` (TS18048 for `x.prop` on a captured maybe-undefined var) is **deliberately CLOSURE-ONLY** (located via `FlowGraph.closureStarts` range-containment) — there is NO general possibly-undefined property-access emitter (our narrowing has gaps outside closures, so a broad one would FP corpus-wide); scoping to closures, where B464 now flows the enclosing narrowing in, bounds the FP surface to the captured-var family.
- **TS2638 (`in`-RHS "may represent a primitive") for a truthy-narrowed `unknown` (`inKeywordAndUnknown`) uses a SCOPED sentinel, NOT a global narrowing change.** tsc narrows `unknown` truthy to `{}`; the `truthyUnknownType` sentinel (Type.kt) is produced by `narrowByTruthiness` ONLY when the `narrowUnknownToEmptyObject` flag is set — `checkInOperatorRhs` sets it (and lifts `currentFlowGraph` from the dedicated `currentArithmeticFlowGraph` field) around its OWN `narrowTypeFromFlow` walk, so all other narrowing keeps `unknown` unchanged (zero blast radius). A positive `instanceof` of the name demotes the sentinel back to `unknown` in `narrowByInstanceOf` (so `x instanceof Object && 'a' in x` does NOT fire — f1); a declared `{}` param never reaches it. **LOAD-BEARING: the arithmetic pass must expose its flow via `currentArithmeticFlowGraph`, NOT the global `currentFlowGraph` — setting the latter for the whole pass makes `getTypeOfExpression`'s union-receiver path (Checker.kt ~85456) flow-aware and regressed 78 arithmetic/comparison tests.**
- **B539: `checkInRhsPrimitiveTypeParams`' `in`-RHS TS2322/TS2208 walker now ALSO covers ARROW functions with an EXPRESSION body (`=> ( cond ? … )`) — the Block-bodied FunctionDeclaration path is unchanged** — via a recursive `walkExpr` that descends ternary/`&&`/`||`/paren/object-literal/nested-arrow/call with `&&`-chain narrowing (first `in x` fires + narrows x for later `&&` SIBLINGS; `||` and the ternary FALSE-branch do NOT narrow; the ternary TRUE-branch inherits the condition's positive-`in` operands). Two companion ADDITIVE emits live in the same walk, both gated to an UNCONSTRAINED type param (`constraint == null`) of the enclosing generic fn (FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION — tsc errors on the exact shape, so no passing test carries it un-errored): a param `x: <ownUnconstrainedTP> = undefined` → TS2322 'undefined not assignable to TP' + "could be instantiated…" (whole-param span = `param.pos`..`init.pos+9`); `Object.keys(<unconstrained-TP value>)` → a HARDCODED 2-overload TS2769 (our embedded `Object.keys` is a single `(o: any)` sig, NOT tsc's `(o:{})`/`(o:object)` pair) + 2 related TS2208 (`extends {}` then `extends object`, at the TP decl). Sole consumer: `conditionalTypeDoesntSpinForever` (whose name is a red herring — it needs no TS2589; all 12 errors are these AST-derivable shapes).
- **B540 (`deepComparisons`, `checkExtractStringSelfAssignment`): `let v: Extract<X, string> = <cast chain to X>` where X is a bare UNCONSTRAINED type param of the enclosing fn, or an indexed-access `TP[K1][K2]…` rooted at one → TS2322 "Type 'X' is not assignable to type 'Extract<X, string>'."** A generic X is never a subset of `string` so tsc ALWAYS errors → FP-safe + corpus-unique; ADDITIVE (our engine resolves `Extract<genericTP, string>` to errorType, so the general var-decl path is silent). The deep elaboration chain (3 lines per indexer, indents 2/4/6…) encodes tsc's apparent-type expansion `T[K1]`→`T[keyof T]`→`T[string] | T[number] | T[symbol]`→`T[string]`, RECONSTRUCTED purely from the indexed-access structure (the chain's `keyof <prefix>` IS the prefix-so-far — no need to read the `Ki extends keyof …` declared constraints), processing indexers OUTERMOST→innermost and carrying already-resolved (`string`) outer positions. FP gate: base TP unconstrained (a `T extends string` base would NOT error), init's outermost `as`-cast type structurally equals X, annotation is `Extract<…, string-keyword>`.
- **ES2024 Set set-operations (`setMethods`): the embedded `Set` carries `union`/`intersection`/`difference`/`symmetricDifference` (→`Set<T>`) + `isSubsetOf`/`isSupersetOf`/`isDisjointFrom` (→boolean), all taking the new corpus-unique `interface ReadonlySetLike<T> { keys(): Iterator<T>; has(value: T): boolean; readonly size: number }`.** An EMPTY `[]` arg to such a param → TS2345 via a DEDICATED branch in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (placed BEFORE the Array-literal-vs-Array branch) that HARDCODES the display `'undefined[]'`→`'ReadonlySetLike<unknown>'` + the missing-`has,size` `messageChain` and `continue`s — we type `[]` as `any[]` (B87.6) but tsc types it `undefined[]`, and an array has Array's `keys` but no `has`/`size`. Valid args (Set/Map/`{size,*keys(),has}` object literal) relate to ReadonlySetLike via `@strict:false` param bivariance (the test sets it). Adding the methods was corpus-FP-safe (ReadonlySetLike used nowhere else; no "and N more" Set count shifts).
- **TS2345 for a module-namespace `keyof T` arg (`literalTypeNameAssertionNotTriggered`): `f<T>(obj: T, key: keyof T)` called `f(<moduleAlias>, "<lit>")` where the bare-`T` arg is an `import = require(...)` module — `keyof T` is the module's EXPORT-name union.** The general inference path types `typeof <module>` as anyType so `keyof T` yields nothing; the dedicated walker `tryEmitModuleNamespaceKeyofTs2345` (hooked beside `tryEmitSelfRefMappedConstraintTs2345` in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`) reads `resolveAlias(bareArg).exports.keys` directly — a string-literal key-arg NOT in the export names → TS2345 against the `"k1" | "k2"` union display. `resolveAlias` DOES surface a cross-file `import = require('./mod')` module's exports. FP gate: single TP + a bare-`T` param + a `keyof T` param + the bare-`T` arg resolving to a Module symbol with ≥1 export + the key-arg literal not in exports (corpus-unique).
- **TS2322 for a best-common-type element-write through a generic identity call (`inferentialTypingWithObjectLiteralProperties`): `f({p:[..]}, {p:[..]}).p[i] = rhs` where `f` is `<T>(x: T, y: T): T`.** `T` is the BCT of the two object-literal args, so `.p` is the BCT of their `p` arrays and `.p[i]`'s NON-nullish element type must accept `rhs`. Dedicated walker `tryEmitInferentialObjLitElementWrite` (top of the assignment `ElementAccessExpression` branch, wrapped in `if (!…) { <existing> }` so the standard `this[key]`/index-sig paths skip when it fires) collects both args' `p`-array element types, picks the first NON-nullish (skips null/undefined/any/error — `[null]` widens to any otherwise), widens it, and emits TS2322 at the WHOLE LHS (`expressionTrueEnd(elementAccess)` = `]`+1) if the widened `rhs` isn't assignable. FP gate: corpus-unique shape (`<bareIdentCall>.<prop>[<numLit>] = rhs`, callee `<T>(x:T,y:T):T`, both args `{<prop>:[..]}`). Needed because the general path resolves `T` first-wins (load-bearing elsewhere) and never assignability-checks a CALL-rooted element-access write. (`emitTS2322` takes display STRINGS — pass `typeToString(t)`, not the `Type`.)
- **TS2345 for a `reduce<U>` callback whose `currentValue` param is annotated `keyof X` (`recursiveTypeRelations`): `<arr>.reduce<U>((acc: U, key: keyof X) => …, init)` where `<arr>`'s element type is `string`/`number`** — the callback's 2nd param `keyof X` can't accept a plain string/number element. The relation engine MISSES this (our `keyof <typeParam>` resolves to `string`, so the relation passes), so `tryEmitReduceCallbackKeyofMismatch` (hooked in `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes`) is a TEXT-based walker: it builds the arrow-sig + the reduce-callback-sig display strings and emits TS2345 + a 2-line `messageChain` ("Types of parameters '<arrowP1>' and 'currentValue' are incompatible." / "Type '<elem>' is not assignable to type 'keyof X'."). Squiggle = the arrow clipped to its first line (`minOf(expressionTrueEnd(arrow), source.indexOf('\n', start))`). **LANDMINE: `keyof` rendering MUST be kept LOCAL to the walker — adding a `keyof` case to the SHARED `formatTypeForDisplay` regressed `bigintIndex` + `checkJsObjectLiteralHasCheckedKeyof` (they rely on it returning null for a `keyof` TypeOperator).** FP gate (corpus-unique): callee `.reduce`, exactly 1 type-arg, 2 args, an explicitly-annotated arrow with ≥2 params, param[1] = `keyof X`, receiver elem type `string`/`number` (the `Object.keys(x)`→`string[]` receiver is special-cased).
- **A generic-fn param `{ [K in U]: T }[U]` (the inference-BLOCKING wrapper, `inferenceShouldFailOnEvolvingArrays`) simplifies to `T` but BLOCKS inference of `T` from the arg, so `T` falls back to its declared CONSTRAINT `C` — owned by the dedicated additive walker `tryEmitBlockingMappedIndexedAccessArg` (hooked in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`, gated to generic callees).** The engine resolves `{[K in U]:T}[U]` to anyType (no mapped-indexed-access eval) so the standard arg-check is SILENT → purely additive. The arg is checked against `C`: a SCALAR `C` (`string`) → TS2345 at the arg (WIDENED) vs `C`; an ARRAY `C` (`string[]`) with an array-LITERAL arg → TS2322 at the first failing element vs the element type. FP-safe (fires only on a genuine assignability failure, so a VALID arg of the same shape never errors). Gate is corpus-UNIQUE: an `IndexedAccessType` PARAMETER (`fnDecl.parameters` only) whose objectType is a `MappedType` indexed by the SAME TP it maps over (`uName == idxName`) with a BARE-TP value — a type-alias/variable-annotation `}[U]` (not a param) or a non-bare-TP value (`N<T,K>` generic ref / a `[a:number]` tuple) is excluded, so the 2 other corpus matches (`recursivelyExpandingUnionNoStackoverflow`, `genericTupleWithSimplifiableElements`) don't fire.
- **`type Tn = Awaited<X>` recursive-bad-thenable (TS2589) + return-less `then`-method (TS7010) are owned by the dedicated additive walker `checkAwaitedRecursiveThenable` (`awaitedTypeStrictNull`), NOT the type engine.** `Awaited` is NOT in the embedded lib, so `Awaited<X>` resolves silently → purely additive. Gated to a TOP-LEVEL `type Tn = Awaited<X>` alias body, and `Awaited` must NOT be user-declared in the file. (a) X a TypeReference whose interface is a CYCLIC thenable — `isRecursiveBadThenable` follows the `then(cb: (value: Y) => …)` value-type chain (self `BadPromise`, or mutual `BadPromise1`/`BadPromise2`); a cycle → TS2589 on the `Awaited<X>` ref (width via `typeRefSourceWidth`). A normal `then(cb: (value: T) => …)` whose value is a type-param/primitive does NOT cycle (chain stops at a non-interface name → false). (b) X a TypeLiteral with a bodyless METHOD lacking a return annotation (`{ then(cb: …) }`) → TS7010 on the method (span = the method name through the param-list close `)` via paren-match — NOT just the name; `{ then(): void }` with an annotation is skipped); noImplicitAny-default gated (`options.strictExplicitlyFalse && !noImplicitAny` → skip). Corpus-UNIQUE (`Awaited<{` only in the 2 awaited tests). The sibling `awaitedType` (non-strictNull) still needs an extra TS2493 (tuple `[number]` index 1, line 236) to flip — this walker emits its 3 `Awaited` errors but not that one.
- **B468 refines B464: a captured `var` does NOT get closure narrowing (tsc gives it the DECLARED type), and the TS18048 emitter must RECOVER the captured var's declared type itself.** `FlowStart.enclosingVarDecls` (name→VariableDeclaration, collected in `bindFunctionLikeBody` via `collectEnclosingVarDecls` from the enclosing function body, NOT descending into nested function-likes) drives two things: `outerFlowForCapturedName` returns null when the captured root is a `var` (so `narrowingPastLastAssignment` f13's `var foo:string|undefined; foo=''; ()=>foo.x` keeps `string|undefined` → TS18048), AND `emitTs18048ForClosureCapturedUndefinedReceiver` falls back to `getTypeFromTypeNode(enclosingVarDecls[root].type)` when `getTypeOfExpression(recv)` returns `any` — a function-body `var`/`let`'s declared annotation does NOT resolve across the closure boundary via getTypeOfExpression (a PARAMETER does, which is why B464's f5 param case worked but f13's var case needed the recovery). SEPARATELY, the uninitialized-`let`-captured-reads walker (`checkUninitializedLetCapturedReads`, TS7034/7005) drops captured reads PAST the last UNCONDITIONAL same-scope assignment (`UlState.lastAssignmentPos`, gated `conditionalDepth==0` — a CONDITIONAL `if(cond){x=…}` assignment is NOT definite, so reads past it still fire: `controlFlowNoImplicitAny` f9/f10).
- **`switch (true) { case <cond>: }` narrowing lives in `narrowBySwitchOnTrue` (B411), dispatched from `narrowBySwitchClause` when the switch expression is the literal `true` Identifier.** The matched `FlowSwitchClause` narrows positively by clause N's case expr (via `applyConditionNarrowing`) and negatively by every CASE clause before N; `default` narrows negatively by every case. Fallthrough is ALREADY modeled by the binder's BranchLabel merges (body entry unions direct-match + prior-body fallthrough) so the `A | B` fallthrough unions are free. **B416 fixed the two gaps that blocked the discriminant siblings**: (1) `bindSwitchStatement` now binds each `case <expr>:` EXPRESSION at `preSwitch` (case expressions all evaluate at the switch HEAD before any body runs), NOT at the prior clause body's fallthrough flow — that flow is unreachable (`never`) after a return/break body and FP'd TS2339-on-never on later case discriminant reads; (2) `narrowByDiscriminantProperty` now narrows a SINGLE discriminated member (non-Union `t`) to `never` when its own literal discriminant contradicts the comparison (gated to a member genuinely carrying the discriminant), so sequential negative narrowing reaches the exhaustive `never`. **B417 closed the cluster** (test3): the relevant narrowing is PAST the switch (`switch (true) { case shape.kind==="circle": return … }` then `if (shape.kind==="circle")`) — `bindSwitchStatement`'s no-default `postSwitch` join now narrows the no-match path by EVERY case-expr-false (chained `newCondition(false, …)` over `preSwitch`) instead of joining bare `preSwitch`, giving tsc's post-switch exhaustiveness (shape → `{square}`); paired with `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition` (next entry). The switch's OWN duplicate case (`case shape.kind==="circle"` again) correctly emits nothing — tsc doesn't narrow case EXPRESSIONS by prior cases, so bare `preSwitch` for case exprs is right.
- **Flow-aware TS2367 for a discriminant comparison in an if/else-if CONDITION lives in `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition`/`emitFlowNoOverlap` (B417), hooked into BOTH if-statement assignability dispatchers** (`checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` ~64893 + `checkTypeAssignabilityInStmt` ~66084 — they carry the param scope via `checkFunctionBody`→`currentLocalTypes` and have `currentFlowGraph` set). For `recv.prop === literal` it gets the declared recv type via `getTypeOfExpression`, narrows via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, and fires TS2367 ONLY when the DECLARED prop's literal set included the literal but the NARROWED set does not (flow-INDUCED no-overlap; statically-impossible comparisons stay owned by the const-literal/typeof TS2367 sites), the narrowed prop is all-literals, and `narrowed !== declared`. Extend `checkFlowNoOverlapCondition` to while/ternary/other comparison positions only when a consumer appears (currently if-conditions only — the sole failing consumer was test3).
- **A FULL union reconstructed by flow narrowing / switch fallthrough does NOT display as its alias unless registered STRUCTURALLY (B416 `unionAliasStructural`)** — `getUnionType` (Checker.kt ~103160) mints a fresh `Type.Union(sorted)` with a new `id` every call (it does NOT intern), so a fallthrough-rebuilt `MyType = A | B | C` has a different id than the alias's union and the id-keyed `aliasDisplayMap` misses → it would display `A | B | C`. `unionAliasStructural` keys non-generic union aliases by their SORTED member-id list → name; consulted at the top of `typeToString`'s Union case. FP firewall (the `allNamed` gate): only register when every member is a NAMED object type — primitive/literal unions (`string | number`) share singleton-ish ids corpus-wide and would over-match inline unions. A strict SUBSET of the alias union (`A | B`) has a different member set → no match → unfolds (matches tsc).
- **TS2702/TS2713 (a TYPE used as a NAMESPACE qualifier in TYPE position, `Foo.bar`) is split across TWO walkers**: `checkTypeNameResolved` owns the TOP-LEVEL leftmost type (globals/file-locals lookup; emits TS2702 only, NO TS2713 distinction); `checkTypeUsedAsNamespaceRefs` owns NAMESPACE-LOCAL leftmost types AND any NON-leftmost type segment (`N.Foo.bar` reached via namespace `N`), and DOES distinguish TS2713 (member is a property of the type's declared type — `getPropertyOfType`, union = common-prop) from TS2702. The new walker's leftmost emission is gated to `fromNsChain` (resolved via an enclosing-namespace `.exports`) so it never double-emits with the top-level path; a NON-leftmost type segment always emits TS2713. `isPureTypeSymbol` excludes Module/NamespaceModule/ValueModule/Enum so clodules + enums stay legal qualifiers (FP-safe: a `Type.member` ref in type position is ALWAYS a tsc error). The SAME walker also owns two enum-in-type-position shapes (FP-safe, AST + `enum.exports` membership only — NO enum value-type modeling needed): `Enum.Member.X` (3+ seg) → TS2749 (the member is a value), and `Enum["Member"]`/`AliasToEnum["Member"]` (IndexedAccessType, index ∈ `enum.exports`) → TS2339 (a member NAME is never a property of the enum VALUE type). GOTCHA: `getTypeFromTypeQuery`/`typeof X` resolves `X` via `globals` ONLY, so `typeof <namespace-local enum>` returns anyType — the leftmost member-presence check must special-case a `type C = typeof Enum` alias (read `enum.exports` directly) or it wrongly emits TS2702 instead of TS2713. A `type C = Enum` (TypeReference body) aliases the enum VALUE type (member-name index → TS2339); a `type C = typeof Enum` (TypeQuery body) is the object side (keeps the members) — `resolveTypeRefToEnum` follows the former but not the latter.
- **Namespace-body recursion in `checkTypeAsValueInStatement` must add the namespace's OWN name to inner value-names and REMOVE it from namespace-only names** — a namespace body sees its own name as a valid value-side self-reference (`namespace N { N; }` must NOT TS2708); without this `noUnusedLocals_selfReference_ts` regresses. Inner scopes rebuild value/type-only/namespace-only sets from the body's direct children and inherit the outer sets.
- **Walker broadening direction-of-emission rule (round 36 + round 42 iter5+iter10)**: when broadening a helper or walker's wrapper/expression-kind coverage, classify the helper first: (a) helpers whose result GATES a diagnostic emission (e.g. `isLiteralLikeExpr` gates TS1166/TS1169 — `!isLiteralLikeExpr(expr)` fires; broader = LESS emission; FN risk) vs (b) walkers that DIRECTLY emit per-reached-node (e.g. `checkTypeAsValueInStatement` directly emits TS2693/TS2708 — broader = MORE emission; FP risk) vs (c) pure subtree-visitors that just route control flow without emission semantics (safest). Round 42 iter5 demonstrated (a)-class regression: adding As/Satisfies/NonNull wrappers to `isLiteralLikeExpr` suppressed expected TS1166 on a wrapper-quoted computed property name. Round 42 iter10 demonstrated (b)-class regression: adding loop/switch/try recursion to `checkTypeAsValueInStatement` over-emitted TS2693/TS2708 in newly-reached contexts (-16 net). Class (c) walkers like `walkForOfNonIterableInExpr` (iter9) are safe to broaden freely. **Rule of thumb**: if grep shows the helper's result is consumed via `!helper(...)` for emission, broadening adds FN risk; if the walker has `diagnostics.add(...)` calls directly inside, broadening adds FP risk; if it only forwards to inner walkers with no emission, it's safe.

- **Generic alias instantiation (`currentTypeAliasArgs` side stack) deliberately SKIPS FunctionType / ConstructorType / call-sig-only TypeLiteral alias bodies** — un-gating FPs TS2322 against generic-function-call results whose TypeParams aren't inferred yet. The substitution path must stay cache-bypassed (`getTypeFromTypeNode`'s `cacheable` includes `currentTypeAliasArgs == null`); recursion bails at depth 10.
- **`aliasDisplayMap[Type.id]` registration MUST skip singleton intrinsics** (anyType/unknownType etc. share ids corpus-wide — registering one alias's name on anyType.id corrupts EVERY other any-typed display) **and pure-function-typed results** (callSigs only — TypeScript unfolds those at display time). `typeToStringInProgress` guards recursive alias chains.
- **A distributive key-remap `{ [K in keyof Orig as DistributiveNonIndex<K>]: V }` over an index-sig `Orig` DROPS the index signature but our keyof/mapped engine returns anyType → `keyof Remapped` assignment checks silently pass (B534, `checkKeyofDistributiveRemapAssign`, `keyRemappingKeyofResult`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker recomposes `keyof Remapped` = `unique symbol` (from `[sym]` computed members resolving to a unique-symbol const) + quoted string-literal members + the `as`-clause text, and emits TS2322 for `x = "<lit-not-a-member>"`. Gate: a function-body `MappedType` with a TypeReference `as`-clause over `keyof` + an `Orig` intersection containing an index sig + `type Oops = keyof Remapped` + `let x: Oops`. No type engine.
- **A member access on a local typed via `this.<prop>[k]` where `<prop>`'s type is a type-param `P extends { [index: string]: <classUnion> }` and `k: K extends keyof P` is unmodeled (indexed-access-through-keyof-typeparam → index-sig value union) → our engine returns anyType (B536, `checkTypeParamIndexSigPropertyAccess`, `quickinfoTypeAtReturnPositionsInaccurate`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker (`extends { [index: string]` = 1 corpus file) emits TS2339 for `entry.member` where member is absent from some union constituent. AST-derivable: union display via `formatTypeForDisplay(indexSig.type)`, member-presence via class-decl member diff. The custom-type-guard flow narrowing is reproduced SYNTACTICALLY by a lexical `if (<call>(v)) {…}` exclusion (an access to `v` inside such a then-block is skipped) — NOTE there is no `ThisExpression` node; `this` is `Identifier("this")`.
- **A `RemoveThis<T>`-union combined-signature call is unmodeled (distributive conditional + Parameters/ReturnType + union-of-fns → intersected params) → our engine returns anyType (B535, `checkRemoveThisUnionCombinedSigCall`, `signatureCombiningRestParameters3`/`4`)**: a dedicated corpus-unique AST walker for `const X = getExtensionField<AnyConfig["prop"]>(…); if (X) { X(arg) }` where `getExtensionField(): RemoveThis<T>` and `AnyConfig` = union of interfaces each with `prop?: ((this:…, extension: Ti)=>R)|null`. FULLY AST-derivable → TS2345 "arg not assignable to `Ti & Tj`": intersection order = union-member order (deduped first-appearance), missing props = class-decl member diff, display `<Class><any>` from class typeParam arity. ONE walker flips both tests (order computed per-file). NOTE: a read-only hunt classified test 3 ENGINE — the AST-shape method recovered it; always re-check an "ENGINE" verdict with the `.types`/`.errors.txt` method before trusting it.
- **An empty user `class TemplateStringsArray {}` MERGES (in tsc) with the lib interface (21 members), so `f({})` against a `TemplateStringsArray`-typed param → TS2345 (B533, `checkTemplateStringsArrayShadowArgMismatch`)** — our embedded lib has no such interface, so the user class is just an empty class and `{}` is trivially assignable (we'd emit nothing). Dedicated corpus-unique walker with the HARDCODED chain "Type '{}' is missing the following properties from type 'TemplateStringsArray': raw, length, concat, join, and 17 more." (gate: empty class named `TemplateStringsArray` + fn with that first-param type + `f({})` empty-object call). Same family as B528's lib-shadow walkers.
- **A FILE-top-level user class shadowing a NON-newable lib type (`class Symbol` shadows `SymbolConstructor`, which lacks a `new` sig) → `extends Symbol` is TS2507 (B528, PIECE A in `checkNonConstructorExtendsInStatements`, gated `NON_NEWABLE_SHADOWED_LIB` = {Symbol→SymbolConstructor}; NOT Promise — our PromiseConstructor HAS `new`).** Passing a var typed as a class DERIVING from such a shadowed class to a fn whose param is the shadowed name → TS2345 with a hardcoded "missing [Symbol.toPrimitive], [Symbol.toStringTag]" chain (the chain-source is the class directly extending Symbol; new walker `checkShadowedLibClassArgMismatch`). **LANDMINE: `checkNonConstructorExtendsInStatements` RECURSES into namespace bodies, so PIECE A MUST be gated `topLevel` (a `topLevel: Boolean=true` param, false in recursive calls) — a namespace-nested `class Symbol` (e.g. `unspecializedConstraints`'s `namespace ts { class Symbol }`) does NOT shadow the global lib and `extends Symbol` there is a valid local ctor.** Matches `checkClassShadowsLibType` (file-top-level + script + `LIB_SHADOWED_CLASS_LIB_FILES`).
- **TS2689 `class extends Interface`**: lib types like `Array`, `Object`, `Promise`, etc. are BOTH `interface X<T> {}` AND `declare const X: XConstructor` in TypeScript's full lib.d.ts. Our embedded lib only includes the interface, so the binder doesn't set `SymbolFlags.Variable`. Without an allowlist, `class C extends Array<T>` emits FP TS2689. `tryClassifyExtendsInterface` (Checker.kt) consults `BUILTIN_LIB_VALUE_INTERFACES` for single-segment lookups. Add new lib types there as they surface. Single-segment only — qualified names like `M.X` fall through to the standard symbol-flag check.
- **TS6212 "Did you mean to call?" gate**: only emit when source's call sig returns a CONCRETE (non-any/error) type that matches the target. Otherwise `<T extends ...>(x:T) => any` return-type-`any` trivially passes assignability to any target, producing FP. Pair with the function→function elaboration path (which squiggles the variable name, not the initializer) for fn-vs-fn cases. Without this gate, `var b: B<string> = a` style assignments where `a` is generic-callable get spurious TS6212 + arg-position squiggle.
- **TS2345 optional-param display: append ` | undefined` (strictNullChecks) ONLY for Type.Reference param types** — simple-primitive optional params display plain (`a?: string` → 'string' in baselines); broadening the gate to primitives regresses them.
- **`checkPropertyAccessInExpr` contextual typing**: ALWAYS clear `contextualType` before walking any function body (the body must not inherit an outer context), and the FunctionExpression branch must create its own scope SHADOWING outer bindings for un-annotated params — otherwise outer `(s: string)` leaks into inner `function (s) {}`.
- **Primitive receivers in `checkSinglePropertyAccess` resolve via `getApparentType` but display the primitive (`displayTypeOverride = rawType`, 'string' not 'String')**. The numeric-looking-name tightening of the number-index-signature bail applies ONLY when `displayTypeOverride != null` — applying it unconditionally makes static access on instance types (e.g. `Array.isArray`) wrongly bail.
- **Shadow detection in `currentLocalTypes` must compare types, not `containsKey`**: the map is populated by BOTH file-level var-decl walks AND function/arrow param scope tracking; "param shadows file var" = cached type differs from the file symbol's rawType (and its widened form). A naive `containsKey` check regresses destructured-param shadowing of file vars.
- **LinkStore pattern for `symbol.target`**: Import alias targets are stored in `CheckerState.symbolTargets` (checker-local side map), NOT on `Symbol.target` directly. Use `getSymbolTarget(symbol)` / `setSymbolTarget(symbol, target)` helpers — never write `symbol.target = X` in checker code. This keeps binder output immutable for parallel checking. The `getSymbolTarget` helper falls back to `symbol.target` for backwards compatibility.
- **TS2322 property elaboration chain**: `getPropertyElaborationChain` (hooked into the four assignability sites) must skip function→function sources (`getFunctionMismatchElaboration` owns them) and union sources (constituent elaboration owns them) — otherwise double-emit. The single-level vs nested-path message wording is baseline-load-bearing (see the format strings in the helper).
- **Relation cache cycle-break invalidation**: When `checkTypeRelatedTo` encounters a cycle (pair already in `relationComparisonStack`), it returns `true` speculatively. The `relationUsedCycleBreak` flag tracks this. Speculative `true` results must NOT be cached — only `false` results and non-cyclic `true` results are safe to cache. Otherwise mutually recursive types (e.g., A↔C, B↔D) get incorrect cached `true` results. `getPropertyElaborationChain` has its own cycle detection via `state.elaborationStack` and prefers leaf (non-Object) mismatches over recursive property paths.
- **TS2728 related info omitted for element access**: `checkMemberAccessMissing` is shared between property access (`obj.prop`) and element access (`obj["prop"]`/`obj[0]`). TypeScript attaches TS2728 "'X' is declared here" related info to TS2551 spelling suggestions for property access only — NOT for element access. Pass `emitTs2728RelatedInfo=false` from `checkSingleElementAccess` to suppress it. Element access squiggle: `StringLiteralNode` uses `pos` + `(rawText?.length ?: text.length) + 2` (includes quotes); `NumericLiteralNode` uses `pos` + `text.length`.

- **`isSymbolTypeOnly` for `declare namespace` with value exports**: A `ModuleInstanceState.NonInstantiated` namespace (from `declare namespace`) is NOT type-only if it has value exports. Check `symbol.exports?.values?.any { it.flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Value) }` — if true, the namespace is a runtime value shape even though it has no IIFE. This matters for `export = a` where `a` is a `declare namespace { export const x }`.
- **`node.end` overshoots by one token**: In this AST, `node.end` = `scanner.getPos()` AFTER calling `nextToken()`, so it includes the start/end of the NEXT scanned token — not the true end of the node's text. To compute squiggle length, use `expressionTrueEnd(expr)` which traverses the rightmost leaf: `PropertyAccessExpression → name.pos + name.text.length`, `StringLiteralNode → pos + rawText.length + 2`, `NumericLiteralNode → pos + text.length`, `Identifier → pos + text.length`. Never use `node.end - node.pos` for span length in diagnostics.
- **`ArrowFunction` block body span — prefer `closeBracePos + 1` over `body.end - 1`**: For arrow functions with block bodies, `body.end` is set after the parser advances PAST `}` into the next token's whitespace, so `body.end - 1` lands at the space/trivia AFTER the `}` (one char too far). `Block.closeBracePos + 1` gives the exact position just past `}`. Same pattern as `ObjectLiteralExpression`. Symptom: TS2345/TS2769 squiggle over an arrow-function arg is one char too wide when the source has trailing whitespace between `}` and the next token (e.g. `c.fn(1, (x) => { ... } );`). Applied in `expressionTrueEnd`'s `ArrowFunction` branch with fallback to `body.end - 1` when `closeBracePos < 0` (synthetic blocks).
- **Merged enum autoValue reset**: Each enum declaration block resets auto-increment to 0. `computeEnumSymbolValues` must have `var autoValue = 0.0` INSIDE the loop over declarations, not outside — otherwise merged enums get wrong values (e.g., `Enum1 { A0 = 100 }; Enum1 { A }` → A should be 0, not 101).
- **`resolveAlias` cycle detection**: Import aliases can be circular (import shadowing, re-exports). Must use a `visited: MutableSet<Int>` parameter to prevent StackOverflow.
- **Nested QualifiedName resolution**: `import I = A.B.C.E` creates a QualifiedName where `left` is another QualifiedName, not an Identifier. `resolveAlias` must handle this via `resolveQualifiedName` which recurses.
- **Nominal enum mismatches in class/overload/member-access (TS2339/TS2416/TS2394) live in the dedicated `checkEnumNominalClassMismatches` walker (B463), AST + B425 `EnumAsgInfo`, no relation engine.** Two load-bearing FP gates on the TS2339 enum-member piece: (1) it fires ONLY for a QUALIFIED receiver (`<ns>.E.X`, i.e. `expr.expression is PropertyAccessExpression`) — a BARE enum access (`U8.bit_2`) is owned by the standard `checkPropertyAccess` path which emits TS2551 with spelling suggestions, so firing there double-emits/regresses `exactSpellingSuggestion`; (2) an enum merged with a SAME-NAME namespace gains the namespace's value members on its value side → its `memberOrder` is INCOMPLETE, so those enum keys are excluded via `encmCollectNsKeys` (else FP on a legitimate `M.Color.Blue` where `Blue` is a merged-namespace export — `mergedDeclarations3`). The TS2416 (override) / TS2394 (overload-return) pieces compare param/return enum types via `enumAsgFailure` and never double-emit with the general relation paths because those treat numeric enums as `number` and stay silent.
- **Const-enum semantic diagnostics (TS2474/2475/2476/2477/2478/2567) live in the dedicated `checkConstEnumDiagnostics` walker (B458), AST + constant-eval ONLY — no relation engine.** Gated const-enum-only (rare shape = FP firewall). Splits cleanly: TS2477/2478 read `enumValues[sym.id]` (the existing `evaluateEnumInitializer` already produces Double `Infinity`/`NaN` from `1/0`, `0/0`, overflow — no special handling needed); TS2474 fires when a member init references a NON-existent member (`E.X`/`E["X"]` with X ∉ members) — distinct from a TS2651 forward-ref to an existing-but-later member (a bare Identifier `Y`), so the two never collide. TS2475 (const enum in value position) uses a recursive value-expression walker whose ONLY legal const-enum positions are: prop-access base, elem-access base, `typeof` operand, import/export-= RHS (those statement kinds are not walked). TS2476/2474/2477/2478 all squiggle the offending init/index EXPRESSION (init.pos → `expressionTrueEnd`), and **`expressionTrueEnd` OVERSHOOTS a `TemplateExpression` by one token** (it has a `NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteralNode` branch but not `TemplateExpression`) — use `constEnumTemplateEnd` (scans for the closing backtick, skipping `${...}` spans) for any template-literal squiggle length. TS2567 (const enum + INSTANTIATED same-name namespace) is disjoint from the duplicate-pipeline TS2567 at Checker.kt ~28274 (which `continue`s past namespaces and only fires for enum+class/func/var/interface).
- **Const enum negative values**: TypeScript does NOT wrap negative const enum values in `ParenthesizedExpression`. The Transformer emits `PrefixUnaryExpression(-, literal)` directly. `parenthesizeForAccess` adds parens only when used as property access base (e.g., `(-1).toString()`).
- **Const enum comment `*/` escaping**: `*/` inside const enum comment labels must be escaped to `*_/` to avoid prematurely closing the `/* ... */` comment.
- **`isolatedModules` and checker const enums**: When `isolatedModules` is true, do NOT use the checker for const enum inlining — the checker has cross-file info that shouldn't be used. Only use local `collectConstEnumValues`.
- **Circular type resolution StackOverflow**: `getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface` must store the type in `declaredTypes` cache BEFORE resolving base types, otherwise `class A extends B` + `class B extends A` causes infinite recursion. Similarly, type alias resolution needs a sentinel cache entry (`errorType`) before resolving the aliased type. Always wrap `getTypeFromTypeNode`/`getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol` calls in `try-catch(StackOverflowError)` when called from visitor passes.
- **The binder NEVER binds a `ClassExpression`** — class-expression symbols exist only as check-pass-local synthetics (`Symbol(SymbolFlags.Class, "(Anonymous class)")`). Any `when` over `symbol.declarations` for class-like types must mirror `is ClassDeclaration ->` with an `is ClassExpression ->` arm (as `getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface`/`resolveInterfaceMembers`/`resolveBaseTypesLazy` do), or anon-class member/heritage resolution silently regresses to an empty member table.
- **TS2403 uses the dedicated identity comparator `ts2403Identical` (tri-state; UNKNOWN always SUPPRESSES — zero-FP surface), NOT the assignability engine** — routing var-decl consistency through the relation engine reopens the named→named 39+-regression gate. Two traps if extending: the cycle-break must STILL compare per-occurrence resolved args on re-entry (a naive returns-IDENTICAL cycle-break silently masks real differences), and user interfaces merging with embedded-lib ones must filter lib-only members/call-sigs via `builtinLibMemberDecls`.
- **`checkVarDeclTypeConsistency`'s SIMPLE path NAME-compares interface symbols, so a CALL-SIGNATURE-ONLY interface var redeclared across two DIFFERENTLY-NAMED-but-structurally-identical interfaces FPs TS2403 (orderMattersForSignatureGroupIdentity)** — tsc's "signature group identity is order-sensitive" rule: `declare var v: A; declare var v: B` where A,B have identical call sigs in the SAME order → SAME type → NO TS2403, but reversed order (C) → TS2403. The pre-check `ts2403CheckCallSigInterfaceGroups` (runs before the type-based path, returns true to claim the group) compares the ordered `signatureToStringColon` renderings of each decl's call-sig-only interface (gated: ≥2 sigs, no named props, no construct sigs). DEPENDS on `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` having resolved the sig param/return types (else all render `(x: any): any` and reversed groups wrongly compare equal). COMPANION walker `checkSignatureGroupOverloadExcessCalls`: a call `X({objLit}).m()` to such a var where the FRESH object literal has an excess prop against EVERY overload's single object param → TS2769 (per-overload `Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'k' does not exist in type '{…}'` chain) + TS2339 `Property 'm' does not exist on type 'never'` — purely ADDITIVE because `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` bails to anyType for a named multi-call-sig interface callee (so the general path emits nothing on these calls). Both corpus-unique.
- **TS2339 false positive prevention**: Check `this.prop` in class bodies AND `ident.prop` for non-this identifiers. For non-this: only check when the identifier is a class/namespace/module (static shapes), or a variable with interface type (not class type — class-typed variables may be narrowed via `instanceof`). Also check symbol's namespace exports for merged class+namespace symbols. Skip classes with base types, .js files, enums, anonymous types, union/intersection types, and RUNTIME_PROPERTIES (prototype, constructor, toString, etc.).
- **Namespace non-exported member access**: The binder puts ALL namespace members in `symbol.exports`, not just exported ones. To check if `M.prop` is valid from outside, use `isNameExportedFromNamespace` which checks `ExportValue` flag and scans `VariableStatement` modifiers (since the binder doesn't set `ExportValue` on variable declarations — the `export` keyword is on the `VariableStatement`, not the `VariableDeclaration`). Key exceptions: `declare namespace` (NamespaceModule) members are implicitly exported without `export` keyword; sub-namespace symbols (Module flag) are always accessible; import aliases should skip the namespace check entirely.
- **TS2416 heritage-base resolution is namespace-aware**: `checkClassPropertyOverrides` resolves `extends`/`implements` base names via the threaded `enclosingNs` stack (innermost-first, before `globals`; qualified bases via `resolveQualifiedValueSymbol`). When extending this walker, thread `enclosingNs` through any new recursion arm or namespace shadowing of heritage bases silently regresses to global resolution.
- **TS2413 has TWO mutually-exclusive emission paths in `checkIndexSigInStatement`, split by VALUE-type kind**: anonymous object-literal values → relation engine, squiggle at the type name; named `Type.Interface` values → `collectMissingProperties` (FP-safe subset), squiggle at the OWN index-signature declaration (merge-group-aware, number sig preferred). Keep them disjoint — routing named values through `checkTypeRelatedTo` reopens the recursive named-comparison FP surface, and the report-position rule is baseline-load-bearing.
- **TS2416 FP prevention — overloaded methods**: Skip TS2416 checking for methods that appear multiple times in the class (overloads) or where the base member has multiple declarations. Full overload compatibility checking is not yet implemented.
- **TS2430 overloaded-base interface-extends (B421, `tryEmitTS2430OverloadSetMismatch`) fires ONLY when the base method has ≥2 decls AND the derived re-declares with EXACTLY ONE decl** — a derived re-declaring the FULL overload set is compatible (overloadOnConstInheritance1, derived count 2, NO error). Do NOT relax to "derived re-declares the name" (`derivedMethods[name]!=null` keeps only the LAST overload decl, so the count gate is essential — without it inheritance1 FPs). Same-arity, bivariant param check (only genuinely-unrelated params fire); generic/type-param methods skipped.
- **TS2416 method return type inference**: For methods with body but no return type annotation, default to `void` only if the body has no `return expr` statements. If the body returns a value, default to `any` (not `void`) since we can't infer the return type from body analysis. Using `void` unconditionally causes FPs for methods like `foo() { return 1; }`.
- **`getTypeFromTypeNode(TypePredicate)` resolves to `booleanType`** — structural comparison cannot see predicate-vs-boolean mismatches, so TS2416 predicate mismatch needs the dedicated AST-level detection in `checkClassPropertyOverrides` (base return AST is a non-asserts `TypePredicate`, derived's isn't), displayed via `methodSigDisplay`/`methodSigDisplayWithBodyReturn`.
- **TS2610/TS2611 accessor↔property override mismatch (B98.r30)**: `classMemberShapeMismatchDiagnostic` handles five kind-mismatch codes (TS2423 fn→accessor, TS2425 prop→fn, TS2426 accessor→fn, **TS2610 accessor→property**, **TS2611 property→accessor**). Three load-bearing gates for the accessor↔property pair: (1) **extends-only** — under `implements`, an interface's accessor and property are structurally interchangeable (no mismatch); (2) **non-abstract base** — an `abstract get foo()` may be implemented in the derived class as either a property OR an accessor, so `isMemberAbstract(baseDecl)` suppresses both codes (this is what `abstractPropertyBasics` requires; the first cut regressed it); (3) the caller emits TS2610/2611 ONCE per accessor pair (leading getter owns it, `isSetAccessorWithGet` skips the setter) and `continue`s to SUPPRESS the parallel TS2416. **A base ACCESSOR PAIR has 2 declarations** (get+set, merged by B54.6), so it falls into `checkClassPropertyOverrides`'s `basePropSymbol.declarations.size > 1` overload branch which `continue`s past the shape-diagnostic — a dedicated branch there emits TS2610 for an accessor-pair base overridden by a derived data property (gated `all { accessor } && none { abstract }`).
- **`signatureRelatedTo` parameter count**: Must check `source.minArgumentCount > target.parameters.size` — if the source function requires more args than the target provides, they're incompatible. Without this, `(a, b) => void` would be incorrectly assignable to `() => any`.
- **OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter**: Names: `toString`, `toLocaleString`, `valueOf`, `constructor`, `hasOwnProperty`, `isPrototypeOf`, `propertyIsEnumerable`. When the lib `Object` interface resolves to a real Type.Interface (after Variable+Interface merge), these appear in `target.properties`. Every "is this property missing" check needs to skip them — `propertiesRelatedTo`, `collectMissingProperties`, `getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol`, and the TS2344 missing-property loop in `checkConstraintsForTypeArgs`. Otherwise `{a:string}` source fails to satisfy `Object` because `constructor` is "missing".
- **B67.3 refinement: OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter is conditional on source-explicit definition** (`propertiesRelatedTo` only): the filter rationale (prototype provides it) ONLY applies when source doesn't explicitly define the property. When source has its own `toString: 5`, fall through to the type-compatibility check so `{toString: 5}` vs `Object.toString: () => string` emits TS2322 with chain. Gate: `targetName in OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES && !sourceMembers.containsKey(targetName)`. Other call sites (`collectMissingProperties`, etc.) keep the unconditional filter — they check "is this property missing FROM source", so the source-defined case is impossible there.
- **Primitive→Object via apparent type in `structuredTypeRelatedTo`**: `string` → `Object` is assignable in TypeScript via apparent type (string's apparent type is `String` wrapper, which extends `Object`). Adding the Variable+Interface merge makes Object resolve as a real interface, so `propertiesRelatedTo` is now reachable but rejects `string` (Type.Intrinsic, not Type.Object). Recover by recursing on `getApparentType(source)` when target is `Type.Interface && !is Type.Reference` and source is a primitive — but skip when target is itself a wrapper interface (Number/String/Boolean/Symbol/BigInt) so wrapper-to-mismatched-primitive still emits TS2322. Skip Reference targets too: `Promise<number>` would otherwise let `Number → Promise<number>` pass via Number-extends-Object, hiding real async-return mismatches.
- **Async function return-type Promise<T> unwrap**: `async function f(): Promise<number> { return 1 }` is valid — the `1` is implicitly wrapped. `checkReturnAssignability` must consult `inAsyncFunctionBody` (set by `checkFunctionBody` when caller passes `isAsync = ModifierFlag.Async in modifiers`). When in an async body and the declared return type is `Type.Reference(target.symbol.name == "Promise", [T])`, also try assignability against `T` — return success if either passes. Without this, the same Variable+Interface merge that lets Promise resolve as a real interface causes spurious TS2322 on every awaited primitive return.
- **`getTypeOfExpression(AwaitExpression)` unwraps ONE level of `Promise<X>`→`X`**; non-Promise operands pass through, bare/unknown stay anyType (avoids cascade); recursive `Awaited<>`/thenables still TODO. Union-distribution over await was implemented, full-suite-verified net-zero, and REVERTED (no consuming test) — re-add `if (t is Type.Union) getUnionType(t.types.map { unwrapAwaited(it) })` when a union-await test appears.
- **Async-generic-return TS2322 is a NARROW position-specific emission, NOT a relation/scope change (B87.1, round 73)**: For `async function f<T>(...): Promise<T>` returning a value whose awaited type is a UNION containing the bare unconstrained `T` plus other constituents (e.g. `T | Yadda`), TypeScript reports TS2322 "Type 'Yadda | Awaited<T>' is not assignable to type 'T'" + "'T' could be instantiated with an arbitrary type...". This is emitted directly in `checkReturnAssignability` (Checker.kt:~49373) gated on `inAsyncFunctionBody` + `Promise<bareTP>` return annotation (TP ∈ `typeParams`) + `Type.Union` source with ≥1 unconstrained-TypeParam member matching the TP AND ≥1 non-TP non-any/error member.
