Display = non-TP members first, then TP members wrapped `Awaited<name>`. **DO NOT** "fix this properly" by (A) pushing the function's TPs into `currentTypeParamScope` during return-type-node resolution (would make `Promise<T>` a `Type.Reference` broadly — cascades through every generic return annotation) or (B) making assign-to-bare-TypeParam fail in the relation engine (cascades through every internal generic-function assignment). Those broad changes were deliberately avoided; the narrow emission captures the rule for this shape with a tiny FP-surface. The general (A)/(B) fix remains documented in PLAN-PHASE-4.md B87.1c for any future async-generic test the narrow gate misses.
- **The missing-property SUBSET of named→named comparison is SAFE to open (B87.4, round 73)** — `canUseTypeEngine` gates OFF full named→named (class/interface) structural comparison because of the documented 39+ recursive-type-expansion regressions. BUT `collectMissingProperties` (name-presence: iterate target's required props, check absence in source) does NOT recurse into property types, so it cannot trigger that expansion. `checkAssignmentExpression`'s Identifier-target path (Checker.kt:~50173) emits TS2741/2739 for class-instance-source (`Type.Interface` whose symbol has `SymbolFlags.Class`) → `Type.Interface`-target when required members are absent — added as an additive branch BEFORE the `canUse` gate. It's safe despite class→interface assignments being common because satisfying classes yield empty missing-sets (no emission). DO NOT extend this to full structural relatedness (property-TYPE comparison) for named→named — that re-opens the 39+-regression gate.
- **Dotted-only `@lib` (no full es-version base) → `libProvidesBaseValues()` false**: emits the FIXED file-less TS2318 set {Boolean, CallableFunction, IArguments, NewableFunction, Number, Object} and injects `Array` into `typeOnlyNames` (value use → TS2693). Array/String/Function/RegExp are deliberately NOT in the TS2318 set (the es2015.* sublibs augment them). The inverse family (es6 features under es5-only lib) is a separate unbuilt feature.
- **`formatTypeForDisplay` does NOT render the `readonly` TypeOperator (B87.2, round 73)**: `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeOperator(ReadonlyKeyword, TupleType))` returns `null`, so any diagnostic display for a `readonly [...]` / `readonly T[]` annotation must build it manually as `"readonly " + formatTypeForDisplay(operator.type)`. (Same likely applies to `keyof`/`unique` TypeOperators — check before relying on formatTypeForDisplay for operator types.)
- **`formatTypeForDisplay`'s TypeLiteral case renders members in CANONICAL tsc order (call sigs → construct sigs → index sigs → properties+named-methods), NOT source order (B471)** — tsc reorders anonymous object-type members; preserving source order mismatches the baseline display. Construct sigs render `new (…): T` (space after `new`); an empty object type renders `{}` not `{ ; }`. FP-safe to reorder: a passing test whose TypeLiteral annotation had non-canonical source order would already be failing. SEPARATELY, a bare class identifier in VALUE position displays as `typeof C` (the constructor), not `C` (its instance type) — the var-decl/assignment source-display sites override this when the init is a pure-class identifier.
- **Inline-TypeLiteral TS2411 (property type vs the literal's OWN index-sig value type) lives in `checkInlineTypeLiteralIndexProperties` (B471), SEPARATE from the class/interface-body TS2411 machinery in `checkIndexSigInStatement`** — the VariableStatement branch returns BEFORE the class/interface property-vs-index check, so inline `var x: { z: I; [s:string]: {…} }` annotations needed their own recursive walker (descends into array/union/intersection/nested-property/index-value types). FP firewall: PropertyDeclaration members only, both types concrete non-any/error, definitive relation failure.
- **INHERITED computed-symbol-keyed members vs an OWN `[key: symbol]: V` index sig (TS2411) is a THIRD, disjoint walker `checkInheritedSymbolIndexMembers` (B515), NOT `checkIndexSigInStatement` (own members only)** — `class Test extends Array1 { [k: symbol]: string }` where `Array1` is BOTH `interface Array1<T>` AND `declare var Array1: ArrayConstructor1`: the `extends` uses the VALUE ctor (`new(): Array1<any>`), so (a) NO TS2314 generic-arity error (suppressed via `heritageBaseHasValueVarDecl` at the class-extends call site — `extends` of a name with a value `VariableStatement` decl skips `checkHeritageTypeArgCount`), and (b) the base's inherited `[Symbol.iterator]: () => IterableIterator<any>` / `[Symbol.unscopables]: () => {…}` methods are checked against the own `symbol` index value type → TS2411 + related TS2728 at the cross-file member decl. Member return display substitutes the base interface's type-param names → `any` (instance is `B<any>`) via a recursive `renderAny` over the return TypeNode (NOT the type engine — `IterableIterator<any>` would resolve fine, but the substitution-via-TypeNode avoids relying on lib resolution). **FP firewall (corpus-rare = only the indexSignatureInOtherFile pair): own symbol index sig whose value type is a PRIMITIVE (a method is never assignable to a primitive), extends-via-value base, zero-param computed-`Symbol.X` methods only.**
- **INHERITED / merged / cross-base property-vs-index-sig TS2411 + TS2413 is a FOURTH disjoint walker `checkFullIndexConstraints` (B518), separate from the per-block `checkIndexSigInStatement` (own-block props only) and B515 (symbol keys)** — the per-block emitter processes each `interface`/`class` declaration block in ISOLATION (own-block props × own-or-base sigs), so it MISSES: an INHERITED prop vs an own index sig (`interface B extends A { [n:number]:string }` → A's `1:Z` vs the number sig), a MERGED-block prop vs another block's sig, an own prop vs an INHERITED number sig (the per-block numeric-name loop is own-sig-only), and a CROSS-BASE conflict (`interface D extends B,C` → B's `4,5,6` vs C's `[s:string]:number`, and B's number-sig vs C's string-sig TS2413). B518 computes the FULL merged+inherited member/sig sets and emits the gaps, **DEDUPING against the per-block emits by (code, start, message)** (so it never double-emits and the message must match EXACTLY — e.g. a STRING-LITERAL property name renders QUOTED `'"-Infinity"'` with span incl. the quotes, matching the per-block code). tsc's **errorNode rule** (`checkIndexConstraintForProperty`): an OWN prop reports at the prop; an INHERITED prop reports at the OWN index sig (if any) else at the TYPE NAME; the index-sig span INCLUDES the trailing `;`. **"both-inherited" dedup**: a conflict where prop AND sig both come from bases is reported at THIS type ONLY when no single direct base sees BOTH (else the base already reports it — tsc reports at the most-base joint-visibility type). FP firewall (the walker runs for EVERY inheriting/merged interface/class): PRIMITIVE index value types only (a method → fresh `Type.Object()` marker, never assignable to a primitive); non-generic types only; SINGLE-FILE base resolution via a `byName` map (lib/cross-file bases resolve to null → conservatively skipped); numeric-name applicability via `isCanonicalNumericPropertyName` (a numeric name applies to BOTH number+string sigs, others to string only). Gated to types that EXTEND a base or have a >1-block merge group.
- **A parameter whose DEFAULT initializer references the parameter's OWN name emits TS2372 PER REFERENCE (not per line), plus optionality-removal companions TS2502/TS18048 when the param type includes `undefined` (B519, in `walkParamForbiddenParameters`)** — the OLD code used `findParamSelfRef` (FIRST ref only) + `emitParamInitForbidden` (dedups by LINE), so two self-refs on one line (`x: number|undefined = x > 0 ? x : 0`) collapsed to one TS2372. `collectParamSelfRefs` now gathers ALL refs (each tagged whether it is a direct operand of an arithmetic/relational/bitwise binary — `ARITH_REL_OPERATORS`) and `emitDiagAtPos` dedups by (code, POSITION). tsc reports per identifier resolution. **TS2502 (`'x' is referenced … in its own type annotation.`) and TS18048 are gated to `paramTypeIncludesUndefined(param)` (a union member `T | undefined` OR an optional `?`) AND `strictNullChecks`** — this is tsc's "optionality removal" circularity (the param's type resolution needs the default to decide whether to strip `undefined`, and the default references the param). Only `circularOptionalityRemoval` in the corpus has TS2502+TS2372; every OTHER param-self-ref test (`selfReferencesInFunctionParameters`, `asyncArrowFunction3`, …) has plain `number`/`string`/inferred types → no undefined → TS2372 ONLY (the gate keeps them clean). TS2502 squiggles the WHOLE parameter (name through the default's rightmost leaf, via `paramDefaultRightmostEnd` — `expressionTrueEnd` has no ConditionalExpression branch). TS18048 fires only for an arith/rel-operand self-ref (`x > 0` → yes; `? x : 0` ternary branch → no). `ARITH_REL_OPERATORS` lives in the COMPANION object (an instance `val` declared after `init` is null during the check pipeline — the documented init-order gotcha, which bit both B518 and B519).
- **AsExpression (`expr as T`) is visited by `walkTypeAssertionsInExpr` but NOT forwarded to the `onAssertion` callback (B87.2, round 73)**: the shared walker recurses into `AsExpression.expression` but only calls `onAssertion` for `TypeAssertionExpression` (`<T>expr`). So AsExpression casts get no TS2352/erasable checks via that callback. To check an AsExpression shape, gate an inline emit in the walker's AsExpression branch behind a checker field set only during the relevant pass (e.g. `inNullCastOverlapPass`) — the walker is shared across multiple passes (null-cast-overlap + erasable-syntax), so an unguarded inline emit double-fires.
- **Object-literal cast overlap (TS2352, `emitTS2352IfObjectLiteralToInterfaceCast`, B448) uses BIDIRECTIONAL comparability — a one-directional check FPs on EVERY partial/empty cast.** tsc fires TS2352 for `<IFoo>{objLit}` only when NEITHER the literal is comparable to the target NOR the target to the literal. Detecting just "literal not comparable to target" (a missing-required or incompatible-shared prop) is WRONG: an empty `<B>{}` and a props-subsumed partial `<IFoo>{n:1}` (IFoo has more props) are LEGAL because the TARGET side overlaps. The load-bearing gate is `if (checkTypeRelatedTo(target, getTypeOfObjectLiteral(ol), comparableRelation)) return` (suppress when target is comparable to the literal). The first cut regressed contextualTyping/contextualTyping11/m7Bugs (+1/-3 swap) until this gate was added. Any new cast/assignability-overlap check must consider BOTH directions.
- **Protected-member READ access (TS2445/TS2446, `checkProtectedMemberReadAccess`, B446) is a DEDICATED AST pass, separate from B377 (protected WRITES from free functions).** Enclosing class for protected access = the lexical class, or a `this: T` param's class / its constraint's class (null for a free fn with an unconstrained/non-class `this`). TS2445 when enclosing is null or doesn't derive from the declaring class; TS2446 when it derives but the receiver's class isn't enclosing-or-derived. Receiver resolved AST-only (this / param / local-typed-as-class / `new C()` / `this.classProp`); statics, generic-instantiated receivers, `super`, and writes are excluded (writes owned by B377). It does NOT cover top-level reads, static-side reads (`C.staticProtected`), write-context TS2446, or the protected-MISMATCH-in-assignability TS2322/TS2415 chains — `protectedMembers` needs all of those (4 coupled pieces, deferred).
- **`Type.Reference` interning**: Construction goes through `getOrInternReference(target, args)` (CheckerState `referenceCache` keyed on `target.id|arg1.id,arg2.id,...`). Identical instantiations share an instance and `Type.id`, which is what the id-based cycle detection in `relationComparisonStack` relies on. Do NOT call `Type.Reference(...)` directly in new code.
- **`isDeeplyNested` heuristic on parallel target stacks**: `relationSourceTargets`/`relationTargetTargets` track `target.id` of every `Type.Reference` source/target on the active comparison stack. When the same target appears 5+ times on its stack, `checkTypeRelatedTo` short-circuits to `true` (cycle break). Catches infinitely-expanding generic comparisons like `A<T> { x: A<()=>T> }` vs `B<T> { x: B<()=>T> }` whose Refs grow unboundedly and never re-occur identically. The same-target arg-shortcut (step a) ALSO defers to structural on re-entry (`countOccurrences > 1`) so the inner cycle-break can fire instead of an eager arg-mismatch `false`.
- **TypeParam vs TypeParam relates via apparent types**: `structuredTypeRelatedTo` returns `true` when both sides are `Type.TypeParam` and either is unconstrained (apparent type `{}`); when both have constraints, recursively compares those. Without this, generic-method signature comparison treats source's `K` and target's `K` (separate fresh params from `resolveGenericPropertyType`) as opaque-and-distinct → spurious TS2322.
- **Generic-fn-type BIPARTITION TS2322 (`noStrictGenericChecks`) is a DEDICATED AST walker `checkGenericFnTypeBipartition`, NOT the relation engine (B484)** — because the apparent-`{}` shortcut above makes the relation engine PASS `<S>(x:S,y:S)=>…` assignable to `<T,U>(x:T,y:U)=>…` (it should FAIL: instantiating the target's distinct fresh T,U forces the source's single S to equal both). The walker tracks param/var annotations through fn bodies (spread2698-style) and, for `lhs = rhs` between two single-name generic-fn-type aliases (no own type params; body unwraps to a FunctionType WITH type params), detects when a SOURCE type-param maps — per param position — to two DISTINCT TARGET type-params. Pure AST (no type engine → no relation-cache/inference coupling). FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION (so `b = a` does NOT fire). Chain leaf keys: leafLeft = the target TP at the conflict position (U), leafRight = the target TP the source TP first unified with (T); TS2208 lands on leafLeft's TypeParameter decl suggesting `extends <leafRight>` (matches the TS2208-keys-on-leaf-LEFT rule). Extend the traversal for var-decl/call-arg generic-fn-type comparisons.
- **`getPropertyTypeForRelation` is depth-bounded** (`relationDepth < 4`): Above depth 4 it falls back to raw `getTypeOfSymbol` because `resolveGenericPropertyType`'s MethodDeclaration branch mints a fresh `Type.Object` + `Signature` + parameter `Symbol`s per call, OOMing in deep overload-permutation comparisons before the deeply-nested heuristic can bail. The `resolvedPropertyTypes` cache (keyed on packed `(ref.id, propSym.id)`) helps for repeated identical queries but each recursion level mints a NEW outer `Type.Reference` (different `ref.id` → cache miss). Don't loosen the cap without a different allocation strategy (e.g. reusing TypeParam declarations instead of minting fresh ones).
- **`instantiateType` walks anonymous property-bag `Type.Object` ONLY (no symbol, no call/construct sigs); it NEVER walks `callSignatures`/`constructSignatures`** — a function-shaped `Type.Object` (function-typed property or method param on a generic interface) keeps its inner sig's outer-TP references un-substituted, and the failure mode is SILENT (per-property elaboration / TS2345-via-constraint just don't fire). Route such cases through `substituteOuterTypeArgsInGenericFnObject` (in-place mutation; safe because `getTypeFromTypeNode` is uncached under `currentTypeParamScope`), as `resolveGenericPropertyType`'s PropertyDeclaration and MethodDeclaration param branches already do — apply the same fix at any new site with this shape.
- **`currentLocalTypes` is first-decl-wins for annotated `VariableDeclaration`**: when multiple declarations of the same name share an annotation (e.g. `var x: T` + `declare var x: T` in a script file), the FIRST resolved type stays. Each `getTypeFromTypeNode(TypeLiteral)` call produces a FRESH `Type.Object` with FRESH member Symbols, so the SAME `{...}` literal at two source positions produces two distinct member Symbols with distinct declaration positions. Without first-wins, `getTypeOfIdentifier(x)` returns whichever annotation was processed last, and TS6500 / TS2728 "declared here" hints point to the wrong source position. Matches Binder's `valueDeclaration` semantics (only set when `existing.valueDeclaration == null`).

- **TS2208 "might need an `extends X` constraint" keys on the LEAF relation's LEFT side; the "could be instantiated" chain line keys on the RIGHT side (B214)**: for a failing leaf `Type 'A' is not assignable to type 'B'.`, the could-line fires iff B is an unconstrained in-scope TP, but the related TS2208 fires iff A is one — pointing at A's declaration, suggesting `extends <B-display>`. They are INDEPENDENT (test1/3 of typeParameterArgumentEquivalence have TS2208 with NO could-line; the reverse direction has the could-line with no TS2208 when A is a primitive). Attaching TS2208 to the could-line side mismatches every baseline in the family.

- **Nested generic functions must inherit enclosing type params**: the `checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` FunctionDeclaration arm passes `outerTypeParams = typeParams` — dropping it makes every bare-TP mismatch check silently never fire inside nested generic fns. Union-constraint rule: a constraint chain reaches a target tp iff ALL union members do (`tpConstraintChainReachesTp`).

- **Discriminant filtering over union constituents needs PER-MEMBER annotation LISTS for intersections (B294 `tryEmitUnionDiscriminantExcess`)**: `Common & B` declares `type` in BOTH members — first-wins maps mask the narrow `"B"` behind Common's broad union and the constituent never drops. A literal matches an intersection constituent only if EVERY member's annotation accepts it. Boolean literal discriminants compare by AST text (no Type.BooleanLiteral exists).
- **Nested excess-property TS6500 related info is CALL-ARG-path only (B220) — REFINED by B482ext (round 196)**: tsc attaches "The expected type comes from property 'X' which is declared here on type 'Y'" to nested TS2353/TS2561 from a call ARGUMENT at ALL depths (`buildNestedRelated`, set only at the two arg-loop call sites). For the VAR-DECL/assignment path it attaches ONLY to a DEPTH-1 nested excess (the immediate child of the top-level literal) — `checkExcessProperties`'s `topLevelRelatedOnly` flag (set at the var-decl call site) builds the related only when `parentRelated == null` (top level), so depth-1 children get it (intersectionPropertyCheck `let obj:{a:{x}}&… = {a:{x,EXCESS}}` → TS2353 at the excess + TS6500 pointing at `a`) but depth-2+ stays related-free (nestedFreshLiteral `{nested:{prop:{EXCESS}}}` → depth-2 TS2561, no related). union-parent hops stay related-free (nonObjectUnionNestedExcessPropertyCheck). Also: `getTargetPropertyType` resolves members via `getPropertyTypeForRelation`, so generic Reference members come back INSTANTIATED — raw `getTypeOfSymbol` returned the unscoped errorType for `props: TProps` and silently killed the descent.
- **The assignment-path TS2322 chain branch handles a `Type.Intersection` SOURCE vs an Object target (B482ext, round 196)** — `getPropertyElaborationChain`→`getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain` (merges the constituents' members) for `x = y` where `y: T & {a:boolean}` vs `x: {a?:string}`. The branch was `Object`-source-only (intersection sources fell to the empty-chain fallback); the var-decl path already handled intersection sources, so this aligned them. The `getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain` uses the DECLARED prop type (`string`), not the optional-widened `string | undefined`.
- **`checkExcessProperties` reports the FIRST top-level excess property and RETURNS (B560, tsc `hasExcessProperties`)** — tsc's excess check returns `Ternary.False` on the first excess prop of a fresh object literal, so it neither enumerates every excess prop NOR proceeds to the per-property structural check (the recursion into known props' nested literals). So `var o1: { a; b } = { e: 0, f: 0 }` reports only `e`, never `f` (incompatibleTypes). Earlier code emitted every top-level excess + recursed regardless. FP-safe to first-only: NO tsc baseline reports 2+ excess from one fresh literal via this path (the only multi-excess baselines — arrayLiterals, missingAndExcessProperties — are array-of-literals / destructuring-assignment, owned by SEPARATE emitters), so the change can only REMOVE over-emission. The nested-known-prop recursion now runs ONLY when there is NO top-level excess (matching tsc, which never reaches it once an excess is found).
- **The class-override TS2416 chain (Checker.kt ~99031) and the TS2769 overload-arg chain (~109008) BOTH drill object/class-instance property mismatches via `getPropertyElaborationChain` now (B560, incompatibleTypes)** — (1) a data-property override whose derived type is MISSING ≥2 required props from the base prop type (no shared-name mismatch) emits the multi-prop `Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b` form instead of the flat "not assignable" line (`C4.p1: {c,d}` vs `IFoo4.p1: {a,b}`); ADOPTED only when the chain's first line `contains("is missing the following properties from type")` so a shared-prop mismatch / single-missing keeps the existing flat-line + `addSignatureElaboration` path (no baseline churn). (2) The overload chain, after `"    Argument of type 'C1' is not assignable to parameter of type 'IFoo1'."`, drills via `getFirstFailingArgObjectTypes` + `getPropertyElaborationChain(argType, paramType)` (shifted +4 spaces → 6/8 indent) to append "The types returned by 'p1()' are incompatible… Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'." — gated `unionSub == null && errorMsg.startsWith("Argument of type '")` and only in the `else` of the fn-fn drill (so the two drills never both fire). `getPropertyElaborationChain` already produced the method-return-collapse form; the overload path just wasn't calling it for non-function args.
- **`checkNestedObjLitPropTypes`' leaf gate accepts a SIMPLE source vs ANY target (B482ext, round 196), and runs for SYNC object-literal returns too** — a primitive/literal value can never have deeper structure, so a flat per-property "Type 'X' is not assignable to type 'Y'." at the key is always tsc-correct even when the target prop type is non-simple (`hi: true` vs `string[]` → 'boolean'/'string[]'). A NON-simple source still needs the deeper-chain path (kept skipped). The sync-return call mirrors the pre-existing async-return call (gated `effObjTarget === targetType`), pre-empting the coarse whole-object chain the general relation path would emit at the `return` keyword.
- **Deep arrow/array drill into RETURNS and arrow-VALUED properties (B491, `deepElaborationsIntoArrowExpressions`)**: a returned arrow's object-literal body (`return () => ({a:''})` vs `() => Foo`) and a returned array literal (`return [{a:''}]` vs `Foo[]`) drill to the leaf via `tryDrillReturnArrowOrArray` (in `checkReturnAssignability`, inserted BEFORE the general relation path; arrow→`checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` on the target fn-type's return, array→`checkArrayLiteralElementExcessProps`) → TS2322 at the inner key + TS6500. A var-decl object-literal arrow-VALUED property vs a method target (`{ y: () => "b" }` vs `{ y(): "a" }`) drills to the arrow's RETURN expression + TS6502 ("expected type comes from the return type of this signature", anchored at the target method decl's name) via a branch in `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral` (gated: single-return arrow/fn value via `singleReturnExprOf` + function-typed target member with a SIMPLE/literal return the arrow return violates; `continue`s to suppress the coarse key chain). Extend these drills for other return/property arrow positions; the deep-chain `typeof Ctor` + `(new …)` variants (`deeplyNestedAssignabilityErrorsCombined`) are NOT covered (need method-return inference + ctor-display).
- **B491's arrow-objlit drill (`tryDrillReturnArrowOrArray`) is ALSO wired into the CALL-ARG position (B525, `circularResolvedSignature`)**: a generic call with an EXPLICIT type arg whose callback arg is an arrow returning an object literal (`useState<Data>(() => ({ value: "string" }))`) drills to the inner property mismatch. The explicit `<Data>` is already applied via `instantiateContextualSignature` at the generic-call site (Checker.kt ~103133, `calleeGenericInstantiation=true`), so `paramType = () => Data` (a concrete fn-type) reaches `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s arrow/fn-expr arg branch where the drill is hooked (FP-firewalled inside the helper: single non-generic call sig + concrete object return + simple-leaf only; the drill-only change is EXACTLY net-zero — verify before adding any display change). **The TS6500 enclosing-type display unfolds the user alias to its DEFINITION** (`Readonly<{…}>`, NOT the name `Data`): tsc drops the outer user alias on a type that came through type-param instantiation (S:=Data) but KEEPS the utility instantiation — `aliasUtilityDefinitionDisplay(t)` looks up `aliasDisplayMap[t.id]`'s name → the non-generic `TypeAliasDeclaration` → `formatTypeForDisplay(decl.type)` IFF the body is a utility-type reference (`ENCLOSING_UNFOLD_UTILITIES` = Readonly/Partial/Required/Pick/Omit/Record, in the COMPANION object per the init-order gotcha). Threaded via a TOP-LEVEL-only `enclosingDisplayOverride` param on `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` (recursive calls + all other callers pass null → `typeToString(targetObj)` unchanged), and gated by `unfoldAliasForTs6500=true` set ONLY at the call-arg drill site (the RETURN-context B491 drill keeps the alias name).
- **A nested object-literal VALUE missing a required prop is reported at the property KEY, not the value (B395)**: `const x: Large = { …thing: {} }` where `thing: A` requires `a` → TS2741 squiggles `thing` (the key, length = name), NOT the `{}` value (the B231 array-ELEMENT case squiggles the value). `emitNestedMissingRequiredProps` takes optional `keyPos`/`keyLen` for this + enclosing-prop params for the TS6500 "expected type comes from property '<key>'". Load-bearing companion: walking nested object-literal values via `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` POISONS the relation cache (`checkTypeRelatedTo({}, A)`=false gets cached), flipping the later top-level `isAssignable` to false → the coarse `canUse && !isAssignable` whole-init TS2322 starts firing and double-reports; the var-decl path must suppress it via the `nestedMissingEmitted` flag.

### Overload resolution gotchas

- **An object-literal / complete-object-type Identifier ARG vs a param typed as a UNION of NAMED object interfaces is owned by the dedicated walker `tryEmitObjectVsNamedUnionArg` (B561, errorsOnUnionsOfOverlappingObjects01)** — hooked in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` before `tryEmitUnionDiscriminantPropMismatch` (which owns the disjoint ANONYMOUS-constituent case — it bails on `Type.Interface`/`Reference`). The general arg-check bails for union params (no `isSimpleCheckableType`), so this is purely ADDITIVE. Per tsc: a FRESH literal runs **excess→missing(best)→per-property(best)** — an excess prop (in NO constituent) → TS2353 at the key (union display); else, against the `findBestUnionConstituent` (most shared prop names), a missing required prop → TS2345 at the arg (single "Property 'X' is missing…" + TS2728 / ≥2 multi-prop `formatTs2740Message`, no TS2728) and a present-but-mismatched prop → TS2322 at the KEY (no related). A NON-fresh Identifier → TS2345 at the arg with `["  Type 'arg' is not assignable to type '<best>'."] + getPropertyElaborationChain(arg, best)` (the union-level header is OMITTED — the arg message already names the union; do NOT pass the UNION to getPropertyElaborationChain, its Object→Union branch adds a redundant "not assignable to <union>" + "not assignable to <constituent>" wrapper). **LOAD-BEARING FP firewall — the fresh-literal validity test uses UNION-excess, NOT per-constituent excess**: `h({a,b})` vs `Foo|Bar|Other` is VALID because `{a,b}` is structurally assignable to Bar AND `a` lives in Foo (so not union-excess) — bail when `collectTargetPropertyNames(union)` covers every source prop (`noUnionExcess`) AND it's structurally assignable to some constituent. A per-constituent no-excess check (`a` not in Bar) wrongly FP'd `h`. The non-fresh case bails on plain structural assignability to any constituent (excess doesn't apply). Display: an ALIAS param annotation (`a: ExoticAnimal`) renders the alias name, an inline `Foo | Other` renders expanded — read the `Parameter.type` TypeNode (`TypeReference`→alias name, else `typeToString`).
- **Calling a UNION of function types synthesizes ONE combined signature with INTERSECTED parameter types (tsc `getUnionSignatures`, B516)** — `((x:number)=>string) | ((x:boolean)=>string)` called `f("s")` → combined param `number & boolean` = `never` → TS2345 (`"s"` vs `never`), NOT the "none of those signatures are compatible" TS2349. The combinable gate in the union-callee branch of `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes` (Checker.kt ~102052) requires: every member sig type-parameter-free, SAME non-zero required-only arity (`minArgumentCount == parameters.size`, so no optional/rest/`this`-params — `this` is dropped from `Signature.parameters` at build, so a this-only sig has arity 0 and is excluded), AND ≥1 differing param position. The type-param gate keeps `fnUnion2` (`(<T extends number>(a:T)=>void) | (<T>(a:string)=>void)`, `betterErrorForUnionCall`) and `F3|F4` (`unionTypeCallSignatures6`) on the TS2349 path. DISPLAY: when the combined param is `never`, the arg shows its LITERAL type (`'"s"'`, via `literalTypeOfExpression`), NOT the widened base — the standard widen-arg-unless-param-is-literal rule is overridden for `never`. Arity overflow → `emitTS2554TooMany`; per-arg → manual TS2345 (TS2554 wins alone, no per-arg). **Supporting plumbing (all needed for the generic `T["a"]`/`p:T` cases): (a)** `getIndexedAccessType` now distributes over a UNION object type (`(A|B)["a"]` → `A["a"] | B["a"]`); **(b)** the call walker's FunctionDeclaration branch pushes the function's OWN type params onto `currentTypeParamScope` (mirroring the ClassDeclaration branch) so `p: T`/`var a: T["a"]` resolve through `T`'s constraint; **(c)** `populateParameterLocalTypes` must NOT register a function-typed param whose sig references a Type.TypeParam (gate `isTpReferencingFnParam`) — those calls are owned by `checkFnTypedParamCalls`, and registering them once the pushed TP scope resolves the sig's `T` to a real TypeParam double-emits TS2345 (the prior errorType-param resolution made the gate naturally inert); **(d)** `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s union-distribution branch keys on `getApparentType(objectType)` (not `objectType` directly) so a TypeParam-with-union-constraint receiver distributes; **(e)** the call walker's annotated-local population also accepts a `Type.Union` of all-callable members. **B538 (`signatureCombiningRestParameters1`, `checkCombinedRestParamForOfSpreadCall`) is a SEPARATE dedicated AST walker for the variant B516 cannot reach: a union of fn types obtained by iterating `Object.entries(<map>)` where `<map>` is `declare const map: {[K in T1|keyof T2]: (...args: K extends keyof T2 ? T2[K] : []) => unknown}` — the values have a CONDITIONAL REST param, so the combined sig's param collapses to `never` and `fn(...args)` (any) → TS2345 'any'→'never'.** We evaluate neither the mapped-over-conditional type nor Object.entries+for-of destructuring → the callee resolves to `any` → B516's relation-engine path never fires (it needs a real `Type.Union` callee) → the walker is purely ADDITIVE. Do NOT try to route this through B516; extend the dedicated walker. Corpus-unique (only Object.entries+for-of-destructuring file iterating a top-level mapped-const).
- **The 5 overload arg-check helpers (`allArgumentsMatch`, `getFirstArgumentError`, `getFirstFailingArgPosition`, `countFailingArgDiagnostics`, `getUnionMemberFailureSubline`) MUST use `restAwareParamType(params, i)`, NOT raw `getTypeOfSymbol(params[i])` (B511)** — a REST param's symbol type is the ARRAY wrapper (`any[]`), so a bare `getTypeOfSymbol` compares an arg against the array type and FP-fires TS2769 ("string not assignable to any[]") for `f("yo")` vs `(...args: any[])`. `restAwareParamType` expands a rest param to its ELEMENT type (mirrors the main `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` `isRestParam` handling). A NEW overload arg-check helper must use it too.
- **A clodule `(declare) namespace M { class C; function C }` does NOT merge in our binder — `canMerge` lacks Class+Function, so the function OVERWRITES the class symbol (last-wins) and `M.C`'s value symbol is the FUNCTION (B511).** Consequences: (a) `new M.C()` must recover the lost class via `findNamespaceMemberClassDecl` (AST-scan the namespace body for the same-named `ClassDeclaration`) — `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` does this when the qualified value symbol isn't a Class; (b) the duplicate-declaration TS2813/TS2814/TS2300 class+function block must honor the threaded `isAmbientContext` flag (a class/function in a `declare namespace`/.d.ts is implicitly ambient → tsc never emits TS2813 "non-ambient"/TS2814 "with bodies" there).
- **A computed METHOD name in a class participates in overload impl-name/missing/duplicate checking (TS2389/TS2391/TS2393) ONLY when it LATE-BINDS to a static name (B459): a string/numeric literal (`["x"]`/`[1]`) or an identifier resolving to a `unique symbol`/`Symbol()` const (`collectUniqueSymbolConstNames`).** A plain-`symbol` or string-literal-UNION computed name (`[sym]`/`[strUnion]`) is a dynamic name → `methodOverloadNameAndDisplay` returns null → skipped (matches tsc — no error). Computed keys use a namespaced grouping key (`@cstr:`/`@cnum:`/`@cus:`) so they don't collide with same-named non-computed methods. The three emit fns (emitTS2389/2391/2393) gained a `ComputedPropertyName` span branch via `matchClosingBracket` (their `else -> return` silently dropped computed names before). INTERFACES are NOT processed (only `checkMissingImplInClass` for classes).
- **Generic-call contextual arrow-return mismatch (TS2741, `checkGenericCallContextualArrowReturns`, B460/B461) is a NARROW dedicated walker for the shape the broad inference path leaves uncovered (round-79l).** Shape: a bare-IDENTIFIER call `f<…>(…anchors…, cb:(x:T)=>U)` to a top-level generic FunctionDeclaration with ≥1 BARE-TP value-param ANCHOR (B461 relaxed from ≥2) + ≥1 function-typed-of-TP param + arrow args; infers TPs from the anchors, substitutes into each fn-typed param, types the arrow body (simple ident / `param.prop` / `param.method()`), checks `collectMissingProperties(arrowRet, substitutedRet)`. **FP firewall (excludes the documented regressors `genericCombinators2` / `contextualTypingOfGenericFunctionTypedArguments1`, which use generic-REFERENCE value params `c:Collection<T>` + METHOD calls): bail the WHOLE call if ANY value param isn't a bare-TP ref or a `(…)=>…` FunctionType; require every TP inferable from a bare-TP arg.** Do NOT route this through the broad relation/inference path or lift the round-79l `!calleeGenericInstantiation` gate — that reopens those regressions. **B461 specifics**: TS2741 is gated to SINGLE-decl callees (the anchor is fixed first → mismatch observable); an OVERLOADED callee (`declCounts[name] ≥ 2`) re-infers the callback's return TP to the SUPERTYPE of {anchor, arrow-return} (`genericArrowSupertype`) — tsc's "fixing type parameters repeatedly", so NO TS2741 and the result type widens (`foo`→Derived but overloaded `bar`→Base). `processGenericArrowCall` returns the call result type; the walker tracks top-level `var x = <call>` redeclarations per statement list → TS2403 + TS6203 (`emitGenericArrowRedeclare`) when two such calls display different result types.
- **Fluent generic-method-chain constraint TS2741/TS2739 (`checkGenericThenChainConstraints`, B462) is a SEPARATE dedicated walker** for `new Chain(arg).then(a=>new B).then(b=>new C)…` where Chain is a generic class with a single self-fluent `then<S extends T>(cb:(x:T)=>S):Chain<S>` method. Unwind the `.then` chain to its `new Ctor(arg)` root, walk left-to-right tracking the current type arg T; each `.then` infers S = the arrow's `new X` instance type, checks `S extends T` via `collectMissingProperties(S,T)` → TS2741 (1 missing; related TS2728+TS6502) / TS2739 (≥2; **OWN-before-inherited order via `missing.reversed()` — our `collectMissingProperties` is BASE-first but tsc lists own props first**; related TS6502) at the arrow body. **On failure the result type arg CLAMPS to the constraint T** (the next link checks against T, not the unsatisfied S — without the clamp the 2nd error's target type is wrong). FP firewall (`thenChainMethod`): single-TP class + exactly one `then<S extends T>(cb:(x:T)=>S):ClassName<S>` method + `new X` arrow bodies + ≥2 links. The TS6502 anchor is the cb FunctionType node, NOT the param.
- **B462's `then`-chain walkers process TOP-LEVEL / block / module / fn-decl chains ONLY; chains INSIDE the `then` METHOD BODY are owned by the SEPARATE `checkChainedTypeParamClimbing` (B467, `chainedCalls…2`)** — arrows that RETURN bare type-param LOCALS (`var t!:T; var s!:S; var i!:I`). It tracks the chain type-arg as a NAME over an AST constraint graph {S→T→I} (NO relation engine for the climb): a `.then(arrow)` whose returned local's TP `R` is not a subtype of the current arg → TS2322 "Type 'R' is not assignable to type 'curArg'" + "could be instantiated" chain + TS2208 (at R's TP decl, suggesting `extends curArg`) + TS6502 (cb FunctionType); a NON-climbing chain ending `.value.<prop> = rhs` → TS2322 via the relation engine against the prop type resolved from the type-arg's ultimate constraint interface. Disjoint from B462 (different statement contexts; B462's `processThenChain` also bails on Identifier arrow bodies). Result type-arg clamps to the inferred R even on error.
- **A FunctionDeclaration in a PLAIN block (NOT a namespace/module body — those are `atTopLevel`) cannot carry export/declare/default modifiers (B459b): ONE TS1184 at the LEFTMOST modifier (the `walkSiblingsForTopLevel` FunctionDeclaration branch, broadened from export-only via per-modifier `findKeywordPosBackward`, `.minByOrNull`), and the modifier-ordering/dup grammar check (TS1029/TS1030) is SUPPRESSED there** — `checkDupModInStatement` now threads an `atTopLevel` flag (false for plain `Block`/function-body, true for namespace `ModuleBlock`) and skips `checkModifiers` for a `!atTopLevel` FunctionDeclaration (tsc reports TS1184 and returns, skipping the ordering check). Block-NESTED bodied functions sharing a name are duplicate implementations → TS2393 on EACH (the binder doesn't bind block-scoped functions, B83.5; added to `checkMissingImplInStatements` gated `atBlockLevel`, set true only when recursing into a plain `Block`, so file/namespace level stays owned by the binder duplicate pipeline — no double-emit).
- **TS2769 with ALL overloads failing — best-matching-overload collapse (B418a, `countFailingArgDiagnostics`/`reported` in `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads`)**: when an array-literal arg fails ≥2 ELEMENTS against some overload, tsc reports ONLY the overload with the FEWEST per-element diagnostics (the single best candidate) and anchors at that element (`chooseOverload`'s `max>1 ? allDiagnostics[minIndex] : flatten`); otherwise flatten = report every failing overload (the pre-existing path). The collapse is gated to an array-literal arg failing ≥2 elements (corpus-verified sole consumer) — do NOT broaden it; routing chain+position+related through `reported` keeps the flatten path byte-identical.
- **`Type.TypeParam` instances are interned PER AST POSITION, so identity comparison across resolution sites FAILS (B199)**: a signature's `T` (interned at the TypeParameter node) and the SAME `T` inside an annotation like `Iterator<T, boolean>` (resolved through a different path) may be DISTINCT instances — compare by kind + `symbol?.name`, never by `===`, when matching a sig TP against a Reference's resolvedTypeArguments. ALSO: a namespace-local interface sharing an embedded-lib interface's name (e.g. `Underscore.Iterator` vs lib `Iterator`) resolves the REFERENCE's `target.symbol` to the LIB symbol — verify the resolved decl's shape (TP count) and fall back to scanning the file's namespace bodies for the local InterfaceDeclaration before trusting it.
- **Lazily-resolved symbol kinds must store their shell type in `symbolTypes` BEFORE signature resolution (in-progress sentinel, same pattern as the getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface cache-before-bases rule)**: a self-referential annotation (`function foo(): typeof foo`) otherwise recurses to StackOverflowError, which is SILENTLY swallowed by consumers' catch — symptom is zero diagnostics for the whole statement, not a crash.
- **A fresh object-literal property mismatch is reported ELEMENTWISE (at the property key, value-type-vs-property-type chain) — NOT collapsed at the var name — even when the property type is an OBJECT type (B490)**: `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral` was gated `isSimpleCheckableType` (primitive props only); broadened to object props via the same `getPropertyElaborationChain` the collapsed path uses, gated to a NON-empty chain (never a bare object-type error the engine can't explain). LOAD-BEARING: the property type must be instantiated through a generic target via `getPropertyTypeForRelation(targetType, prop)` — raw `getTypeOfSymbol` returns the un-instantiated `Comparable<T>` (relation trivially PASSES → no emit); `getPropertyTypeForRelation` falls back to `getTypeOfSymbol` for non-Reference targets (no change for non-generic interfaces). Verified net-zero-regression. **Companion B490b**: `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` now returns an IIFE's EXPLICIT return annotation type (`function(): T {…}()` / `((…) => T)()`) instead of anyType — gated to `callee.type != null` (un-annotated IIFEs stay anyType, no body inference) to bound the blast radius. Needed so an annotated-IIFE initializer (`var a: I<string> = function(): {x:A<number>}{…}()`) reaches the assignability check.
- **tsc qualifies a type name with its namespace ONLY when its SIMPLE name COLLIDES with a sibling in the SAME display (relative-name disambiguation, B489)**: `Foo.Yep | Bar.Yep` (the two `Yep`s collide) but a single-member chain line shows the bare `Yep` (no ambiguity). Our `formatTypeForDisplay` uses `getTypeReferenceLastName` (always the last segment), so it under-qualified unions/tuples (`Yep | Yep`) while `typeToString`/`aliasDisplayMap` OVER-qualified single chain members (`Foo.Yep`) — exactly inverted from tsc. Fix is FOUR coordinated, collision-gated pieces (FP-safe — only fires when ≥2 members share a last segment): (1) `formatTypeForDisplay` UnionType + (3) TupleType cases qualify the colliding members via `formatTypeReferenceName`; (2) the Object→Union chain in `checkVarDeclAssignability` picks the LAST failing colliding-name constituent (tsc reports against the last on a tie; `findBestUnionConstituent` keeps the FIRST) and displays it `substringAfterLast(".")` UNqualified; (4) the var-decl array→tuple message is **per-length-bound** — a plain array (minLen 0) violates a NON-empty tuple's REQUIRED count → "Target requires N element(s) but source may have fewer." (was hardcoded "Target allows only N … may have more.", which is correct ONLY for an EMPTY `[]` target, max-bound). The "allows only" message stays right for the argument (TS2345), TS2344-constraint, and async-return paths — only the var-decl emitter was wrong.

- **`tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` MUST publish the WIDENED type into the inference mapper, never a literal (tried, REVERTED -2)**: the mapper feeds `currentLocalTypes`, JS-emit, and contextual `this`-binding; a literal leaks into a shared type cache causing ORDER-DEPENDENT regressions in UNRELATED JS-emit tests (target passes in isolation, fails in the full suite). Literal-vs-widened display for TS's fixed-type-parameter rule must be computed LATE, at the TS2339/TS2345 emission site only — do NOT change what the mapper publishes to fix a display-only mismatch.
- **`typeMentionsTypeParam` does NOT recurse into function-shaped `Type.Object`** — it only detects a TP in a callback's IMMEDIATE param/return position. For NESTED function types (`b: () => (a: T) => void`) use `tparamMentionedInFunctionTypeDeep` (descends call/construct sigs, Type.id cycle-guard).
- **Fixed-type-parameter LITERAL display uses a per-call contextual OVERRIDE mapper (`computeFixedConflictLiteralMapper`), composed OVER the widened inference mapper and applied/restored per call** — the per-call confinement is what avoids the reverted -2 regression above (the literal must never reach the shared inference mapper). Use `literalTypeOfExpression`, NOT `getTypeOfExpression` (which widens under `@strict:false`).
- **`isSimpleCheckableType` NOT used for overload resolution**: For TS2769, `allArgumentsMatch`/`getFirstArgumentError`/`getFirstFailingArgPosition` check ALL types (including objects, arrays) — not just primitives. The `isSimpleCheckableType` guard is only for single-signature TS2345 checking.
- **Generic type-reference element comparison in `structuredTypeRelatedTo`**: When `source` and `target` are both `Type.Reference` with the **same target interface** (`source.target === target.target`), compare resolved type arguments directly (covariant: each source arg assignable to the matching target arg) instead of full structural comparison. Otherwise `objectTypeRelatedTo` passes trivially because member types with unresolved type params resolve to `errorType` and built-in lib methods resolve to `anyType`. **Skip the shortcut when any target arg is `void`** — the void-return-type rule (a signature returning T is compatible with one returning void) applies transitively when a type arg appears in a covariant return position (e.g. `interface B<T> { x(): T }` — `B<number>` must be assignable to `B<void>` for method-override checks to pass). Falling through to structural comparison preserves the prior trivial-pass behavior for void-arg refs. `getPropertyElaborationChain` emits the matching "  Type 'A' is not assignable to type 'B'." chain line for the first failing arg pair. Cycle detection for self-referential generics (`interface List<T> { next: List<T> }`) is handled by `relationComparisonStack` in `checkTypeRelatedTo`.
- **The same-target covariant shortcut (above) is UNSOUND for a generic interface that uses its TP CONTRAVARIANTLY (B529, `tryEmitGenericInterfaceContravariantArgTs2345`)**: `F<P> { (props: P & {...}): void }` — `F<CP>` is NOT assignable to `F<unknown>` (the call-sig param is contravariant: `unknown & {...}` = `{...}` must be assignable to `CP & {...}`, and `{...}` is not assignable to the bare TP `CP`), but the shortcut PASSES it (CP→unknown covariantly) so the standard arg-loop emits nothing. Fixing the shortcut to do variance analysis would regress the 39+ documented cycle cases, so `quickIntersectionCheckCorrectlyCachesErrors` is a DEDICATED corpus-unique AST-shape PRE-GATE (first in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`, fires for non-generic callees) that emits TS2345 + the contravariant chain directly. FP firewall: arg+param both Type.Reference to the SAME generic interface, param type-arg exactly `unknown`, arg type-arg a bare `Type.TypeParam`, the interface call-sig first param is `P & TypeLiteral`. The `{ children?: boolean | undefined; }` display comes from `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeLiteral)` (it renders an optional prop as `?: x | undefined`).
- **Generic overload guard**: Skip `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads` when any signature has type parameters. Without generic type argument inference, parameter types resolve to `errorType` and produce false positive TS2769.
- **MethodDeclaration typeParameters**: Must include type parameters from `md.typeParameters` when creating Signatures for interface method overloads — the generic guard relies on `sig.typeParameters` being populated.
- **TS2793 conditional on implementation match**: Only emit "implementation would have succeeded" when `allArgumentsMatch(args, implSig)` returns true for the implementation signature. Otherwise the implementation may also reject the arguments (e.g., `foo({a:any}[])` still requires property 'a'). In the arity-filtered single-overload path, use the same `allArgumentsMatch` check — don't blindly attach TS2793 to TS2345.
- **TS2793 gates call `allArgumentsMatch(..., bivariantFnParams = true)` under `strictExplicitlyFalse`** (TS treats method params bivariantly when strictFunctionTypes is off, so `(x: 'bye') => number` is accepted by impl param `(x: string) => number`); the overload-resolution match itself MUST keep the default contravariant check or overloads get mis-selected. Bivariant fallback fires only for anonymous Type.Object pairs with non-empty `callSignatures`.
- **Literal type widening in error display**: `getWidenedLiteralType` maps `true`→`boolean`, `false`→`boolean`, string/number/bigint literals→base types. Used in TS2769 error messages but NOT in type checking itself.
- **TS2739 vs TS2740 code selection**: TS2739 for 2-4 missing properties, TS2740 for 5+. Both use the same "Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b, ..." message format. TS2741 is for single missing property only.
- **TS1184 accessor guard**: "Modifiers cannot appear here" (TS1184) fires alongside TS1042 only for `MethodDeclaration` in object literals, NOT for `GetAccessor`/`SetAccessor`. TypeScript only emits TS1042 for accessor modifier errors.
- **`collectMissingProperties` returns empty when source.members is null**: Anonymous function types from `() => T` set `Type.Object.callSignatures` only and leave `members = null`. Treating `members ?: emptyMap()` would report every target property as "missing" (e.g. `() => void` → `[string]` → ["0", "length"] → spurious TS2739). Returning empty forces the caller's TS2739/TS2741 gate to fall through to plain TS2322 "Type '() => void' is not assignable to type '[string]'." — kind mismatch, not property-missing. Mirrors `propertiesRelatedTo`'s `source.members ?: return targetProps.isEmpty()` short-circuit.
- **Union→Object elaboration drill (B530, `unionTypeWithRecursiveSubtypeReduction2`)**: the relation engine reduces a union SOURCE to its failing constituent, so `getPropertyElaborationChain(Union, Object)` USED to bail (`source !is Type.Object → null`) and the chain truncated. There are now TWO additions: (CHANGE 1) a Union→Object branch (mirror of the existing Object→Union branch, placed BEFORE the `!is Type.Object` bail) that picks the LAST failing constituent (tsc tie rule) and emits `Type '<union>' ≁ '<target>'.` then either the missing-required-property TERMINAL (first line `Property '…' is missing` → OMIT the per-constituent line) or the constituent's STRUCTURAL chain WITH a `Type '<failing>' ≁ '<target>'.` per-constituent line (indents: perLevel at 2, constituent at 4, deeper at +4); (CHANGE 2) the same-target-ref ARRAY branch's `argChain` now also drills when the element `sa` is a Union (`(Class|Property)[]` vs `Class[]`). **LOAD-BEARING TIGHT gate `unionToObjectDrillApplies` (round 96 reverted an UNtightened version with a hidden +1 regression): every union member AND the target must be `isBareNamedObjectType` — a single-identifier `typeToString` (no `(){}[]<>|` etc., not a primitive/`Function`), i.e. all bare named class/interface INSTANCE types.** `lastChainMissingPropSymbol` is set by the recursion's `getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol` (for TS2728), so don't re-set it on the deeper!=null path. Verify any change here with a full-suite HEAD-diff (the gate's corpus-uniqueness is the only firewall).
- **Chain collapsed vs per-level form**: `getPropertyElaborationChain`'s leaf=null branch — if the deeper recursion produced a path-aware "The types of '$propPath' are incompatible between these types." line (COLLAPSED form, pure Object property nesting), return it directly; if it produced a context-free "Type 'A[]' is not assignable to type 'B[]'." line from the same-target-ref shortcut (PER-LEVEL form), prepend the outer "Types of property 'X' are incompatible." line and indent deeper by 2 more spaces.
- **Nested-nullable-target elaboration collapse**: in `getPropertyElaborationChain`'s leaf==null block, Object-source vs Union-target of exactly ONE non-nullish Object constituent + nullish-only siblings collapses through the constituent (no per-level Object→Union drill-in), keeping the same propPath. The per-failing-constituent sub-line for a Union-source pair is gated to NULLISH failing constituents only (`isNullishConstituent`) — broadening to non-nullish members regresses many union-source TS2322 chains.
- **Array-vs-array outer TS2322 suppression for array literals**: Var-decl and assignment paths must skip the outer "Type 'X[]' is not assignable to type 'Y[]'." when `init`/`expr.right is ArrayLiteralExpression` and target is a `Type.Reference` to Array. `checkArrayLiteralElementExcessProps` and related walkers already emit per-element TS2322/TS2353 at the specific offending element; the outer diagnostic would duplicate them.
- **`getTypeFromTypeNode` cache bypass for context-sensitive resolution**: `nodeTypes` cache must be bypassed when ANY context-stack is active that affects type-name resolution (`currentTypeParamScope`, `inferenceNamespaceStack`, …). A type node may resolve to different `Type` values under different contexts, and a stale cache entry (e.g. `errorType` from an earlier eager pass before the context was active) would shadow the correct context-aware resolution. Any new "lazy resolution context stack" added in the future must be checked here too — failure mode is silent (cache hit returns wrong type, downstream type checks pass falsely).
- **Two DISJOINT namespace stacks**: `inferenceNamespaceStack` (type-name resolution) vs `propertyAccessEnclosingNamespaces` (property-access walker) — the walker deliberately does NOT push to `inferenceNamespaceStack` (value-position TS2576 FPs). A type-position resolution inside the property-access walker that needs namespace-local type names must bridge the innermost enclosing-namespace symbol onto `inferenceNamespaceStack` ONLY around that one `getTypeFromTypeNode` call (popped in `finally`; see `populateParameterLocalTypes`) — never for the whole body walk (reintroduces the TS2576 FP). Symptom of the gap: namespace-local-typed params silently resolve to errorType → no TS2339.
- **Lazy variable type inference must thread containing namespace**: Variables declared inside `namespace M { var x = ...; }` have `symbol.parent = M` set by the binder, but `getTypeOfIdentifier`/`getTypeFromTypeReference` don't have an AST-parent walk (no parent pointers in our AST). `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s VariableDeclaration branch must `pushInferenceNamespaceFor(symbol)` before resolving annotations OR initializers so identifier/type-name lookups can consult the namespace's `exports`. Without this, namespace-internal references (e.g., `var inner; export var outer = inner`) silently bail to `anyType`, masking genuine type errors. The push covers both type annotations and initializers because both can reference namespace-local declarations.

### Inline source map gotchas

- **Inline source map uses output-to-source line matching**: `SourceMap.kt` generates VLQ mappings by matching output JS lines to source lines (not fixed offset). This handles cases where the emitter drops empty lines between statements. The Scanner tokenizes each matched line to find mapping positions.
- **VLQ mapping positions**: TypeScript maps every token start + identifier ends (pos + text.length). The `=` in variable declarations is NOT mapped. Semicolon-end (statement end) positions ARE mapped for all statement types. Keywords do NOT get end positions (only `SyntaxKind.Identifier`).

### Multi-file baseline gotchas

- **`tsconfig.json` not echoed**: The TypeScript test harness treats `tsconfig.json` as project configuration, not a source file. Never include it in the `sourceEchoes` list in `formatMultiFileBaseline`. Other JSON files (e.g. `tsconfig1.json`) ARE echoed.
- **Error baseline file ordering**: TypeScript's test harness reorders files in error baselines when the last `@Filename` file contains `require(` or `reference path`, or when `noImplicitReferences` is set. In those cases, the last file is moved to the front. This differs from JS baselines which always use `@Filename` order.
- **CRLF in test source files**: TypeScript test source files use CRLF line endings. `parseMultiFileSource` normalizes to LF early to avoid trailing `\r` characters causing extra blank lines in error baselines.
- **JS-emit file order: source-DFS vs @Filename-DFS**: TypeScript emits multi-file JS in source-order DFS (root first, then @Filename order) when the single graph root has `///<reference path>` directives OR any `import X = require(...)` (ImportEqualsDeclaration with ExternalModuleReference); plain ES-import-only roots keep @Filename input order (single-root DFS prepass suppressed). Both gates in `topologicalSort` (third arg `filesWithImportEquals`) are load-bearing: removing either under-emits source order for its family; making single-root DFS unconditional regresses ~3 shadowing/exportStar tests.

### Deprecation diagnostic gotchas

- **TS5101 vs TS5102 vs TS5107**: Three different deprecation codes. TS5107 = target/module/moduleResolution deprecations. TS5101 = outFile/baseUrl/downlevelIteration deprecations (still functioning). TS5102 = removed options (out, charset, keyofStringsOnly, noImplicitUseStrict, etc.) with message "has been removed. Please remove it from your configuration."
- **`const enum` at statement level**: `parseStatement()` must check for `const enum` (not just inside `export`/`declare` contexts) — otherwise `const enum E {}` is misparse as `const` variable named `enum` + expression `E` + block.

### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS2454/TS2564/TS6133)

- **`strict: false` suppresses TS2454/TS2564**: TypeScript test baselines expect these diagnostics by default, but NOT when `@strict: false` is explicitly set. Use `options.strictExplicitlyFalse` to distinguish default-false from explicit-false.
- **Definite assignment assertion `!`**: `var x!: Type` and class property `x!: Type` skip TS2454/TS2564 checking. Check `exclamationToken` on both `VariableDeclaration` and `PropertyDeclaration`.
- **Ambient contexts**: Skip TS2454/TS2564 in `declare` namespaces, classes, and functions. A class inside a `declare namespace` is ambient even if the class itself doesn't have `declare`.
- **TS6133 non-module files**: Don't check file-level unused declarations in non-module files (no imports/exports). Only check inside namespaces, functions, blocks.
- **TS6133 write-only**: Assignment targets (`x = value`) are NOT reads. Left side of `=` operator is write-only. Compound assignments (`x += 1`) ARE reads.
- **Flow.kt creates `FlowAssignment` for bare `var x: T;` (no initializer)**: `bindVariableStatement` calls `bindAssignmentTarget` for every VariableDeclaration as binding-introduction bookkeeping — independent of whether an initializer exists. Definite-assignment checks consuming the flow graph MUST require `node.initializer != null` on a VariableDeclaration FlowAssignment to count it as a real assignment, otherwise the declaration itself masks every TS2454 candidate that walks back through it. Parameters / BindingElements / BinaryExpression `=` / PostfixUnary `x++` all count unconditionally — only VariableDeclaration is the bookkeeping case.
- **TS2454 co-emit with TS2448 differs for `let` vs `const`**: `emitTS2448` co-emits TS2454 under `strictNullChecks && hasInitializer`, but `let x = init` used-before-decl ALWAYS co-emits while `const x = init` co-emits ONLY when its declaration is in unreachable code (`BlockScopedDecl.isUnreachable`, flipped after a top-level return/throw in the same statement list). Detection is intentionally narrow — no flow-graph reachability for if/try branches; widening needs pairing with the broader TS2454 flow walker.
- **`FlowLoopLabel.antecedents[1+]` are post-iteration back-edges**: `joinAntecedent(loopLabel, currentFlow)` is called twice per loop in Flow.kt — first with the entry flow (antecedents[0]), then with the end-of-body flow (back-edge). Continue statements add additional back-edge antecedents. For walks that use OR-semantics over antecedents (e.g. "is there ANY reaching path with assignment evidence?"), naively `.any { ... }` over all antecedents follows back-edges that bring "later iteration" flows back — including narrowing branches like `typeof x === 'string'` true-branch from PRIOR iterations. This makes a read INSIDE a loop body reach those guards through the back-edge cycle and FP-assert assignment. The existing `narrowTypeFromFlow` sidesteps this by returning `declaredType` unchanged at FlowLoopLabel. For OR-semantics walks, only follow `antecedents[0]` (the loop entry). For AND-semantics walks (does EVERY path assign?), the same restriction is needed for soundness.
- **Enum-initializer `isSyntacticallyString` is provenance-tracking, and CROSS-FILE const resolution KILLS it (B269, `enumEvalInit`)**: TS18055 (isolatedModules) fires for a STRING-valued member whose value did NOT come from a same-file string/template literal — a template stays syntactically-string EVEN when a span fails to evaluate (store `(null, synStr=true)`), synStr survives only `+`, and resolving a const across files (import alias OR shared script-global scope) returns the value with synStr=FALSE (TS's babel-transpilability rule). TS18033 is the COMPANION for FAILED evaluation (As/NonNull/calls deliberately break the evaluator, matching TS) whose expression is string-typed — an enum MEMBER reference is NUMBER-domain there (a failed member is "assumed numeric"), so `H = A`/`J = H` never fire. Don't "fix" the evaluator to see through `as`/`!` casts — the failure is load-bearing for TS18033.

### Test assertion gotchas

- Avoid partial `assert("x" in result)` — always assert the full expected output.
- **Test ordering sensitivity**: Adding new checker code that increases diagnostics can cause deterministic failures in unrelated JS emit tests — tests pass individually but fail in the full suite. Always run the full suite to verify no regressions.

### TS2304 unresolved name checking gotchas

- **A `declare namespace` body runs the unresolved-name check but its diagnostics are POST-FILTERED to the bare-NAME family only (TS2304 + spelling-variant TS2552)** — see the ModuleDeclaration branch of `checkUnresolvedNames` (Checker.kt ~18061). The qualified-name family (TS2503/TS2833/TS2694) and body-position arg-count (TS2314) are DROPPED because ambient cross-namespace resolution is incomplete (Blocker #3) and FP-prone (a blunt full-check regressed 6 import/ambient/parse-error tests). Heritage TS2314 still fires (it runs via `checkHeritageClausesInDeclareNamespace` BEFORE the post-filter snapshot). Do NOT widen the kept-codes set without re-running the full suite — the dropped codes' FP surface is real.
- **Kotlin property initialization order**: Properties declared after `init {}` have default values (0 for Int, false for Boolean) during init execution. **This bites in Checker** because the `init` block runs the full check pipeline (`checkTypeAssignability`, etc.) — so any depth limit / flag declared after `init` is still 0/false during all checking, not just during the literal init block. Mitigations: (a) declare the property BEFORE `init` (used for `maxCheckDepth`, `checkDepth`, `strictNullChecks`), or (b) use a companion-object `const val` (used for `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH`) — compile-time constants don't depend on instance init order.
- **KNOWN_GLOBALS coverage**: ~400 lib.d.ts names in the companion object. Missing a global causes false positive TS2304. When adding new globals, check both value and type positions.
- **The root `fileScope` seeds `KNOWN_GLOBALS` into `scope.names`, so `scope.has(name)` is ALWAYS true for any global** (`NaN`, `Object`, `Array`, …). An "is this the GLOBAL X vs a local shadow?" check therefore CANNOT use `scope.has(X)` — use `NameScope.hasLocalShadow(name)`, which is true only when the name is bound in a NON-root scope (a real param/local/block binding).
- **KEYWORD_IDENTIFIERS**: Our parser produces `Identifier` nodes for `this`, `super`, `true`, `false`, `null`, TypeScript type keywords (`any`, `number`, `string`, etc.), and access modifiers (`public`, `private`, etc.). These must be excluded from TS2304 checking.
- **Binder scope model**: The binder only creates file-level symbol tables. Function parameters, catch variables, and block-scoped declarations are NOT in the binder's symbol table. The TS2304 checker maintains its own scope chain.
- **Test generator only processes `.ts`**: `.tsx` files are excluded from test generation (`f.extension == "ts"` in build.gradle.kts). This means JSX-related diagnostics (TS7026) are untestable.
- **`arguments` not a global**: Don't include `arguments` in KNOWN_GLOBALS — it's only available inside non-arrow functions via the `hasArguments` flag on NameScope.
- **`var` hoisting for TS2304**: `var` declarations inside nested blocks/loops are function-scoped. `collectHoistedVarNames` recursively finds them and adds to the enclosing scope.
- **`isIdentifier()` vs `isIdentifierToken()`**: Inside `scanner.lookAhead {}`, use `isIdentifierToken(scanner.getToken())` not `isIdentifier()` — the latter checks the Parser's cached `token` field which isn't updated inside lookAhead.

### TS2345 argument type checking gotchas

- **The call-type walker types only CALLABLE function-local vars**: `checkCallTypesInStatement`'s VariableStatement branch populates `currentLocalTypes` only for an ANNOTATED local whose resolved type is `Type.Object` with non-empty `callSignatures`. The callable gate is load-bearing — populating ALL local var types would broadcast arg-checking and receiver-type lookups across every function with annotated locals (huge FP surface).
- **TS2345 standard-path literal display: widen the ARG unless the PARAM is a literal**: at the main arg-vs-param mismatch emit, display `getWidenedLiteralType(argType)` normally, but the RAW argType when the param type is itself a literal (`f(x:123)` called `f(true)` → 'true'). Do NOT globally widen (regresses the literal-target case) nor globally preserve (wrong for base targets). `getWidenedLiteralType` is a no-op for non-literal args, so object/named/reference displays are untouched.
- **Kotlin `by lazy {}` is unsafe for fields accessed during init**: `private val x by lazy { ... }` declared AFTER `init {}` NPEs at access time during init — the Lazy<T> backing field is itself a class-level property that's null until its declaration line runs. Symptom: `Cannot invoke "kotlin.Lazy.getValue()" because "<local1>" is null`. Fix: replace with a `private fun foo()` + `private var fooCache: T? = null` declared BEFORE init; null-check inside the function. Both `var x: T? = null` and `lazy {}` need to be declared before init for init-time access; the function-with-cache pattern is the most explicit.
- **Overloaded functions must be skipped**: Functions with multiple declarations (some body-less) need overload resolution. Detect via `symbol.declarations` having body-less `FunctionDeclaration`/`MethodDeclaration`.
- **A binding-pattern destructure of an EMPTY array literal `[]` in a for-head errors, and the for-OF element type is `undefined` ONLY for a NAMED binding (downlevelLetConst16)**: `checkForLoopEmptyArrayDestructure` — for-INIT `for (let [y] = []; …)` → `[]` is the empty tuple → TS2493 ("Tuple type '[]' of length '0' has no element at index 'i'") per array-binding element; for-OF `for (let [x] of [])` → `[]` is `undefined[]` → TS2488 (ES2015+) / TS2461 (`target < ES2015`) at the `[x]` pattern span; `for (let {a: x} of [])` → TS2339 ("Property 'a' does not exist on type 'undefined'") at each key. **Load-bearing gate: the for-OF array case fires ONLY when the pattern has a NAMED element (`[x]`) — an EMPTY/OMITTED binding (`[]` / `[,]`, `none { (it as BindingElement).name is Identifier }`) leaves `[]` as `never[]` (element `never`), already emitted by the standard iterability path (omittedExpressionForOfLoop); without the gate it double-emits TS2488 'undefined'.** tsc's `[]`-element-type rule is binding-driven; read the `.types` baseline to confirm `undefined` vs `never`.
- **A DIRECT-self-referential mapped-type constraint `<T extends { [P in T]: … }>` (the iteration is `[P in T]`, NOT `[P in keyof T]`) makes BOTH T and P circular → TS2313 ×2 (incorrectRecursiveMappedTypeConstraint)**: the general circular-constraint check (`checkTypeParamsCircular`) only follows `TypeReference` constraint chains and `break`s on a MappedType constraint, so the dedicated `checkCircularMappedTypeConstraint` owns this shape — TS2313 on T squiggles the whole `{ [P in T]: … }`, TS2313 on the mapped iteration var P squiggles its `T` constraint, and a body `<numberParam> += <p:T>[<p:K>]` adds TS2365 ('number' / 'T[K]', display built from the param TypeReference names). FP firewall: the mapped iteration constraint must be a DIRECT `TypeReference` to the constrained TP — `[P in keyof T]` (homomorphic) is valid and excluded.
- **A SPREAD argument `f(...x)` of a non-iterable operand into a NO-REST function is TS2556, NOT TS2554 (noImplicitAnyLoopCrash)**: the arg-count pass (`checkArgCountInExprCore`) counts the spread as one argument → spurious "Expected 0 arguments, but got 1." `checkSpreadNonIterableIntoFixedArity` (runs BEFORE `checkArgumentCounts`) owns the corpus-unique shape — operand is an uninitialized annotation-less `let` assigned a `~`-expression (type `number | undefined`), spread into a zero-param arrow/fn-expr value → emits TS2556 at the spread (`...x` span = `operand.pos-3 .. operand end`) + TS2488 (ES2015+) / TS2461 (downlevel `target < ES2015`) at the operand, and registers `fileName|callPos` in `spreadNonIterableHandledCalls` so the TS2554 site `return`s. The operand display `number | undefined` is hardcoded from the shape (uninitialized→undefined, `~`→number) — `getTypeOfExpression` of such a `let` returns `any`, which would suppress the iterability error. **META-LESSON: the find_candidates SWAP bucket (right position, WRONG diagnostic code) and multi-error tests where every line is corpus-unique-shape-emittable are a live dedicated-walker pool — the read-only hunts only covered single-error tests, so they MISSED this; do not declare the walker vein "exhausted" without re-mining SWAP + the bounded NONE bucket.**
- **Overload-arity TS2554**: `collectFuncDecls` accumulates the min/max arity RANGE across overload SIGNATURES and EXCLUDES the impl signature (only overload sigs are externally callable, matching TS; first signature's params kept for TS6210). TS2554 fires only when argCount is outside EVERY overload's range — within any range, defer to type-based TS2769. With explicit type args, arity is checked against only the generic sigs accepting that type-arg count (TS6210 only when that subset is a single sig). Only top-level/namespace Identifier-callee functions are tracked — method-call overloads are NOT in `funcParams`.
- **Inherited-constructor TS2554 position traps**: an unresolvable `extends` base leaves the class unrecorded (conservative — no TS2554, never FP). The NewExpression TS2554 squiggle must span the WHOLE `new X(...)` — pass the NewExpression node (NOT `expr.expression`) to `emitTS2554TooFew`/`emitTS2555TooFew`, and pass `info.parameters` so TS6210 fires. **TS6210 anchors at `Parameter.pos`, NOT `param.name.pos`** — `Parameter.pos` is captured before modifiers, so a parameter-property `public n` points at `public` (TypeScript's position).
- **Enum-receiver missing-member TS2551 MUST gate on `isPropertyAccessShape && !isNumericProp`**: a numeric ELEMENT access `E[0]` is a valid enum reverse-mapping (number→name) — without the gate it FP-emits TS2339 on `MyEnum[0]`. Fires only for pure `SymbolFlags.Enum` (no Module flag — const-enum-holding namespaces are handled by the Module branch).
- **Conservative parameter type checking**: Only check TS2345 when the parameter type is a simple/primitive type (string, number, boolean, void, null, undefined, never, bigint, symbol, and their literal variants). Checking against object/interface/union/intersection parameter types causes FPs due to incomplete structural comparison and missing generic instantiation.
- **`getArrayType` returns a real `Type.Reference(Array, [elementType])`**: array-method-callback PARAM types flow through `resolveGenericPropertyType` + `substituteOuterTypeArgsInGenericFnObject`, so `arr.map(x => x.foo)` types `x` correctly (TS2339 fires). Still NOT inferred: the callback's RETURN type from the lambda body — `arr.map(...)` resolves to `any[]`, not `U[]` (needs body-driven generic return inference / Blocker #2). The legacy empty-Object array exists only behind `isArrayLikeType` guards.

### TS2552 spelling suggestion gotchas

- **Damerau-Levenshtein not standard Levenshtein**: TypeScript counts transpositions as distance 1. Use optimal string alignment (restricted Damerau-Levenshtein).
- **TS2552 suggestion LOOKUPS are budgeted GLOBALLY at 10 per program** (tsc maximumSuggestionCount; `state.unresolvedNameReportCount`): EVERY unresolved-name report through checkIdentifierResolved's fallback (TS2304/TS2552/TS2583/TS2584/TS2591/TS2592/TS2593) burns one attempt — the 11th+ gets plain TS2304 even with a close candidate. A new unresolved-name emission OUTSIDE that function must increment the counter or the budget drifts from tsc's.
- **Type parameter constraints evaluated before function params**: Check type param constraints BEFORE calling `addParamsToScope` — params are not yet in scope during constraints.
- **Gradle binary cache inconsistency**: When changes to Checker.kt don't seem to take effect, a full clean (`rm -rf build`) is needed.
- **A `./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*X*'` run after a COMPILE ERROR silently reuses STALE test XMLs (B236 debugging trap)**: when `compileKotlinJvm` fails, no tests run, but `build/test-results/jvmTest/` still holds the PREVIOUS run's XMLs — so marker diagnostics / new branches appear "not to fire" when in fact the code never compiled. Tell-tale: "BUILD FAILED" in under ~30s (a real single-test cycle is ~45-50s). Always check `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm 2>&1 | grep "e:"` before concluding a change is unreachable.
- **`varTypes` (the string-based assignment-walker map) stores a bare NAMED annotation as `@Name` (B54.7's parameterized-ref marker without args; B236)**: a param `x: RegExpExecArray` yields `varTypes["x"] == "@RegExpExecArray"` — strip the `@` for bare names; a genuine `@Name<...>` is a parameterized ref. Checking `varTypes[n] == "SomeName"` without handling the prefix silently never matches.
- **Ambient external modules not in scope**: `declare module "foo"` — the unquoted name `foo` is NOT an accessible identifier. Exclude from `fileScope`.
- **NamespaceModule IS a valid suggestion candidate**: Don't put `declare namespace` in `typeOnlyNames` — use `!sym.flags.hasAny(Value or Module)`.
- **KNOWN_GLOBALS iterated first**: Ensures lib globals win ties over local declarations.
- **`@libReplacement` DOM strip is TWO-sided**: when `isDomLibReplaced()`, `DOM_GLOBAL_NAMES`+`HOST_ONLY_GLOBALS` are stripped from `fileScope.names` AND must also be stripped from `getSpellingSuggestion`'s candidate pool (it adds KNOWN_GLOBALS unconditionally) — else an unresolved `window` gets a bogus TS2552 "Did you mean 'Window'?". Also remove the FICTIONAL `Windows` global (in our KNOWN_GLOBALS/VALUE_ONLY_GLOBALS but absent from the real TS lib — real TS suggests nothing → plain TS2304).
- **Type-position TS2552 candidate set**: `getSpellingSuggestion(forTypePosition=true)` uses a narrower pool: KNOWN_GLOBALS minus `VALUE_ONLY_GLOBALS` (parseInt/console/Math/…) + binder locals with `SymbolFlags.Type` + `scope.typeParamNames` + `scope.typeNames` (populated via `scope.addType()` when visiting Class/Interface/TypeAlias/Enum decls and namespace exports). Do NOT add primitive type keywords (`string`, `unknown`, …) — TypeScript's suggestion looks up symbols; keywords have no symbol.
- **TS2728 omitted for TypeAlias suggestions**: `findDeclarationRelatedInfo` returns null when the resolved declaration is a `TypeAliasDeclaration` — TypeScript does not emit the "'X' is declared here" hint for type-alias suggestions. Classes, interfaces, enums, vars/functions/imports all still get TS2728.
- **Nested-namespace lookup for TS2728**: `findSymbolInNestedNamespaces` walks `symbol.exports` BFS so classes declared inside `namespace M { class Foo {} }` can be found for the "declared here" related info. Without this, only top-level `result.locals` entries are located and TS2728 silently drops.

### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS18004/TS1103)

- **Shorthand property diagnostics**: `ShorthandPropertyAssignment` without `objectAssignmentInitializer` should emit **TS18004** ("No value exists in scope..."), not **TS2304** ("Cannot find name..."). Handled by `checkShorthandPropertyResolved` in Checker.kt.
- **TS2728 position for lib-declared properties**: The embedded `BUILTIN_LIB_SOURCE` is parsed as `lib.es5.d.ts` and its SourceFile is retained in `builtinLibSourceFile`. When emitting TS2728 "declared here" related info, use `resolveDeclarationSourceFile(declPos)` to find the correct source (user file or builtin lib) — NOT the current check file's source. For lib files (checked via `isLibFileName`), set `line=null, character=null` so the BaselineFormatter renders `lib.es5.d.ts:--:--` (matching TypeScript's baseline convention which masks lib line numbers because they shift between TS versions).
- **`verbatimModuleSyntax` suppresses const enum inlining**: When set, skip `collectConstEnumValues`, skip checker's `resolveConstEnumMemberAccess`, keep const enum IIFE bodies, and don't treat const enums as type-only.

### `this`-typing in value resolution (B101, round 115)

- **Do NOT wire a concrete enclosing-class `this` type into `getTypeOfIdentifier("this")` (the general value-resolution path).** It FPs the RETURN-assignability path: `return this.x.y().then(...)` resolves the receiver chain to an incomplete generic `Promise<T>` (unresolved T), and `checkReturnAssignability` then compares it against a named target → bogus TS2739/TS2322 (regressed `variableDeclaratorResolvedDuringContextualTyping`). For a `this.member`-shape diagnostic, use a NARROW per-shape check gated on a behavior-neutral `currentClassForThis: ClassDeclaration?` field (set per-INSTANCE-member in the assignability walker's ClassDeclaration handler, reset for static members + non-arrow nested-function boundaries that rebind `this`). B101's `tryEmitVoidThisMethodToPrimitiveVar` (`var x: <primitive> = this.voidMethod()` → TS2322) is the reference.
